URL Name

How-to-create-an-Indeed-Employer-Account

Title

Creating an Employer Account on Indeed

Details

Overview

You need an employer account to post jobs and manage hiring on Indeed. This article explains the requirements, how to create your account, what verification looks like, and what to expect before your jobs go live.

Important: Creating an employer account requires creating a job seeker account first.

Requirements

Age

To use Indeed, you must meet the minimum age for your region:

  • European Economic Area (EEA), Switzerland, United Kingdom, Turkey, and Ukraine: 18 years or older.
  • Japan: The minimum age permitted under Japanese law.
  • All other regions: 16 years or older.

Important: If you’re younger than 16, we must close the account and delete your data.

Location
  • Indeed for Employers is available in select markets.
  • Check our Terms of Service for current availability.

Creating an account

Step 1: Create a job seeker account.
  1. Go to Indeed for Employers and select Sign in.
  2. Choose to sign in with Google, Apple, or a work email address.
    1. If you enter an email address, a code will be sent to your inbox that you can use to verify your email address.
    2. Important: If your company domain uses single sign-on (SSO), you will be redirected to that page after entering your email.
  3. Add and verify your phone number to enable two-factor authentication (2FA). This phone number will be the account’s default sign-in when you return. This is required for employer accounts.
  4. Follow the prompts to finish creating your job seeker account. You do not need to upload a resume or any documents at this stage.

Tip:Using your work email will make it easier to verify your account.

Step 2: Create your employer account.
  1. In the top-right corner, select Employers/Post Job.
  2. Enter your company information.
  3. Click or tap Continue.

Important: If your company already has an employer account on Indeed, ask the account admin to add you to the account.

Once your account has been created, if you need assistance signing in, you can check our article on signing in to your employer account for more information and troubleshooting steps.

What happens next

Verify your account

After signing up, you may be asked to verify your identity or your business. This is a one-time process to prevent impersonation and to protect your company’s brand.

Verification methods may include:

  • Verifying a work email
  • Uploading business documents
  • Completing a third-party identity verification

Visit our article on how to verify your account for more information.

Important: We will never ask for your password or payment information as part of the verification process.

Post a job

Once you create an account, you can post a job on Indeed.

Before you post a job, make sure to check out our:

  • Job posting quality standards
  • Terms of Service

Important:Your job will not appear on Indeed until your account is verified (if required) and our Trust and Safety team has reviewed your job posting. We’ll notify you by email when your job is live.

Security and data

Visit our Trust Center to learn more about Indeed’s security practices and certifications, including SOC 2 and ISO documentation.

Technical support

Issue: I can’t bypass CAPTCHA.

Solution: Make sure cookies are enabled in your browser. Use the accessibility option shown directly on the CAPTCHA screen.

Problem: I need to change the email address. I used Google to create an account.

Solution: Review Google’s Help Center for help on using a different email address.

FAQs

Question: I have two Indeed accounts. Can I merge them?

Answer: No. Merging accounts isn’t supported. If your email is linked to multiple employer accounts, you can switch between them from the company menu in the top-right corner of your dashboard.

Question: I’m not a job seeker. Can I remove the job seeker account?

Answer: No. Your employer account must remain linked to a job seeker account for security and sign-in purposes.

Question: How long does verification take?

Answer: Timing varies by method and location. You’ll receive an email once your job is live.

Contacting us

Need more help? Contact us, and we’ll be happy to help.

Creating an Indeed Hiring Event

Learn how to create an Indeed Hiring Event from your dashboard or using an intake form. Customize your event details and apply standard or Smart Screening. Understand how RSVPs, attendance estimates, and event ad campaigns work to plan interviews and manage hiring efficiently.

  • May 18, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

How-to-use-the-Indeed-Hiring-Events

Title

Creating an Indeed Hiring Event

Details

Overview

Indeed Hiring Events help you interview many candidates for more than one role on the Indeed platform. This article explains how to:

  • Access and set up your hiring event
  • Add jobs and set your budget
  • Configure interview settings and screening questions
  • Review and submit your event
  • Understand attendance estimates

You can create events from your account or through an intake form provided by your Indeed representative.

Getting started

From your account
  1. Sign in to your account.
  2. Navigate to Interviews.
  3. Select Hiring event dashboard at the top of the page.
  4. Select Create an event.

You can create a new event, invite an existing user to create an event, or invite a new user to create an event.

Did you know? Save time by duplicating a previous event. On your Hiring event dashboard, go to the Actions column. Select the three dots next to the event and choose Duplicate.

Intake form
  • Ask your Indeed representative to send you an intake form.
  • Linked users on your account, admins, and Indeed representatives can access the form.
  • Additional functionality will vary based on permissions.
  • Learn more about adding users and setting access levels.

Important: You must have an Indeed account to access the intake form.

Setting up your event

Step 1: Basic interview details
  1. Enter your Company name.
  2. Choose your desired outcome for the event. This won’t be shared with candidates:
    1. Finding more candidates
    2. Managing costs
    3. Checking for specific qualifications
    4. Saving time
  3. Enter the desired number of interviews or select I don’t know.
  4. Confirm whether you’ll interview candidates during the session.
    1. If you’re using Indeed Interview or converting a job on Indeed, you must select Yes.
  5. Select the interview format:
    1. In-person
    2. Virtual
    3. Virtual (external)
  6. Toggle on Optional: Use a previous interview template to copy the details of a previous event. You can still change details if you use a previous template.
  7. Select Continue.
Step 2: Adding jobs
Using existing jobs

Choose this option if you have active jobs on Indeed.

  1. Search by job title or job location.
    1. Jobs linked to previous interview sessions won’t be displayed.
  2. Select Continue.
Creating a new job
  1. Scroll to the bottom of the page.
  2. Select Skip and create new job.
  3. Add job basics to create a new Hosted job for your event.
    1. You’ll be able to find this job in your Jobs tab.
    2. It will close automatically after the event.
    3. You can only reopen it if you use it in a new hiring event.
    4. Learn more about posting a job on Indeed.
  4. Confirm your Job details.
  5. Select Continue.
Step 3: Adding a budget
  1. Enter a Total Budget for your event.
    1. Minimum budget: $500 (US/CA), € 350, or £ 350 based on the market.
    2. Recommended budget:  $250 USD per job for easier-to-fill roles or $500 USD per job for harder-to-fill roles.
  2. Add an optional Campaign name.
  3. Select Continue.

Did you know? Hiring Event minimums are separate from Sponsored Job minimums. They’re on a per-event basis.

Step 4: Selecting your interview settings
Date and time
  • Add up to 12 interview sessions.
Time slots (optional)
  • Allow job seekers to select from multiple scheduling options.
  • Time slots can be 30 minutes or 60 minutes.
Preferred number of interviews
  • The number of interviews per time slot determines how many RSVPs can be accepted.
  • The ratio is 3.33 RSVPs to 1 interview.
    • Example: If you can conduct 10 interviews, the maximum RSVPs you can collect is 34.
  • The maximum number of interviews per time slot is 300. This cap helps avoid overbooking and poor job seeker and interviewer experiences.
  • There’s no limit on the number of RSVPs for events.
Email addresses

Add email addresses for:

  • Hiring event team members
  • Those needing updates
  • Accommodation requests
Event details
  • Event location
  • What the candidate should wear and bring
Application link (optional)
  • You can enter an application link for the job seeker.
  • Only one application link per event can be submitted.
  • There’s no guarantee the candidate will complete the application.
Event title and identifier
  • Create a title and identifier for your event.
Step 5: Adding job-level screening
  1. Select Add screening.
  2. Set up a customized screening per job or add a screening to multiple jobs.
  3. Select Continue.
Step 6: Final review
  1. Review your hiring event details.
  2. Make any final edits.
  3. Select Submit.
What’s next?
  • You’ll receive an email to confirm your event is posted and ready to accept RSVPs.
  • An interview objective campaign will be created to advertise the event. You can find this in your Campaign Management tab.
  • The default setting is to advertise 10 days before the first event date.
  • Jobs will automatically move into the campaign if the event is more than 10 days out.
  • Jobs will return to the original Sponsored Jobs campaign once the event is finished.

Understanding attendance estimates

Why do you use attendance estimates instead of the number of scheduled interviews?
  • Not every job seeker who RSVPs to Hiring events will attend.
  • Attendance estimates help you plan how many interviewers you’ll need for each day and time slot.
How are attendance estimates calculated?

We estimate attendance using:

  • Past events
  • Attendee job titles
  • Location
  • Day of the week
  • Brand awareness
  • Ad spend
Why don’t the ranges for each time slot add up to the total estimated attendees?

We calculate and round estimated attendees across all time slots to give you the most accurate attendance range.

Digital Service Fee

This article explains Indeed’s Digital Service Fee (DSF) effective Jan 1, 2026, showing when it appears on invoices for customers billed in the UK (1.75%), France (2.5%), Italy (3%), and Spain (2.75%), how it is applied by billing address across all products/services, and that it may apply to pre-2026 IOs/contracts that allow DSF. It also clarifies invoice presentation (separate line item), provides a calculation example, and notes that indirect taxes (e.g., VAT) may apply to the invoice total inclusive of DSF, with rates subject to change.

  • Aug 22, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

About-the-Digital-Service-Fee

Title

Digital Service Fee

Details

Overview

Starting January 1, 2026, a Digital Service Fee (DSF) will appear on invoices for services provided in the following countries: France, Italy, Spain, and the United Kingdom.

  • This fee reflects additional costs associated with Digital Services Tax legislation in impacted countries.
  • When applicable, the DSF will be displayed as a separate line item on your invoice.
  • This DSF may apply to insertion orders (IOs) and contracts signed before January 1, 2026, that allow for DSF.
  • The DSF is applicable to all products and services based on your billing address.

DSF rates

United Kingdom

  • A DSF of 1.75% will be added to your invoice issued by Indeed UK Operations Limited or Indeed Ireland Operations Limited.

France

  • A DSF of 2.5% will be added to your invoice issued by Indeed France SAS or Indeed Ireland Operations Limited.

Italy

  • A DSF of 3% will be added to your invoice issued by Indeed Italy SRL or Indeed Ireland Operations Limited.

Spain

  • A DSF of 2.75% will be added to your invoice issued by Indeed Ireland Operations Limited.

Indirect taxes

If applicable, indirect taxes (such as Sales Tax, VAT, GST/HST/QST, or similar) will be applied to the entire invoice amount, including the DSF.

Digital service fee example breakdown

  • Sponsored Jobs $110
  • Promotion -$10
  • DSF @ 2.5% $2.50
  • Net amount* $102.50

*Amount on which indirect tax may be applied (if applicable).

Important: Rates subject to change.

Discrimination and Hate Offenses

This article explains Indeed’s Discrimination and Hate Offenses Policy, helping employers and job seekers understand how to create and maintain inclusive, bias-free job postings. It outlines rules prohibiting discrimination or hate speech based on age, race, religion, gender, disability, or other traits; provides examples of compliant vs. noncompliant language; and explains exceptions (e.g., acting or faith-based roles) and next steps if a post is removed for policy violations.

  • Mar 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Discrimination-and-Hate-Offenses

Title

Discrimination and Hate Offenses

Details

Overview

This policy applies to all employers and job seekers on Indeed. Our goal is to make job searches fair, safe, and welcoming. Indeed doesn’t allow hate speech, discrimination, or offensive content at any time while using the platform. We may remove, hide, or suspend job posts or accounts that violate this policy.

Policy requirements

Age
  • Jobs may not show preference or exclusion based on age.
  • Employers may include minimum age requirements only if required by labor laws or the nature of the job (for example, serving alcohol).
  • Phrases that suggest generational preference, such as “digital native,” are not allowed.
National origin
  • Jobs may not exclude or target people based on national origin, ethnicity, or accent.
  • Language requirements may be listed, but phrasing must be neutral. For example:
    • Do: “Fluent in Spanish and English”
    • Don’t: “English must be your first language.”
Race
  • Jobs may not show preference or exclusion based on race.
  • Race is not a factor in job ability, and any mention of racial preference is not allowed.
  • Limited exceptions exist for acting or modeling roles.
Religion
  • Jobs may not exclude or show preference based on religion.
  • Religion may only be mentioned if it is directly tied to the job (for example, roles in places of worship or faith-based schools).
Gender and sexual orientation
  • Jobs may not show preference or exclusion based on sex, gender identity, or sexual orientation.
  • Consider replacing gendered job titles with neutral terms. For example:
    • Do: “chairperson” or “server”
    • Don’t: “chairman” or “waitress”
  • Limited exceptions may apply for acting, modeling, healthcare, or childcare roles.
Traits that don’t affect job performance

Jobs may not discriminate based on personal characteristics, such as:

  • Disability
  • Genetic information
  • Marital or family status
  • Physical appearance (tattoos, piercings, weight, hair color)
  • Socioeconomic status
  • Smoking habits (except in rare cases, such as childcare roles)
Identity-based insults, targeted abuse, and hate speech
  • We don’t allow hate speech or targeted abuse in any form.
  • Hate speech may include:
    • Slurs
    • Threats
    • Content that promotes hostility toward a person or group
  • Protected traits include, but are not limited to:
    • Race
    • Ethnicity
    • Gender
    • Religion
    • Disability
    • Sexual orientation
    • Family status
Offensive content
  • Content that is vulgar, profane, or demeaning is not acceptable.
  • References to mental health disorders in casual or inaccurate ways (for example, “must be OCD”) are not acceptable.
  • We may remove content including swear words or sexual language.
Photo requests
  • Employers may not request photos or headshots as part of the application process.
  • Exceptions may apply to modeling or acting roles.

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Review job posts for neutral and inclusive language
  • Avoid terms that show bias or preference for any group
  • Focus only on skills and qualifications that affect the job

Contact Indeed Support if you believe we removed your post by mistake.

      1. Click or tap Find Companies.

Square logo

Requirements:

  • File Format: JPEG.

Important: Only one CEO can be listed.

Job seekers are not able to apply to jobs more than once. Keep this in mind when deciding whether to edit an existing job post or create a new one.

Email Address Policy

This article explains Indeed’s Email Address Policy, including the requirement for a stable, permanent email address to create and manage an employer account, and why temporary or disposable burner emails are not allowed. The article also helps employers understand what happens if a disallowed email is used and outlines the steps to restore access—either by using an approved email for new registration or by updating the email and submitting an appeal with valid identification for restricted or suspended accounts.

  • Jan 26, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Email-Address-Policy

Title

Email Address Policy

Details

Overview

Indeed wants to keep its platform safe and secure for everyone. This policy explains what kind of email address employers must use when creating and managing an Indeed account.

This policy applies to all employers using the Indeed platform.

Email address requirements

Approved email addresses

To use Indeed, you must register and keep your account with a stable, permanent email address. This helps protect your account and lets you recover access if there is a problem.

A stable email address:

  • Is meant for long-term use
  • Is protected by a password
  • Allows account recovery
  • Comes from a trusted provider (like Gmail, Outlook, or Yahoo) or your workplace
Unapproved email addresses

Temporary or disposable emails—also called “burner” emails—are not permitted. These emails:

  • Expire after minutes or days
  • Often come from sites like mailinator.com or tempmail.org
  • Do not offer strong security or recovery options

What happens if a temporary email is used

New accounts
  • If you try to create a new account with a temporary or disposable email, account creation may be blocked.
  • You must use a stable email address to finish registration.
Existing accounts

If an existing account is found to use a temporary or disposable email, Indeed may:

  • Restrict the account, or
  • Suspend the account

Next steps

Restoring account access

You can appeal the decision if we restricted or suspended your account because of this policy.

To be reviewed, you must:

  • Update your account with a stable email address that follows this policy.
  • Submit an appeal.
Sending an appeal
  1. Submit the appeal form.
  2. Provide the following information:
    1. Your full name
    2. The reason for your appeal
    3. Your current location
  3. Upload one of these forms of identification:
    1. Government-issued driver’s license or ID
    2. National identity card
    3. Work visa
    4. Passport
    5. Military ID
    6. Student ID
ID requirements
  • The location on your ID must match the general location in your profile.
  • Don’t send an ID that is edited or changed in any way.

Important: We only use your ID to check your appeal. We delete your documents after the review is done. Learn more in our Privacy Policy.

FAQs: Company Pages

This article helps employers understand how to find, claim, and manage their Indeed Company Page, including posting a job to create a page, claiming an existing page, merging duplicates, managing reviews and Q&A, and using notifications or alerts to engage with followers. It explains all available options and limitations for responding to reviews, handling questions, and keeping a Company Page up to date.

  • May 20, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

FAQ-company-page

Title

FAQs: Company Pages

Details

Overview

This FAQ gives quick answers to common questions about Indeed Company Pages. You’ll learn how to find and claim your page, how reviews and Q&A work, and what you can do as a page owner. Use this guide to understand the basic steps and key features of Company Pages.

Claiming a page

Question: I can’t find our Company Page on Indeed. Can I create a page myself?

Answer: Yes. Post a job on Indeed to automatically create a new Company Page. You can then claim the page.

Question: I’m not sure if our company already has a Company Page. Will I risk creating a duplicate page if I post a job?

Answer: No, you won’t create a new page if you post a job from an employer account already linked to a Company Page.

Question: There are multiple pages for my company. Can I merge them?

Answer: Maybe. Contact us to report duplicates. If the pages match, we’ll combine the reviews, ratings, and Q&As. If not, we’ll ask you which page you’d like to keep and remove the others.

Still need help?

Check out our article on claiming your company page for more information and troubleshooting steps.

Using your page

Question: Will I get a notification when someone posts a new question or review?

Answer: No. Page owners get a weekly email with up to three recent questions or reviews.

Question: Can I use the Alerts feature to email my Company Page followers?

Answer: No. Alerts are automatic. If you’ve posted new jobs, we’ll send one email to your followers each week.

Company reviews and Q&As

Question: Can companies reply to reviews?

Answer: Yes. We encourage companies to respond. Learn more about managing company reviews.

Question: Can I pay to remove a bad review?

Answer: No. We don’t remove reviews that follow our content guidelines. Contact us if you believe a review is false, libelous, or doesn’t meet the guidelines.

Question: Can anyone ask or answer questions in the Q&A tab?

Answer: Yes. Anyone visiting your Company Page can take part in Q&A.

Question: Can I edit the Q&A categories?

Answer: No. Categories are automatically created based on the questions people ask.

Fraudulent Job Policy

This article explains Indeed’s Fraudulent Job Policy, helping users understand what constitutes fraudulent job postings (including scams and information theft attempts), how to report suspicious jobs using the Report job button at the bottom of job posts, and how Indeed detects and removes fraudulent content through automated systems, human review, and user reports. Users who interacted with fraudulent jobs can also learn how to protect themselves by accessing Indeed’s Job Seeker Help Center article on safeguarding personal information.

URL Name

Fraudulent-Job-Policy

Title

Fraudulent Job Policy

Details

Overview

Indeed wants to provide a trustworthy platform for everyone. This policy explains how we find and handle fraudulent job postings on our platform.

What are fraudulent job postings?

  • Fraudulent job postings aim to deceive, mislead, or cause harm to job seekers.
  • This includes:
    • Scams
    • Activity meant to steal personal, financial, or sensitive information

How do we find fraudulent job postings?

  • We use both automated systems and human review to help find fraudulent job postings.
  • We also provide a clear and easy way for users to report suspicious jobs to Indeed.
  • To report a job posting, click or tap the Report job button at the bottom of the job post.
  • For more details, check our Job Seeker Help Center article on reporting a job.

How we handle fraudulent jobs

  • We remove job postings that we determine are fraudulent.
  • We may suspend or ban employer accounts involved in fraudulent activity.
  • We may also take other actions to help prevent fraudulent activity.

Next steps

Think you interacted with a fraudulent job? Shared personal information with a suspicious account? Check our Job Seeker Help Center article on how to help protect yourself.

Free Job Post Policy

Learn Indeed’s free job post limits by country: post up to 3 free jobs per month in the US, Canada, France, UK, Germany, and Netherlands with 30-day visibility, or post free jobs in 22+ other countries with up to 120-day visibility. Sponsor jobs to extend visibility beyond free limits or reopen expired posts.

  • Jun 10, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

free-job-post-limit

Title

Free Job Post Policy

Details

Overview

Indeed offers free job postings in countries worldwide. The number of free jobs you can post per month and how long they stay visible depends on where you’re posting.

In the US, Canada, United Kingdom, Germany, France, Italy, Ireland, Belgium, the Netherlands, Spain, Sweden, and Switzerland:

  • You can post up to 3 free jobs on Indeed per calendar month.
  • Each free job stays visible for up to 30 days from the day it’s posted.

In all other countries where free posting is available:

  • Free job posts stay visible for up to 30 days by default.
  • In select countries listed below, free jobs stay visible for up to 120 days.

When you reach your monthly limit or your jobs reach their visibility period, you can sponsor jobs to extend visibility, increase reach, and connect with qualified candidates more efficiently.

Terms, conditions, quality standards, and usage limits apply.

Countries where free jobs are visible for up to 120 days

Free jobs posted in these countries will stay visible for up to 120 days from the day they were posted:

  • Bahrain
  • Colombia
  • Ecuador
  • Hong Kong
  • India
  • Indonesia
  • Japan
  • Kuwait
  • Malaysia
  • Mexico
  • Morocco
  • Nigeria
  • Oman
  • Pakistan
  • Peru
  • Philippines
  • Portugal
  • Qatar
  • Saudi Arabia
  • Turkey
  • United Arab Emirates
  • Vietnam
Smart Sourcing and free job post limits

If you’ve already posted 3 free jobs in the US, Canada, United Kingdom, Germany, France, Italy, Ireland, Belgium, the Netherlands, Spain, Sweden, and Switzerland during the current calendar month, you can still post additional jobs to source candidates with Smart Sourcing.

Here’s what happens with these additional jobs:

  • Your job won’t appear in Indeed search results for job seekers unless sponsored.
  • You’ll see a notification on the Jobs page that you need to sponsor the job for search visibility.
  • Smart Sourcing will still work normally to help you find and invite qualified candidates.

Important: This sponsorship requirement only applies to search visibility. It doesn’t affect your ability to use Smart Sourcing to proactively reach candidates.

FAQs

Question: How will I know when I’ve reached my free job post limit?

Answer: You’ll be notified during the job posting process when you’ve reached your monthly free job posting limit. This limit applies only in the US, Canada, United Kingdom, Germany, France, Italy, Ireland, Belgium, the Netherlands, Spain, Sweden, and Switzerland for jobs posted directly on Indeed.

Question: Can I post more jobs after reaching my monthly limit?

Answer: Yes. You can continue posting by sponsoring your jobs to increase visibility and attract more candidates.

Question: Does the free job post limit apply to all countries?

Answer: No. The 3 jobs per month limit applies only to jobs posted directly on Indeed in the US, Canada, United Kingdom, Germany, France, Italy, Ireland, Belgium, the Netherlands, Spain, Sweden, and Switzerland. Other countries may have different posting limits or no monthly limits.

Question: Does this limit apply to jobs posted through an Applicant Tracking System (ATS)?

Answer: No. This limit only applies to free jobs posted directly on Indeed. It doesn’t affect jobs posted through integrated ATS partners.

Question: How long will my free job posts stay visible?

Answer:

  • In the countries listed above: Free jobs stay visible for up to 120 days from the day they’re posted.

  • In the US, Canada, United Kingdom, Germany, France, Italy, Ireland, Belgium, the Netherlands, Spain, Sweden, and Switzerland: Free jobs stay visible for up to 30 days from the day they’re posted.

  • In all other countries: Free jobs stay visible for up to 30 days from the day they’re posted.

When your free job reaches its visibility limit, you’ll be notified and can choose to sponsor it to maintain visibility.

Question: If I close a free job before its visibility period ends, can I reopen it the next month without it counting toward my limit?

Answer:

  • No. If a job was posted more than 30 days ago, you can’t reopen it to receive organic traffic, even if you closed it before the visibility period ended.

  • To post a new free job, create a new job post. Reopening a closed job from a previous month requires sponsorship.

Generic or Confidential Company Names

Indeed may limit visibility for job posts that use generic or confidential company names, since job seekers prefer to know who they’re applying to. Employers should list clear, accurate company names to build trust and attract more applicants. Sponsoring a job can help increase exposure while keeping the company name confidential.

  • Dec 4, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Policy-Generic-or-Confidential-Company-Name

Title

Generic or Confidential Company Names

Details

Overview

Job seekers want to know which company is offering a role before applying. A clear company name helps people learn about the organization and decide if the job is a good fit.

If a job uses a generic or confidential name (for example, “Confidential Executive Office” or “Confidential Organization”), it may not show in search results or may have limited visibility.

Policy requirements

  • Jobs with generic or confidential company names may be hidden or shown less often.
  • You can still reach job seekers by sponsoring your job. Sponsorship helps your post appear even if the company name is not shown.
  • Use a clear company name whenever possible. This helps job seekers learn about your organization.

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Check your company name and update it to be clear and accurate.
  • Use sponsorship if you need more visibility while keeping the company name confidential.

Contact Indeed Support if you believe we removed your post by mistake.

How To Add Shifts and Schedules to Your Job Post

This article explains how to add shift and schedule details to your Indeed job post—either directly when creating or editing a post, by including them in the job description, or through structured ATS/XML fields. It helps employers clearly format and display work hours, days, and supplemental schedules so jobs appear accurately in search results and match job seeker preferences.

  • Oct 31, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Shift-and-schedule-information-overview

Title

How To Add Shifts and Schedules to Your Job Post

Details

Overview

Shift and schedule information tells job seekers when and how long they would work for a job. A shift is the hours and time of day someone works, like morning, night, or eight-hour shifts. A schedule is the pattern of days and hours each week, such as Monday to Friday or four 10-hour days. Jobs with shifts that match a job seeker’s preferences are highlighted in search results, making them easier to find.

When you include shift and schedule details in your job posting, they’ll appear in the following places:

  • Search results
  • Job page
  • Job description

Tip: You can include more than one shift for a single job if needed. Job seekers will see the shift tag that best matches their preferred availability.

Adding shifts and schedules to your job

When you post directly on Indeed
  1. Create a new job post or edit your existing job.
  2. Select your shift and schedule details under What is the schedule for this job.

Tip: You can update a job’s shift or schedule details anytime by editing the job post.

When you post from your career site or ATS

You can add shift and schedule information in one of these ways:

  • Include it in the job description. Add details about shifts and schedules directly in the job text.
  • Use ATS fields or XML feeds. Many ATS systems let you provide structured shift and schedule information. Check if your ATS offers these options.
  • Ask your ATS representative. If you’re unsure how to add this information, contact your ATS support team for guidance.

Learn more about posting jobs from your career site or Applicant Tracking System (ATS).

Did you know? Indeed may automatically extract shift and schedule information from your job description when it’s written in a clear, structured format. Using the examples below helps your shifts display accurately on your job post.

Formatting your job’s shifts and schedule

To help job seekers understand your job’s availability, it’s best to be specific and consistent when describing shifts and schedules. Use the formats below to clearly show the days, hours, and special requirements.

Important: Include only the shifts and schedules that apply to the specific job you’re posting. Listing multiple roles or mixed schedules in one job can cause confusion or incorrect display.

Schedule examples
Every weekend

This work schedule requires work every weekend.

Examples:

  • Current shifts are Saturday and Sunday, 4:00 p.m. - 10:30 p.m.
  • Required to work Saturday or Sunday
4x10

This full-time work schedule covers four days of ten-hour shifts per week.

  • Do: Use the format (number of days)x(number of hours), such as 4x10
  • Don’t: Write “Four ten-hour workdays”, “4 days at 10 hours a day”, or “4 10-hour shifts per week”.
Supplemental schedules

This covers evenings, weekends, on-call work, overtime, and other potential schedules.

Examples:

  • Evenings as needed
  • Must be willing to work nights and some weekends
  • On-call rotations as required
Shift examples
4-hour shift

A part-time shift lasting four hours.

Example: 4 hours per day, 4 days per week.

Evening shift

Work that happens mainly in the evening.

Examples:

  • Evening shifts required
  • Morning and evening shifts available
Split shift

A shift is split into multiple parts in a day.

Example: Candidates must work a split shift daily or every other weekend.

FAQs

Question: Can I still add shifts to my job after I post it?

Answer: Yes. You can add shifts and schedules when you create the job post or by editing an existing job.

Question: Can I add overtime and holiday schedules to the job post?

Answer: Yes. You can add a supplemental schedule to the job’s shifts and schedule information.

You can’t cancel a Smart Sourcing Enterprise license through the product.

Your plan stays active for the full length of your agreements.

Contacts and billing

You’ll continue to receive contacts and be billed based on the terms in your contract.

How To Change Your Account Language

This article helps Indeed Employers understand how to change their account language, either by updating the Language setting in Employer Settings or by addressing issues that prevent changes, such as browser cookies, linked user permissions, location-based language overrides, and product-specific controls like Smart Sourcing. It also explains how to ensure job-related emails display in the correct language by updating the primary account settings.

  • Dec 18, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

203134794

Title

How To Change Your Account Language

Details

Overview

Your Indeed employer account sets a language based on the country site you used when you signed up. You can change your language at any time in your Employer settings.

Change your language

  1. Sign in to your employer account.
  2. In the top right corner, click or tap your email address.
  3. Select Employer settings from the menu.
  4. Under Your account, find the Language section.
  5. Use the pencil icon to choose your preferred language.
  6. Click or tap Save to apply your changes.

This updates the language across your Indeed dashboard.

Troubleshooting

Issue: My language keeps switching back.

Solution: Your browser may be using old cookies or a saved URL. Try clearing your cache and cookies or opening an incognito window. For step-by-step help, check our system troubleshooting article.

Issue: I can’t find the option to change the language.

Solution: Only account owners or admins can change language settings. If you’re a linked user, ask your account owner or admin to update it.

Issue: My email notifications are in the wrong language.

Solution: Job-related emails follow the language of the main account that posted the job. The account owner can change the primary account’s language to fix this.

Issue: Smart Sourcing or job posting pages show another language.

Solution: Some products use your work country to set the language. Check the language setting within the product or confirm your location. For jobs posted in more than one region, your account may show multi-language job postings by default.

Next steps

If your settings still don’t save, contact Indeed Support for more help.

How To Close Your Job Posting

This article explains how to close/delete and pause a job as well as what action to take if the job posting came from an ATS.

  • Jul 24, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

203134694

Title

How To Close Your Job Posting

Details

Overview

You can change the status of your job posting on Indeed at any time — whether you want to pause applications, close a filled role, or reopen a position. This article explains how to update your job’s status and what each status means.

Important: The short video below shows the full process. You’ll find a written step-by-step guide below. (Video recap: The video shows how to sign in to your Indeed account, navigate to the Jobs tab, and select a new status from the drop-down menu on the right side of the page.)

How to change a job’s status

  1. Sign in to your Indeed account.
  2. Select Jobs and All Jobs on the left-hand rail.
  3. Select the Status of the job on the right-hand side of the page.
  4. Choose a status (Open, Paused, or Closed) from the drop-down menu.

Important: If your job posting is sourced from your career site or applicant tracking system (ATS), you’ll need to remove or close it there first. This ensures it’s also removed from Indeed’s search results.

What each status means

Open
  • Your job appears in search results.
  • If your listing is a Sponsored Job, it can draw from your budget to help increase visibility and attract more applications.
Paused

Pause a job if you’re getting a high volume of applications and want time to review candidates:

  • Your job is removed from search results.
  • Spending stops while the job is paused.
  • The posting remains visible in your jobs dashboard.
  • Candidates will also remain available for review until you close the job.
Closed

Close a job when you’ve made a hire or no longer need to fill the position:

  • Your job is removed from search results.
  • The job is archived for future reference in your closed job list.
  • The job can be used as a template for the future.
  • Your spending for this job stops.

Important: All outstanding payments for sponsored job postings will be processed on the first day of the following month. When you downgrade a job from Sponsored to Free, you may still incur remaining charges for billable activity on our site. These charges will never exceed your maximum budget.

[Embedded video omitted]

FAQs

Question: Can I reopen a closed job?

Answer: Yes. You can reopen a closed job from your jobs dashboard. When you reopen a job, Indeed may ask why you are reopening it. The posted date will update when the job is reopened.

Tip: Want to post a similar job but without the previous spend or candidate history? Create a new job using the previous one as a template. You don’t need to reopen the closed job.

Question: Will pausing a Sponsored Jobs posting affect my budget?

Answer: Yes. Pausing stops all spending right away. If you reopen the job, spending may resume depending on your sponsorship settings.

Question: Will my job posting appear in search results immediately after I set it to Open?

Answer: Maybe. New or reopened jobs may go through moderation before appearing in search results. If your job is sourced from an ATS or career site, make sure it is active there first. Changes made on Indeed alone won’t override a closed or expired source feed. In some cases, a job may also be flagged if it is ineligible for visibility or requires sponsorship.

How to Display Pay Information in Job Postings

This article explains how to display and format pay information in Indeed job postings so it surfaces correctly in search results, job descriptions, and company pages, helping employers meet job seeker expectations for pay transparency. It covers multiple ways to add salary: directly in Indeed via Edit job (single value, range, pays from, pays up to), through an ATS using a pay field or XML 0 element, or by clearly formatted pay text in the job description, plus do’s/don’ts to ensure accurate parsing and visibility.

  • Jun 3, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Indeed-pay-transparency-salary-estimates

Title

How to Display Pay Information in Job Postings

Details

Overview

Pay information is displayed in places like search results, job descriptions, and company pages. It can show in several different ways, based on what is most relevant to your job. We encourage employers to include pay transparency in their job descriptions because it’s one of the top things that job seekers look at.

How to add pay to jobs on Indeed

From your account, edit jobs posted directly on Indeed or ones that are indexed.

  1. Navigate to your jobs page and select the three dots beside your job.
  2. Select Edit job.
  3. Use the Pay section to enter the salary details.

You have a few options to choose from when creating or editing a job.

  • Exact amount: A fixed amount (for example, “$15 per hour”).
  • Range: A range between two amounts (for example, “$10-15 per hour” or “55,000 - 70,000 a year”).
  • Starting amount: Starting from a specific amount (for example, “From $10 per hour” or “Starting at $10 per hour”).
  • Maximum Amount: Capped at a maximum amount (for example, “Up to $15 per hour”).

Important: If you list multiple salaries for a job, Indeed will display whatever pay that is listed first. If your job pays in some other scale or point system, you will need to translate that into hourly or salary pay in order to display your pay on Indeed.

How to add pay to jobs in your ATS

You can still include pay information depending on your setup if you manage jobs outside of Indeed using a career site or Applicant Tracking System (ATS). Here’s how:

  • ATS pay field: Leverage the pay field within your ATS to add salary.
  • XML feed: If you use an XML feed, provide the data within a single 0 element. Please note that Indeed may not display salary or other pay data provided if the pay data is not entered in the correct numerical format. Common mistakes include, but are not limited to:
    • Not including the pay period, e.g. “$10”
    • Not putting the default, official currency for the job country, e.g. “10”, or “€10” for a US job
    • Using words only instead of numbers, e.g. “minimum wage” or “salary negotiable”
  • Job description: Indeed may display salaries provided in the job description. To help ensure it is captured correctly, check out the formatting tips below.

Contact your ATS representative for guidance if you need help with adding salary information.

How to format pay information in your job description

In your job description you can include:
  • Pay indicators: Use terms such as “pay,” “compensation,” or “salary,” and include currency symbols (for example, $) or abbreviations (for example, USD).
  • Pay period: Use common pay periods (for example, per hour, day, week, month, or year).
  • Standard Number Formats: Use standard number formatting, like commas, to separate thousands.
  • Pays from: If you express pay starting from a specific value, use “From” or add a “+” after the value.
  • Pays up to: If you express pay with an upper limit, use “Up to.”
  • Tips and bonuses: If the wage can include income such as tips, it’s best to put them after the pay information. For example: “Wage: $15 / hour + tips.”
What to Avoid
  • More than one salary: Avoid separate salaries for different locations.
  • Using shorthand: For example, avoid using “k” to represent thousands.
  • Using words instead of numbers: For example, “thirty dollars per week.”
  • More than one pay period: For example, “From $40 / hour to $6,000 per month.”
  • Unclear pay ranges: Since pay information is so important to job seekers, it’s best to be as informative as possible.

How To Edit Your Hiring Events

Guides Indeed Hiring Events organizers on how to edit event details, pause and unpause spending, and cancel or reschedule events from the Hiring Events dashboard, including status changes and candidate notifications. Provides two key action paths: Edit (Actions → Edit → Update per section) to change event settings; Pause Spending (Actions → Pause Spending) to temporarily stop promotion and move the event to Pending with defined resume behavior; plus Cancel or Reschedule (Actions → Cancel/Reschedule) to end an event permanently or adjust dates/times with automatic candidate alerts and preservation of original time slots when times don’t change.

  • Jan 30, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

How-to-edit-your-IHP-Hiring-Event

Title

How To Edit Your Hiring Events

Details

Overview

This article explains how to edit event details and pause spending. It also covers how to cancel or reschedule an event and what candidates will be notified about.

Editing an event

  1. Log in and go to your Hiring Events dashboard.
  2. Find the event that you want to edit. Under the Actions column select either the three dots or the pencil icon.
  3. Select Edit.
  4. Select the Update button in each section to save your changes.
How to pause spending
  • Go to your Hiring Events dashboard.
  • To the right of the event, select thethree dots under the Actions.
  • Select Pause Spending.
What happens when an event is paused?

New job seekers won’t be able to find the event by searching. However, job seekers who previously saved a link to the posting will still be able to schedule an interview.

Paused events will move to the Pending status. Unpausing an event will move it back to Active. An event may be paused if:

  • It was manually paused via the dashboard
  • It was automatically paused if the event hit the max RSVPs
  • Finance has flagged the account as being in arrears
Canceling or rescheduling an event

Candidates will receive an email or text if an event has been canceled or rescheduled. Unless it was due to severe weather, they won’t be notified why. However, you do have the option to send the candidates a custom message.

To cancel or schedule an event:

  • Go to your Hiring Events dashboard.
  • Select the three dots under Actions.
  • Select Cancel or Reschedule.
If you are canceling:
  • You must review and confirm the cancellation. The event will show a canceled status.
If you are rescheduling:
  • Select interview date(s) to reschedule. You can change existing event dates/times or you can add/remove dates using the plus (+) and minus (-) buttons on the righthand side.
  • Candidates will keep their original time slot if the event time stays the same.
  • You will be provided an overview of changes for review.

Important: Canceling an event cannot be undone. Consider rescheduling whenever possible to ensure a better candidate experience.

How To Pay Your Indeed Invoices

This article explains how to pay your Indeed invoices using either a credit card (with automatic billing and manual updates via your employer dashboard) or an insertion order (IO) arranged through your Sales or Customer Success representative. It also provides troubleshooting steps for declined payments, browser issues, and fraud errors, plus FAQs and contact guidance to help users securely manage billing information and resolve payment problems.

  • Dec 17, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

360022889112

Title

How To Pay Your Indeed Invoices

Details

Overview

There are different ways to pay for your Indeed products. In this article, we’ll explain the two most common payment methods, offer basic troubleshooting tips if your credit card is declined, and answer common questions about billing. For a complete overview of payment options, contact Indeed Support.

Important: Only users with billing permissions can access this information. Learn more in Adding Users and Setting Access Levels.

Using a credit card

Set up your billing information and payment method.
  1. Sign in to your Indeed account.
  2. Click or tap your email address in the top right corner.
  3. Select Billing and invoices from the menu.
  4. Open the Information tab to add or edit:
    1. Billing contact
    2. Billing information
    3. Payment method
  5. Click or tap Update when you’re done

Important: Contact Indeed Support to change your preferred currency.

Pay your outstanding balance.
  • Your credit card will be charged automatically once payment is due.
  • Check your balance and payment history in the Summary tab of your Billing and invoices page.

Learn more about when and why you’ll get an invoice from Indeed.

Using an insertion order (IO)

How it works
  • When an invoice is overdue, a Pay button will appear.
  • You can pay online with a credit card, online banking, or other electronic methods.
  • IOs are an alternative to manual methods like cheques or bank transfers.
  • After a successful payment, it may take up to 10 days for the invoice to disappear from Payments due.
Who is eligible?
  • Some employers can pay using insertion orders (IOs), but not all accounts are automatically set up for this.
  • Contact your Sales or Customer Success representative to find out if your account can use IO payments.
  • If approved, you’ll receive instructions on how to set up and use IO payments for your invoices.
How to request an insertion order
  1. Contact your Sales or Customer Success representative.
  2. Be ready to provide:
    1. Business details
    2. Expected spend
    3. Payment history (if requested)
  3. Wait for the internal review and confirmation of eligibility.

Important: Make sure your billing details are complete and correct before contacting us.

Troubleshooting

Issue: My credit card was declined.

Solutions:

  1. Confirm your card details:
    1. Card number, expiration date, CVV/CVC, and billing address
    2. Make sure funds are available
  2. Check your card status:
    1. Ensure your card is not expired or cancelled.
    2. Contact your bank for a replacement if needed.
  3. Review your online transaction settings:
    1. Ensure online and recurring transactions are allowed.
    2. Set up 3D Secure if required.
Issue: The fields to add credit card information are grayed out or unclickable.

Solution: Review your browser settings. Follow our troubleshooting steps to fix common browser issues.

Issue: I can’t update my credit card. I’m getting fraud-related errors.

Solutions:

  1. Try these troubleshooting steps:
    1. Clear your browser’s cache and cookies.
    2. Try updating your payment information from a different network or device (such as mobile data).
    3. If your card was declined, contact your bank to confirm the transaction is authorized.
  2. Contact Indeed Support. We’ve listed the information we’ll need in the Contact us section below.

FAQs

Question: I’m having trouble updating my billing information. Can I email the details to you?

Answer: No. For security reasons, Indeed can’t accept credit card information via email. Update all details directly in the employer dashboard.

Question: I’m redirected to my bank or credit card company to confirm the payment. Is this normal?

Answer: Yes. This is called Strong Customer Authentication (SCA). SCA is a two-factor process that secures online payments. You’ll have to sign in to your credit card account and approve the transaction there.

Contact us

Contact Indeed Support if you need more help. For issues with updating your credit card, be ready to provide the following information:

  • Type of card (Visa, Mastercard, etc.)
  • Bank or card issuer
  • Last four digits of the card
  • Billing country
  • Which steps have you already tried:
    • From the browser issues troubleshooting article
    • Did you try a 4G/5G or a different network?
    • If your card was declined, did you contact your bank?

Explains how Indeed Hiring Events admins or users with IHE access can send, edit, and manage bulk custom emails to candidates, including timing and delivery behavior. Covers two edit paths: Email candidates (Actions → Email candidates) to update the message directly, and Edit (Actions → Edit → toggle “Send a custom email to all candidates”) to customize and save; notes that emails send the morning after RSVP and updates do not retroactively notify existing RSVPs.

  • Jan 30, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Using-custom-emails-on-Indeed-Hiring-Events

Title

How To Send Custom Emails for Indeed Hiring Events

Details

Overview

Easily send bulk emails to job seekers prior to their Indeed Hiring Events (IHE) interviews using our custom email tool. This article explains how to send, edit, and manage these emails from your account..

Important: Only account admins or users with IHE access can create or edit custom emails.

There are two ways to edit the IHE custom email:

Email Candidates:
  1. Go to your Indeed Hiring Events Dashboard and locate your event.
  2. Select the three dots under the Actions column and choose Email candidates.
  3. In the pop-up window, edit your email message to candidates.
  4. Select Update to save your changes.
Edit:
  1. Go to your Indeed Hiring Events Dashboard and locate your event.
  2. Select the three dots under the Actions column and choose Edit.
  3. Select the toggle next to Send a custom email to all candidates. This will open the editor where you can customize your email.
  4. Select Update to save your changes.

Note: If the Send a custom email to all candidates toggle is set to the off position, future candidates won’t receive an email.

FAQs

Question: When will my Indeed Hiring Events custom email be sent?

Answer: Candidates will receive your message the morning after they RSVP to your event.

Question: If I make changes to my custom email will existing candidates be notified?

Answer: No. If a candidate has already submitted an RSVP they won’t receive new or updated messages. We recommend setting up your custom email before your event goes live to make sure all candidates receive it.

How To Source Candidates on Indeed

This article helps employers use Indeed Smart Sourcing to find and contact qualified candidates by getting matched with job seekers or searching by role, skills, location, and experience. It also explains how to set up alerts, review resumes, organize candidates into projects, and message job seekers to build a strong talent pipeline.

Title

How To Source Candidates on Indeed

Details

Overview

With Indeed Smart Sourcing, you can quickly find and contact job seekers open to new jobs and match your job post. This article shows you how to get started, search for candidates, set up alerts, manage candidate profiles, and message job seekers.

Getting started

  1. Get a Smart Sourcing subscription. You can start with a 14-day free trial.
  2. Sign in to your employer account.
  3. Click or tab Smart Sourcing on the top left of the page.

Find candidates

Get matched with candidates.
  1. Go to the Matched candidates tab to see people who fit your job post.
  2. Click or tap Change to get matches for a different job.

Tip: If you’re not finding qualified candidates, you can update your job post to get better results.

Automate your outreach.
  1. Go to your Smart Sourcing dashboard.
  2. Choose Select a job to add to Sourcing Assistant.
  3. You’ll see the Welcome to Sourcing Assistant window.
  4. Follow the instructions to set up the assistant.

Watch now: Smart Sourcing with Sourcing Assistant webinar

Search for candidates.
  1. Open the Search for Candidates tab.
  2. Search by job title, location, or keywords like skills or company names.
Use advanced search.
  1. Click or tap the three dots on the right to open Advanced search.
  2. Filter by more options like years of experience or education.
Review resumes.
  1. After you search, you’ll get a list of candidates.
  2. Select a candidate’s name to access their full resume.
Set up alerts.
  1. Turn on Alerts for any saved search.
    1. We’ll email you when new job seekers match that search.
    2. You can stop or delete alerts anytime.

Manage and engage candidates

Add candidates to a project.
  1. Add candidates to a project to keep their profiles in one place.
    1. If you have a Professional subscription, you can share projects with your team and ask for feedback.
    2. Track notes, status updates, and alerts together.
Contact job seekers.
  1. Message candidates who match your job.
  2. Use a message template or write your own.
  3. Choose the job post you’re contacting them about, or add a new one.
  4. Review your message, then click Contact.
  5. We’ll send up to three reminders if the candidate doesn’t reply.

How to Switch Between Indeed Employer Accounts

This article is for employers and recruiters who have access to multiple employer accounts and need to switch between them. It explains how account access works and shows you how to change accounts at sign in or after logging in.

  • Mar 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

How-to-switch-Indeed-accounts

Title

How to Switch Between Indeed Employer Accounts

Details

Overview

If you manage more than one business or hiring group, you may have access to more than one Indeed employer account. Each account lets you post jobs, manage billing, and use hiring tools.

This article is for employers and recruiters who have access to multiple employer accounts and need to switch between them. It explains how account access works and shows you how to change accounts at sign in or after logging in.

What is an employer account?

An employer account is used by a person or company that is hiring. From this account, you can:

  • Post jobs
  • Buy plans or subscriptions
  • Manage billing and payments

How user access works

  • The person who creates an employer account becomes the owner.
  • Many people can have access to one account. Also, one person can have access to many accounts.
  • Every employer account shows the company’s name. If you have access to two or more accounts, Indeed will also show you the email of the person who owns them. This helps you know which account you are using.

Tip: Read our article about Adding Users and Setting Access Levels for more information.

When to switch accounts

You may need to switch between accounts if:

  • You manage hiring for different locations or departments
  • You have one account for job postings and another for resume plans
  • You work with multiple clients or brands under separate accounts

Note: Always make sure you’re in the right account before posting jobs or making changes.

Switching accounts

There are two main ways to switch accounts:

  • At sign in
  • After you have logged in
When signing in
  1. Sign in to Indeed with your email and password.
  2. If you have access to more than one account, you will see a page listing all of them.
  3. Choose the one you want to use.
After signing in
  1. Click the company name at the top of the page.
  2. Select the account you want to use.
  3. Click the person icon in the top-right corner.
  4. Click Switch accounts. This opens a page with all your accounts.
  5. Choose the account you want to switch to.

How To Update or Change Your Company Name on Indeed

Details

Overview

Your company name appears in job postings, on your Company Page, and in organization settings on Indeed. This guide explains how to edit your company name on an Indeed job posting or update your business name on your Indeed account, depending on where you want the change to appear.

Updating your company name on a job posting

When to update your company name

Each job posting displays a company name so job seekers know where they are applying. You can update the company name on a posting if:

  • The name is wrong or misspelled
  • You’re posting on behalf of another company
  • You own multiple businesses
  • You need to post confidentially
Steps
  1. Sign in to your Indeed account.
  2. Go to the Jobs tab in your Employer Dashboard.
  3. Click or tap Edit Job for the job you want to edit.
  4. Select the pencil icon next to the Company for this job field.
  5. Choose the reason for the change (some options may need a paid feature).
  6. Enter the updated company name and select Done to save your changes.

Learn more about editing your job posts.

Updating your Company Page or organization settings

If you need to change your employer name on your Indeed Company Page or in your organization settings, our team can help:

  1. Contact Indeed Support.
  2. Include your Company Page URL and company website URL.
  3. Explain the reason for the update.

Technical support

Issue: I can’t edit my company name on a job posting.

Solution: Some edits may require a paid feature. Check your Employer Dashboard or contact Indeed support.

How To Verify Your Account

Learn how to verify your Indeed employer account to safely post jobs by choosing one of four secure methods: Call to verify, using your Work email with a verified domain, ID.me verification with a government ID, or uploading business documents such as a license or articles of incorporation.

  • Jun 22, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Verification-Center-Document-Verification-Requests

Title

How To Verify Your Account

Details

Overview

Account verification is a one-time step that keeps your company’s job postings safe. It confirms your connection to your company so that only authorized users can post jobs on its behalf. Depending on where you are located and your account’s current status, you may see different verification options.

Before your jobs can go live on Indeed, our team reviews your information. Most requests are approved quickly, but sometimes we may ask for more details or documents. Thank you for your patience as we work to protect your company and prevent fraud.

Important: You don’t need to follow the steps below if your company’s account is already verified. Ask the account administrator to add you as a user instead.

Verifying your account

Getting started
  1. Sign in or create your employer account on Indeed.
  2. Click or tap the red banner at the top of your page.
  3. Choose one of the four verification methods below.
ID.me verification
Submit a government-issued ID.
  • Any documents you submit through ID.me will not be accessed or stored by Indeed. Review the ID.me privacy policy for more information.
  • Note: ID.me is not available in IL, TX, or WA.
Work email
  • Enter your company’s verified domain email to receive a one-time code.
  • A verified domain email matches the company’s website domain (for example, yourname@example.com matches example.com).
Call to verify
  • Speak with a member of our account verification team.
Business documents

Upload one of the following:

  • Articles of incorporation
  • Business license
  • Company liability insurance
  • Office utility bill
  • Bank Statement
  • Lease or franchise agreement
  • Tax permit
  • Staffing agency employment letter or pay slip

We do not accept:

  • Publicly available documents
  • Employer Identification Number (EIN) tax documents
  • ACORD certificates of insurance

If you’re submitting a document issued by one of the states or territories listed below, please make sure it isn’t publicly accessible. We recommend submitting additional documents, like a utility bill or insurance document, for verification.

States and territories with public business registries include:

  • Alaska
  • Arizona
  • California
  • Colorado
  • Connecticut
  • Florida
  • Georgia
  • Idaho
  • Indiana
  • Kansas
  • Kentucky
  • Maryland
  • Massachusetts
  • Michigan
  • Mississippi
  • Missouri
  • New Hampshire
  • North Carolina
  • Ohio
  • Oregon
  • Puerto Rico
  • Rhode Island
  • South Dakota
  • Texas
  • Vermont
  • Washington
  • Wyoming
What happens next
  • Once you’ve submitted your verification, our team will review your request.
  • While we aim for a smooth process, we may need additional reviews or documents.

FAQs

Question: Can I appeal my account rejection?

Answer: Yes. Visit the Appeal Rejection page and sign in to your account. You’ll find a link to the appeal form where you can attach the requested documentation.

Indeed Analytics for Jobs and Campaigns

An overview of the Jobs and campaigns dashboards in Indeed Analytics for Indeed Employers.

  • Mar 18, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Understanding-Your-Jobs-and-Campaigns-Dashboards

Title

Indeed Analytics for Jobs and Campaigns

Details

Overview

Indeed Analytics has a number of features and reports that help you track performance on Indeed. This article focuses on metrics specific to your jobs and campaigns.

Getting started

Here’s how to access the jobs and campaigns dashboard in Indeed Analytics:

  1. Sign in to your account.
  2. From the left-hand navigation bar, select Analytics, then Jobs and campaigns.

Jobs and campaigns dashboard

Metrics
  • Impressions: The number of times your Sponsored Job is shown
  • Clickthrough rate (CTR): The ratio of impressions to clicks
  • Clicks: The number of times a job seeker clicks on your Sponsored Job to view the complete job details
  • Apply start rate (ASR): The ratio of clicks to apply starts
  • Apply starts: The number of times the Apply button was clicked after clicking on your Sponsored Jobs
  • Apply completion rate: The number of completed applications
  • Applies: The number of job seekers who applied to your Sponsored Job
  • Cost: The cost you have accrued for your Sponsored Job
  • Apply rate: The percentage of job seekers who apply after clicking on your Sponsored Job
  • Cost per click: The average cost you incur for each click you receive
  • Cost per apply start: The average cost you incur for apply starts
  • Cost per apply: Your cost divided by the number of applies
Summary View
  • The Performance funnel lets you see basic metrics like number of applies, clicks, and impressions.
    • Use Traffic to select Organic, Sponsored jobs, or Combined.
  • Cost summary shows your total sponsored job spend and broken-down metrics like cost per click.
  • Engagement summary shows key metrics over time.
  • Desktop vs. mobile clicks highlights the importance of your jobs being mobile-optimized.
  • Your highest- and lowest-ranked jobs can help you identify where to make adjustments.
Detailed report
  • Metrics over time lets you view and compare a wide range of key metrics over a period of time.
  • The Metrics table is filterable and customizable.
    • Select the plus button on the top right to add or remove columns.
    • Use View By to select how you want the data displayed.

Note: Traffic filters data based on the type of traffic a job received, not the sponsored status of the job. Some jobs can have both sponsored and organic traffic and may show up in all three traffic options. The sponsored and organic views will show only part of a job’s traffic, while the combined view will show all of it.

FAQs

Question: How often are reporting analytics updated?

Answer: Timeliness expectations vary by metric. Here are the estimates for how quickly data will be updated after an action occurs:

  • Impressions: within 48 hours
  • Sponsored clicks: within 24 hours
  • Sponsored apply starts: within 24 hours
  • Applies: within 48 hours
  • Budget spent: within 24 hours
  • All other reporting metrics: within 48 hours

Indeed Apply Policy

Indeed Apply helps people apply for jobs directly on Indeed. Employers may need to follow transparency, accountability, and use standards for the best experience.

  • Apr 21, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Policy-Indeed-Apply

Title

Indeed Apply Policy

Details

Overview

Indeed Apply lets people apply for jobs directly on Indeed. This helps make the hiring process faster and easier for both job seekers and employers.

Employers using Indeed Apply are asked to be clear about how they use job seeker data, stay responsible for their setup, and follow Indeed’s standards for how the tool is used.

Transparency

Employers are responsible for explaining what job seeker data they collect and how it will be used.

Indeed may direct job seekers to employers for purposes of data subject access rights for applications sent through Indeed Apply.

Accountability

Employers using Indeed Apply may need to:

  • Agree to Indeed’s Terms of Service and Privacy Policy.
  • Share contact information so Indeed can reach them if needed to address potential concerns and communicate any changes about Indeed Apply.
  • Work with Indeed to set up or complete their integration with Indeed Apply.

This helps ensure the job application process runs smoothly and that data is sent correctly.

Standards of Use

To help job seekers have a smooth experience, employers using Indeed Apply must follow these standards:

  • Job seekers must be able to complete a full apply using Indeed Apply. They must not be sent to another site or asked to apply again elsewhere.
  • Indeed Apply and Disposition Sync integrations must be available before Indeed can accept ATS, employer, or third-party job integrations.
  • If present in the external application flow, all screener questions must be replicated in the Indeed Apply setup.
  • All job content, including screener questions, must be legal and comply with Indeed’s Terms of Service and Site Rules.
  • US-based ATSes, or ones that support employers with US-based jobs, must include Equal Employment Opportunity (EEO) tools when needed.
  • Indeed Apply can be managed by an ATS, an employer, or an Indeed-approved third party chosen by the employer.
  • Indeed must approve any third-party building an integration for an employer before starting.
  • Employers may not post jobs with more than 100 screener questions.
  • Integrations may not improperly share job seeker PII with external parties.

Next Steps

If your job was removed or hidden, you may:

  • Review your post to confirm it uses Indeed Apply correctly.
  • Make sure job seekers can apply directly on Indeed without extra steps.
  • Check that your contact and integration details are up to date.
  • Reduce your screener questions if there are more than 100.
  • Contact Indeed Support if you think your job was removed by mistake.
