Indeed’s Email Policy

This article explains Indeed’s Email Policy, which allows users to contact job seekers only about the job they applied for, and details that misuse, such as sending spam, asking for a re-application on another site, or scams, may result in job removal or limited platform access.

  • Jul 15, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Indeeds-Email-Policy

Title

Indeed’s Email Policy

Details

Overview

Indeed’s email system can only be used to talk to job seekers who applied for your jobs. If you misuse the system, Indeed may remove your jobs or limit your access to it.

Why this policy exists

When you post a job, you can use our email system to reply to people who apply. This tool is meant only for communicating with job seekers about the job they applied for. We will take action if the email system is used for other reasons.

Here are some examples of misuse:

  • Asking a job seeker to apply for the same job again on a different website.
  • Sending spam to job seekers about other, unrelated jobs.
  • Scammers contacting job seekers to get private information.

Using the email system in the wrong way hurts a job seeker’s trust and can put them in danger. We must control our email system to protect job seekers from bad actors.

This policy applies everywhere. If you do not follow it, your jobs will not be visible on Indeed.

Indeed’s Job Board Policy

This policy outlines Indeed’s guidelines for existing job board partners, focusing on content standards, quality, and eligibility to ensure a high-quality experience for job seekers. It also states that Indeed is no longer accepting new job board partnerships as of January 4, 2019, with a few country-specific exceptions.

  • Jul 20, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

204575734

Title

Indeed’s Job Board Policy

Details

Overview

This policy applies to all current job board partners whose content appears on Indeed. To give job seekers the best experience, your content must follow our standards. These rules cover job quality, the application process, and your relationship with Indeed.

Job board content requires sponsorship for visibility on Indeed.

Policy requirements

Job quality

All jobs must:

  • Be real, current, and paid opportunities
  • Be posted by the hiring company
  • Include all necessary details, such as:
    • Company name
    • Location
    • A complete job description
Clear application process
  • The application process must be simple, free, and completed on your site.
  • No redirects or required logins.
Unique content
  • The content you provide must be unique and not already on Indeed.
  • This helps prevent duplicate job postings.
Relationship with Indeed
  • You must have a direct relationship with Indeed.
  • Third parties are not allowed to manage your presence on the site.

What you need to know

  • As of January 4, 2019, Indeed is no longer accepting new job board partnerships.
  • Job boards removed or denied for quality reasons will not be reconsidered.
  • Rebranding job boards is not allowed.
  • Job board content requires sponsorship for visibility on Indeed.

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job or job board:

  • Review your jobs and processes against the policy requirements above.
  • Fix any issues with job quality, missing details, or misleading information.
  • Make sure each job links to a direct, free, and login-free application process on your site.
  • Remove duplicate content that may already exist on Indeed.

Contact Indeed Support if you believe we removed your job or job board by mistake.

Indeed’s Company Page Policy

This policy outlines the rules for Indeed Company Pages, including pages per brand. It applies to all employers with a Company Page on Indeed.

  • Sep 11, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Policy-Company-Pages

Title

Indeed’s Company Page Policy

Details

Who this applies to

This policy applies to employers and companies with Company Pages on Indeed.

Overview

Indeed Company Pages help job seekers learn about potential employers. They also allow companies to showcase their brand.

  • Each employer brand can only have one Company Page.
  • An employer brand refers to the name and logo that employees and job seekers recognize.

To ensure accuracy and consistency:

  • Your Company Page should cover your whole company, not just one office or department.
  • Indeed doesn’t allow separate pages by location (like U.S., Canada, or U.K.) or by division (like Finance, HR, or Marketing).
  • Division-specific pages are not supported. For example, an employer brand may have more than one department or other business units. There will be one Company Page for all of them.

What you need to know

Employers can customize parts of their Company Pages. This allows you to write to audiences in different divisions or locales all on one page.

Next steps

Indeed regularly reviews Company Pages to make sure they follow our guidelines. This includes merging duplicate pages. These are Indeed’s decisions and they may change at any time. Contact Indeed support if you need help managing your Company Page.

Indeed’s Company Reviews Policy

This policy outlines the guidelines for Indeed Company Reviews. It applies to all employers with a Company Page on Indeed.

  • Sep 11, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Policy-Company-Reviews

Title

Indeed’s Company Reviews Policy

Details

Who this applies to

This policy applies to employers and companies with Company Pages on Indeed. It outlines ways to respond to Q&A and Company reviews written by current or former employees.

Overview

Employer responses are official representations of their company. They can add context to reviews or Q&A content. Here are a few tips for responding to reviews, questions, or answers:

  • Be timely and professional. If you decide to respond, do so in a timely manner. Always be professional, as your responses are visible to everyone.
  • Keep things positive. Thank the reviewer for sharing their experience.
  • Do not respond to combative reviewers. Avoid engaging with hostile or aggressive reviews.
  • Do not disclose personal information. Don’t try to figure out who the author is. Reviews and Q&A content are anonymous.
  • Do not threaten legal or other actions. Reviews aren’t the place to report or start legal action against anyone.
  • Feel free to take individual cases offline. Include company contact details or relevant internal information in your responses if you’re open to followup.

What you need to know

Employer responses to reviews, questions, and answers can address concerns. They can also make things more open and clear for everyone who’s reviewing now or in the future. Reviewers can write new reviews if their opinions change. Keep this in mind when clearing up misunderstandings.

Next steps

Employer responses are posted shortly after submission. Indeed’s Trust & Safety team will review the response if someone reports it. We reserve the right to remove or not post your response if it violates any of the above guidelines.

Integrating Your Applicant Tracking System With Hiring Events

This article explains how to integrate your Applicant Tracking System (ATS) with Indeed Hiring Events using Indeed Apply Sync to collect both RSVPs and applications in one workflow, enabling faster candidate review and scheduling in your ATS. Covers two setup paths—running events with full ATS integration (Application + RSVP) or without integration (RSVP only)—plus requirements, troubleshooting, candidate experience, and tracking hiring results.

  • Jan 30, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

How-to-integrate-your-ATS-with-Hiring-Events

Title

Integrating Your Applicant Tracking System With Hiring Events

Details

Overview

This guide shows how to connect your Applicant Tracking System (ATS) to Indeed Hiring Events. When you connect your ATS with Indeed Apply Sync, you can collect RSVPs and applications at the same time. Candidates who RSVP and apply will appear in your ATS, so your team can review and schedule faster.

Getting started

Make sure your Indeed Apply ATS integration is turned on:

  1. Visit Indeed’s Integrations Hub.
  2. Select your ATS.
  3. Follow the steps to connect.

Important: Contact your Indeed account team if you need help turning on your ATS integration.

Adding jobs to your event

  1. Create your Hiring Event or open an existing one.
  2. When adding jobs to the event, review the Submission type column.
  3. Select jobs with submission type Application, RSVP to collect both RSVPs and applications.

Important: If a job shows RSVP only, it won’t send applications to your ATS. It will collect RSVPs only.

What candidates experience

When job seekers follow the link to Schedule an interview in your job listing, they’ll submit their application, answer your screening questions, and schedule the interview in a few simple steps.

Technical support

Issue: I can only select “RSVP only” as the submission type.

Solution:

  1. Make sure that:
    1. Indeed Apply Sync is turned on for your ATS.
    2. The jobs you add are posted through your ATS.
  2. If needed, re-sync the job from your ATS and try adding it again.
Issue: RSVPs appear, but applications are missing in my ATS

Solution:

  1. Check that the job’s submission type was set to “Application, RSVP” during event setup.
  2. Confirm the ATS connection is active in your Indeed account and that the requisition is open and accepting applications in your ATS.

FAQs

Question: Can I run a Hiring Event without ATS integration?

Answer: Yes. Your jobs will be “RSVP only.” You can manage candidates in Indeed.

Question: Can I add any job to my Hiring Event?

Answer: No. You can only include jobs posted through your ATS that have Indeed Apply Sync turned on.

Question: Can I track hiring results?

Answer: Yes. Use your Hiring Event dashboard to review RSVPs, interviews, and activity. Use your ATS reports to track applicant status, interviews, offers, and hires.

Job Posting Integrity and Duplicates

This article explains Indeed’s Job Posting Integrity and Duplicates policy, helping employers understand how to avoid duplicate or misleading job listings by posting each job only once, using one accurate title, and updating rather than reposting existing roles.

  • Mar 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Job-Posting-Integrity-and-Duplicates

Title

Job Posting Integrity and Duplicates

Details

Overview

This policy applies to all employers posting jobs on Indeed. Our goal is to make the job search simple, fair, and clear for job seekers. To support this, each real job opportunity should appear only once on Indeed.

Posting the same role more than once, or reposting in a way that confuses search results, may cause posts to be hidden, removed, or require sponsorship.

Policy requirements

Post each job only once.
  • Each job should appear only once on the platform.
  • Use one clear, accurate job title per role.
  • Make sure the details (pay, location, duties) match across all postings.
  • If a job is available in more than one location, post it once per location.
Don’t repost your job.
  • Update an existing job post instead of creating a new one.
  • Only repost if the role has closed and later reopens.
  • Keep details accurate and consistent across reposted jobs.

Tip: Need to keep a job posting in the search results a little longer? Consider sponsoring your job.

Duplicate or reopened jobs

Posting a duplicate job
  • When a new job appears to be a copy of an existing job, Indeed may prevent you from posting the job.
  • You may be blocked from posting the job before it ever gets moderated.
  • Review your existing job listings to check for an active or recent version of the same role.
Reopening an old job
  • If you re-open an old job, you may see the job as “Flagged”.
  • Contact Indeed for help if your reopened job was flagged.

Important: If you’re asked to sponsor a job but believe it isn’t a duplicate, follow the next steps at the bottom of this article.

What happens if you break this policy

First issues
  • Your job post may be hidden or shown lower in search results.
  • You may be required to sponsor your job post to have it appear.
Repeated or serious issues
  • You may lose access to free or organic job visibility.
  • You may lose access to job seeker matching services.
  • Your account may be restricted or removed from Indeed.

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Check if the role was posted more than once.
  • Update your original job post instead of creating duplicates.
  • Make sure each role is unique, with accurate job details.
  • Post only real, open jobs and update them as needed.

Contact Indeed Support if you believe we removed your post by mistake.

Job Posting Location Policy

This article explains Indeed’s job posting location policy, helping employers choose the correct job location for office-based, hybrid, remote, statewide, nationwide, and travel-based roles. It details how to post jobs accurately by using the most specific address, state-level or nationwide options, and rules for roles requiring travel, ensuring clear job seeker search results.

  • Jun 22, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Appropriate-Use-of-Location-on-Indeed

Title

Job Posting Location Policy

Details

Overview

This policy helps you pick the right job location when posting on Indeed. It covers in-person, fully remote, hybrid, and on-the-road jobs. The rules apply to all employers on the platform.

Following these rules helps your jobs reach more job seekers. All jobs may still appear less frequently or be removed at Indeed’s discretion to ensure job seekers see high-quality job that match their search results.

Job Location Policies

Jobs must be posted with the most specific and accurate location. Location variations are not allowed. For example, if the role requires reporting to an office in Los Angeles, the job should list Los Angeles as the location.

  • Jobs Without a City and/or State: High-quality remote or on-the-road jobs can be posted with a nationwide or statewide location when the role can be performed anywhere within that state or nation. If the job requires on-site work, an address or post code must be provided.
  • Targeting Locations: Only post jobs in locations where your business actually works.

How to Choose the Right Location

In-person jobs

Work that takes place fully on-site at a specific location.

  • Provide the street address where the job seeker will report, or at minimum the postcode.
  • You may hide the street address if needed.
Fully remote jobs

Work that does not require any on-site presence.

  • List the correct state or country if there are residency requirements (for example, must live in a specific state or nation).
  • Learn more about posting a remote job.
Hybrid jobs

Work that includes both on-site and remote components.

  • Provide the street address where the job seeker will report, or at minimum the postcode.
  • You may hide the street address if needed.
On the road or Travel jobs

Work that involves traveling between locations rather than staying in one fixed place.

  • If the job has a starting point, include the street address. If not, list the general area where work occurs.
  • Select On the road if employees can work remotely when they aren’t traveling.
    • In the job description, include:
      • Expected travel percentage (for example, 25% or 75%)
      • Whether you’ll cover travel expenses
      • State that, other than travel, this job can be remote
Exceptions / Special Cases
  • Jobs based in more than one real work location can be posted to each location.
  • A client can post a job statewide or nationwide on Indeed, but this is intended only for roles that are fully remote or truly on-the-road/transitory across the whole state or country. Posting broader-than-city for fixed in-person or hybrid roles is against policy; those should use the most specific city/address possible.

Job Posting Quality Standards

Learn how to create clear, professional job postings that meet Indeed’s quality standards. Improve visibility and avoid removal by writing accurate titles, concise descriptions, and avoiding spam or keyword stuffing. Keywords: job posting standards, Indeed policy, clear job titles, avoid spam, hiring guidelines.

  • Jul 6, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

115005915763

Title

Job Posting Quality Standards

Details

Overview

This policy applies to all employers posting jobs on Indeed. Our goal is to make job posts clear and easy to understand. All jobs should be accurate, easy to read, and professional. Posts that do not meet quality standards may be hidden, removed, or require sponsorship.

Policy requirements

Write clear job descriptions.
  • Include the company name, pay, work hours, benefits, and duties.
  • Use full sentences and professional language.
  • Avoid spam-like content, like all caps, extra punctuation, or too many abbreviations.
  • Keep the tone clear and focused on the job.
Example:
  • Do: “We’re hiring a full-term cashier at [company name]. Pay starts at [amount] per hour. Duties include helping customers, managing the register, and keeping the checkout area clean.”
  • Don’t: “HIRING CASHIER $$$ APPLY NOW!!! GREAT PAY!!!”
Use professional job titles.
  • Keep titles simple, short, and accurate.
  • Use the real job title only.
  • Don’t use clickbait, emojis, symbols, or unclear text.
  • Avoid adding details like location, pay, or shifts. These belong in the job description.
Example:
  • Do: “Warehouse Associate”
  • Don’t: “Warehouse Associate - $20/hr Night Shift!!!”
Avoid keyword stuffing.
  • Don’t repeat the same keywords to boost search results.
  • Don’t list competitors’ names.
  • Don’t include long lists of job titles or company names.
  • Write short sentences that describe the job in a clear way.
Example:
  • Do: “We’re looking for a customer service representative to assist clients by phone and email.”
  • Don’t: “Customer service, customer service, sales, call center, data entry, admin”

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Check the title and description for clear, simple language.
  • Remove extra symbols, repeated words, or spam-like content.
  • Make sure all details about the job are correct.

Contact Indeed Support if you believe we removed your post by mistake.

Maintaining User Safety

Indeed keeps job seekers safe by protecting personal information and requiring employers to follow rules when posting jobs, photos, or videos. Employers should not ask for unnecessary personal data, upload poor-quality or irrelevant photos, or misuse videos, as this can harm privacy and fairness. Following these rules builds trust, attracts good candidates, and keeps jobs visible and compliant worldwide.

  • Mar 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Maintaining-User-Safety-On-Indeed

Title

Maintaining User Safety

Details

Overview

Indeed works to keep job seekers safe and protect their personal information. To give everyone a good experience, you must follow the rules when posting jobs or company content. Misusing personal data, photos, or videos can harm job seekers. It can also create privacy problems, and lead to unfair treatment.

By following these rules, you help build trust and attract good candidates. This keeps Indeed safe for everyone. These rules apply worldwide. If a job breaks them, it may appear lower in search results or lose access to some Indeed features. Employers should not ask for personal details before making a job offer. Doing so too early can make job seekers uncomfortable and discourage strong applicants.

Requesting Personally Identifiable Information (PII)

You can’t ask for personal information that isn’t needed in the job post or application.

Unnecessary PII includes (but is not limited to):

  • Full date of birth
  • Government-issued IDs (for example, social security number, driver’s license)
  • Financial details (for example, bank account or credit card numbers)
  • Sensitive personal documents (for example, copies of passports or birth certificates)
  • Unnecessary contact information (for example, home address or personal phone numbers)

Photos in job posts and company pages

Photos help job seekers understand your workplace and company culture.

Note: If a photo is not allowed, it may be because of quality issues. Avoid uploading blurry, pixelated, or disoriented photos. You can improve the quality and re-upload.

To keep Indeed safe and professional, all photos must meet the following standards:

Relevance and ownership:

  • Photos must relate to your company or the job.
  • You must own the rights or have permission to use the photo.
  • Do not use stock photos unless they are customized to represent your company.

Do not include:

  • Photos of children unless it’s directly related to the job, for example, childcare jobs
  • Blurry, pixelated, or poor-quality images
  • Unsafe or unhealthy work conditions
  • Hate speech, violent, vulgar, or adult content
  • External links, QR codes, or ads
  • Sensitive data (addresses, IDs, financial details, or private company info)

Note: By uploading a photo, you confirm you have the legal right to use it and agree to Indeed’s Terms of Service.

Requesting job seeker videos

Video interviews can save time for both you and job candidates. Job seekers can record at their convenience and re-do answers. However, using videos for other purposes can:

  • Create privacy risks
  • Harm the job seeker experience
  • Lead to misuse or discrimination

Examples of allowed use:

  • Culture fit questions (for example, “What is the last book you read?”)
  • Job-specific screening questions
  • Acting and modeling jobs

Examples of not allowed use:

  • Marketing or advertising purposes
  • Selling video footage
  • Sharing interviews online
  • Requiring unrelated tasks on video

Next steps

If your job post or company content is flagged for violating this policy:

  1. Review the policy section related to your violation (PII, photos, or videos).
  2. Remove or edit the content that does not meet Indeed’s standards.
  3. Re-upload or update your content with compliant information.
  4. If your post was rejected because of photo quality, upload a clearer or sharper image.
  5. For repeated issues, contact Indeed support for help understanding the requirements.

Managing Company Reviews

This article explains how employers can manage Indeed Company Page reviews by encouraging employee feedback, highlighting or featuring positive reviews, and responding to reviews professionally. It also covers options for reporting or moving reviews that violate guidelines or are posted to the wrong page, and provides steps for addressing missing or negative reviews.

  • Sep 12, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

205297680

Title

Managing Company Reviews

Details

Overview

Company Page reviews help job seekers learn what it’s like to work at your business. They also give you insights into what employees value and where you can improve. With a claimed Company Page, you can respond to reviews, highlight positive feedback, and show potential hires that you value transparency. This guide is for employers who want to manage reviews on their Company Page.

Why reviews matter

  • Build trust: Job seekers rely on authentic feedback before applying.
  • Give employees a voice: Reviews let employees share their experiences.
  • Gain insights: Regularly checking reviews helps you understand employee needs.

How to manage reviews effectively

Encourage employees to leave and rate reviews
  • Explain why reviews matter for both employees and job seekers.
  • Ask current employees to leave honest reviews.
  • Employees can mark reviews as helpful or not helpful.
  • The most helpful reviews are highlighted for job seekers.

Did you know? Employers with Employer Branding Hub (EBH) can select a Featured review to show at the top of the Reviews tab.

Respond to reviews the right way

Find out more about responding to reviews and Q&A in our Company Reviews policy.

Check reviews regularly

Make time to review and respond so job seekers see you’re active.

How to respond to reviews

  1. Select Add a Comment near the bottom of the review.
  2. Type your response in the text box.
  3. Click or tap Add Comment to publish your response.

How to report or correct reviews

When reviews can be removed
  • Reviews are not removed unless they break our content guidelines.
  • They may also be redacted (text removed, star rating kept).
  • Only the reviewer can delete their own post.
Report a review that violates guidelines
  1. Find the review on your Company Page.
  2. Use the Report option to flag it.
  3. Our team will review and decide if it violates policy.
Move an irrelevant review
  • You can request to move a review posted to the wrong Company Page.
  • Only employers with claimed pages can submit a migration request.
Missing reviews
  • New reviews usually appear within 72 hours.
  • Contact support if more than five business days pass and the review is still missing.

FAQs

Question: Can I remove a negative review?

Answer: No. Reviews stay up unless they violate our content guidelines. You can respond professionally or report the review if it doesn’t follow our guidelines.

Question: Can an employee post more than one review?

Answer: No. Each employee can post one review per company. They may submit a new review that follows our guidelines if their review is removed.

Managing Indeed Browser Notifications

Indeed browser notifications are short alerts sent to your computer or phone. They keep you updated on interview times, calls, and job post changes, even when Indeed is not open in your browser. Turn on browser notifications

When you sign in to Indeed, a pop-up may appear asking if you want notifications.

  1. Click or tap Turn on notifications or Notify me in the pop-up.

You’ll start getting Indeed notifications right away.

  • Google Chrome
  • Firefox
  • Safari
  • Microsoft Edge
  • Opera

Troubleshooting

Issue: I enabled browser notifications, but I’m still not getting them.

Solution: Your device’s system settings may be blocking them — even if your browser is set to allow them. Update your system notification settings using the steps for your operating system:

  • Mac OS
  • Windows

Managing Sponsored Job Budgets

When creating sponsored jobs on Indeed, you can choose a budget to help your job get more visibility, or you can use Indeed’s recommended budget.

  • May 8, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

360026629712

Title

Managing Sponsored Job Budgets

Details

Overview

A sponsored job budget controls how much you spend on average per day to advertise a job posting on Indeed. It affects how often job seekers see your job and works on a pay-per-performance basis.

Plan and budget options

When sponsoring a job, you choose a plan and set a daily budget:

  • Standard
    • Uses Indeed’s recommended daily budget for your job.
    • This amount is set automatically and can’t be edited.
  • Premium
    • Uses a higher recommended daily budget for more visibility.
    • This amount is set automatically and can’t be edited.
  • Custom
    • Set your own daily amount within your market’s limits.

Your actual daily spend may vary but will average out to your set daily budget over time.

Standard
  • Recommended by Indeed based on your job title, location, and market competition
  • Works well for most hiring needs
  • The daily budget is set automatically—you can’t edit it under this plan

To use a different amount, switch to Custom.

Premium
  • Designed for more visibility and relevant results
  • Works best when a role is hard to fill, requires multiple hires, or is urgent
  • The daily budget is set automatically at a higher rate—you can’t edit it under this plan

To spend more or less than the Premium amount, switch to Custom. A high enough custom budget may still qualify for Premium-level visibility, depending on your market.

Learn more about the advantages of Using Premium Sponsored Jobs.

Custom
  • Set your own daily budget within your market’s minimum and maximum limits.
  • A barometer indicates how well your sponsored job is likely to perform at your chosen budget.
  • Higher custom budgets may unlock Premium-level performance in some markets.

The minimum daily budget varies by market.

Update your budget

  1. Sign in to your Indeed Employer account.
  2. Navigate to your Jobs page.
  3. Click or tap the three dots next to the job you want to edit.
  4. Select Manage budget.
  5. Update your plan, daily budget amount, or duration.
  6. Save your changes. It may take a little while for changes to show in your reports.
Update your plan duration
  • Continuous (no end date) — Maximum weekly budget = 7.5 × daily budget
  • Set running time (like 15 or 30 days) — Maximum total budget = daily budget × number of days
  • Custom end date — Maximum total budget = daily budget × number of days

Daily spend may go above or below your set amount. However, it will never exceed the overall maximum for your plan period.

FAQs

Question: Can I set a monthly budget?

Answer:No. Budgets are daily. The product shows estimated weekly or total spend so you can plan accordingly.

Question: Is there a price list?

Answer:No. There’s no fixed public price list. You choose your budget during setup, within your market’s limits.

Question: Can I change my end date?

Answer:Yes — at any time, as long as the job is still open and eligible for sponsorship.

Question: Can I switch between Standard, Premium, and Custom?

Answer:Yes. You can switch between plans at any time. Changes take effect going forward and may affect visibility and performance.

Question: Can my daily spend go above or below my set budget?

Answer:Yes. Your daily budget is an average, not a strict cap. Indeed will not exceed:

  • 7.5 × your daily budget per week (continuous plans)
  • Daily budget × number of days (fixed or custom end date plans)

Tip: If your spend seems off, review your plan, budget settings, and targeting. For further help, contact Indeed Support or your Indeed representative.

Title

Messaging Candidates on Indeed

Send messages to individual candidates on Indeed to gauge their interest, schedule an interview, or start the conversation.

FAQs

Question: Can job seekers see if I’m actively online?

Answer: Maybe. It depends on your individual settings. In your Communication settings, you can toggle Show online status on or off. Job seekers will see a green dot next to your name if you’re signed in and Show online status is on.

Question: Can I see if job seekers are actively online?

Answer: Maybe. It depends on the job seeker’s and your individual settings. If a job seeker is signed in and has turned on this feature, you’ll see a green dot next to their name. However, you won’t be able to see the online status of job seekers who’ve enabled this feature if your Show online status is off.

Misleading Content and Activities

This article explains Indeed’s Misleading Content and Activities Policy, helping employers understand how to create clear, accurate, and trustworthy job posts. It covers requirements for posting real, open jobs with correct details on pay, duties, titles, company name, and location, outlines restrictions on fake, vague, or misleading listings, and explains how to fix or appeal removed posts. Employers learn how to stay compliant by using accurate job titles, verified company names, transparent pay and application processes, and proper use of trademarks and locations.

  • Mar 27, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Misleading-Content-and-Activities

Title

Misleading Content and Activities

Details

Overview

This policy applies to all employers posting jobs on Indeed. Our goal is to make job searches clear, accurate, and trustworthy. Posts that could confuse or mislead job seekers may be removed, hidden, or require sponsorship.

Posting requirements

Post real and accurate jobs.
  • List only open and active jobs. Remove expired or filled jobs.
  • Don’t post ads, training programs, franchise opportunities, or multi-level marketing offers.
  • Give full and accurate details about pay, job duties, and work type. Keep these the same wherever you post the job.
  • Don’t use vague, confusing, or misleading language.
  • Don’t post fake jobs.
  • Don’t post jobs just to build a candidate list if no real role exists.
  • Don’t ask candidates to apply elsewhere or say that Indeed applications won’t be considered.
Use clear and correct job titles.
  • Use one job title per role.
  • Don’t post the same job under different titles.
  • Avoid clickbait titles like “Apply Now!” or “Sign-on Bonus!”
  • Don’t add emojis, symbols, or extra details like pay, shifts, or locations in the title.

Did you know? We have more information about job descriptions and titles in our Job Posting Quality Standards article.

Provide the company name.
  • Use a real, verifiable company name.
  • Posts that use a confidential or generic company name may not be allowed or may require sponsorship.
  • Don’t hide your identity or pretend to be another employer.

For more details, read our Generic or Confidential Company Names article.

Post as an authorized employer.
  • Only the hiring company or an approved recruiter can post jobs.
  • Don’t post jobs for another business without their permission.
  • Job boards and aggregators must clearly show who the real employer is.
Use trademarks appropriately.
  • Only use your own brand names or trademarks.
  • Don’t include competitor names or suggest false partnerships.
  • Don’t use well-known brand names just to attract attention.
Use the correct job location.
  • Use the appropriate job type (onsite, remote, or hybrid).
  • Post jobs to the accurate, specific work location:
    • Office jobs: Use the street address or postcode (you may hide the street address from public view).
    • Hybrid or remote jobs: Clearly state this in the job type.
    • Statewide roles: Only if people can work from anywhere in the state.
    • Nationwide roles: Only if people can work from anywhere in the country.
    • Travel jobs: State how much travel is needed, if you cover expenses, and if the role may be remote when not traveling.
  • Jobs posted to a place outside the actual worksite are not allowed.
  • If a role is based in more than one real location, you may post to each location separately.

Read more in our Job Posting Location Policy article.

Quality and transparency requirements

Describe sales and marketing roles clearly.
  • Commission-only or direct sales jobs may need to:
    • Show pay type (base salary, base + commission, or commission-only)
    • State whether the worker is an employee or a contractor
    • Explain the work setting (door-to-door, in-store, remote)
    • List the product or service being sold
    • Provide clear daily tasks
  • Commission-only jobs that are work-from-home or remote, or contain a misleading salary, may require sponsorship.
  • We may remove posts with unclear or misleading sales or marketing details.
Ensure job posts are fair and professional.
  • Don’t use offensive, manipulative, or misleading language.
  • Jobs should appear in search results based on relevance. Don’t use tricks to boost visibility.
  • Job seekers should never pay to apply.
  • Don’t include discriminatory content. Base your hiring decisions on the ability to do the job.

Read more in our Discrimination and Hate Offenses article.

Keep the application process direct and transparent.
  • Job seekers should be able to apply directly through the job post link.
  • Don’t redirect applicants to another website.
If you use a talent network:
  • Joining must be optional and separate from the job application.
  • Only add candidates who give clear, informed consent.
  • Only send emails about new jobs at your company.
  • Don’t share job seekers’ contact details with others.

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Review your post considering the guidelines above.
  • Fix anything inaccurate, missing, or misleading.
  • Remove redirects or forced sign-ups.
  • Make sure job titles, pay, and location match the real role.

Contact Indeed Support if you believe we removed your post by mistake.

My job posting is showing incorrect information

Learn how to edit a job posting on Indeed by choosing the correct method—update directly in your Indeed dashboard, modify synced fields for jobs connected via an ATS or career site (with note on breaking the connection), or make changes in your ATS/career site if the job isn’t linked—and ensure updates display correctly by optimizing job description details like salary, schedule, and location.

  • Apr 24, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

My-job-information-is-showing-incorrect-information

Title

My job posting is showing incorrect information

Details

Overview

You can fix incorrect information on your job listing. How you do this depends on where you posted the job: on Indeed or through a hiring system like an Applicant Tracking System (ATS) or career site.

Edit jobs posted directly on Indeed

If you posted your job on Indeed (not from your career site or ATS):

  1. Sign in to your Indeed account
  2. Go to your Jobs dashboard
  3. Select Managed on Indeed
  4. Select the job you want to edit
  5. Select Edit job
  6. Select the pencil icon next to the details you want to change
  7. Select Save changes

To check your changes, select View public job page on the selected job. Changes usually appear within a few minutes.

Edit jobs connected from your ATS or career site

You may have connected your Indeed account to your ATS or career site. If so, you can edit these jobs on Indeed. Fields marked Synced with career site come from your hiring system. You can still change them on Indeed.

Note: Changes you make on Indeed only update the Indeed listing. They don’t update your career site or ATS. To update those systems, make changes directly in them.

To edit connected jobs:

  1. Sign in to your Indeed account
  2. Go to your Jobs dashboard
  3. Select All jobs to show career site jobs
  4. Select the job you want to edit
  5. Select the pencil icon next to the details you want to change
  6. Select Save changes

Important: Once you edit a connected job on Indeed, the connection to your career site is broken. You’ll need to make all future edits on Indeed.

Edit jobs in your ATS or career site

If you can’t find the job in your Indeed account, but you can see it in Indeed’s search results, your account is not connected to your career site or ATS. Make changes directly in your career site or ATS instead.

Changes will appear on Indeed within a few hours, but can take up to 24 hours. This depends on how busy our system is and how your connection is set up.

How Indeed gets job information from your career site

Indeed gets information from your ATS fields. If a detail doesn’t have a matching field in your ATS, Indeed may pull it from your job description.

To make sure Indeed shows details correctly:

Use clear, simple words in your job description. See examples below:

  • Pay or salary – Include words like “pay,” “salary,” or “compensation” with dollar signs ($) or USD
  • Schedule or shift – Add the word “shift” and “schedule details” before you list the details
  • Location type – Say if the job is remote, hybrid, on the road, or in the office

Troubleshooting

If you’ve tried the steps above but your job still shows incorrect information, try these solutions.

Issue: Your edit is under review

Solution: Some edits require review to ensure your job meets posting guidelines. The review usually completes within 24 hours. Your job will update automatically once approved. You don’t need to take any action during the review. Check back in 24 hours to see your updated job listing.

Issue: Your browser shows an old version

Solution: Your browser may be showing cached content. Clear your browser cache and cookies, then check your job listing again.

If your job information remains incorrect after 24 hours, contact Indeed Support. Include:

  • Your job title
  • The incorrect information you’re seeing
  • What you expect to see instead

We’ll investigate the issue and help resolve it.

Non-Work Opportunities

This article helps users understand Indeed’s policy that all posts must be for real, paid jobs, detailing what content is disallowed, such as non-work opportunities, candidate pooling/general applications, passive income roles, and unpaid internships/trials, and noting that only non-profit volunteer roles are permitted.

  • Nov 18, 2024
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Policy-Non-Job-Posting

Title

Non-Work Opportunities

Details

Overview

Indeed is a job site. All posts must be for real, paid jobs. Posts that offer unpaid work, fake openings, unpaid internships, or other non-job content are not allowed. We may remove or hide posts that could confuse job seekers or break these rules.

This policy is global, but some markets have different rules for certain types of jobs.

Posting requirements

Post real and accurate jobs
  • Don’t post jobs that aren’t real.
  • These include, but aren’t limited to:
    • Ads for services or products
    • Test posts
    • Business opportunities
    • Posts with nonsense words
Avoid candidate pooling
  • Don’t post fake jobs to collect resumes for a database (also called a “bait-and-switch”).
  • These posts are misleading to job seekers and a waste of their time.
  • Examples include jobs labeled “talent bank” or “general application”.
Don’t post passive income opportunities
  • “Passive income” roles that offer money for little to no work aren’t allowed.
  • Examples are:
    • Paid surveys
    • Medical trials
Make sure jobs are paid
  • All jobs must pay money for work done.
  • This includes any training or trial periods.
  • We don’t allow jobs that offer only “experience” or “exposure.”
Unpaid internships

All internships on Indeed must be:

  • Paid
  • Listed as “internship” in the title
Jobs kept open for indefinite periods

Keeping jobs open without a definite hiring need is not allowed.

Misleading job titles or descriptions

Don’t use:

  • Vague or misleading job titles
  • Descriptions that don’t match an actual, available job opening
Unpaid trial periods

Jobs can’t need a trial shift without pay.

Volunteer roles
  • We only allow true volunteer roles at non-profit organizations.
  • The job title must say “volunteer.”
  • We don’t allow volunteer roles at for-profit companies.

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Review your post considering the guidelines above.
  • Contact Indeed support if you believe we removed your post by mistake.

Not Receiving Emails from Indeed?

Ensure you receive all emails from Indeed by checking your Spam or Junk folder, confirming your job-specific application email settings, and verifying your employer account email and communication preferences.

  • Feb 25, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Why-am-I-not-receiving-emails-from-Indeed

Title

Not Receiving Emails from Indeed?

Details

Overview

If you’re not getting emails from Indeed, start by checking your Spam or Junk folder. Next, make sure your email settings for each job and your account settings are set up correctly.

Step 1: Check your spam or junk folders

Sometimes our messages are filtered by mistake. Look for emails from the following addresses and mark them as Not spam:

  • @indeed.com
  • @indeedapply.com
  • @indeedmail.com
  • @indeedemail.com
  • @do-not-reply@indeed.com

To make sure future messages reach your inbox, add these addresses to your safe sender list. If you’re not sure how to do that, your IT team can help—or check your email provider’s help center for instructions.

Step 2: Review your job post’s specific email settings

  1. Go to Jobs (briefcase icon) in the left-hand menu or visit employers.indeed.com/jobs.
  2. Find your job, click the three dots (⋮), and select Edit job.
  3. Scroll to Application method and click the pencil icon to edit.
  4. Check your Application method and email address.
    1. To receive an email for every new application, select Send an individual email each time someone applies. If unchecked, you’ll receive a Daily Summary Email instead.
  5. Click Done, then scroll down and select Save Changes.

Step 3: Check your account email settings

  1. Sign in to your employer account.
  2. Click your email address in the top-right corner of your dashboard.
  3. Select Employer settings.
  4. Under the Profile tab, verify that your email address is correct.
  5. In the Communications tab, make sure your Budget and Commercial email notifications are turned on.

Job posts on Indeed allow employers to advertise their open positions to a wide audience of potential applicants. When you post a job on Indeed, it becomes visible to job seekers, who can then apply for the role. This article explains how to post a job and what to do if issues come up.

There are two ways to post a job:

  2. From your career site or Applicant Tracking System (ATS)

Posting a Remote Job

This article explains how employers can post or update remote and hybrid jobs on Indeed, including how to set job location details, use XML fields, and clarify occasional work-from-home options. Employers will learn step-by-step instructions for posting through their Indeed account or XML feeds while ensuring compliance with the Job Posting Location Policy.

  • Nov 18, 2024
  • Knowledge

URL Name

How-to-Post-a-Remote-Job-on-Indeed

Title

Posting a Remote Job

Details

Overview

Remote, hybrid, and on the road jobs give people flexibility in where they work. A remote job means no regular in-office time. A hybrid job means the job is split between in office and from home or another location. An on the road job means employees are expected to travel for work and may not have a consistent starting location or office.

Key details for posting remote jobs

Posting your job with the right location type helps job seekers find it more easily. It also ensures your posting follows our Job Posting Location Policy.

Remote and hybrid role requirements

Be clear about whether your role is fully remote, hybrid, or on the road. This helps job seekers know what to expect.

Suggested keywords for job descriptions

  • Fully remote roles

    • “This is a remote position”
    • “Fully remote”
    • “100% remote”

  • Hybrid roles

    • “Hybrid remote/in-office”
    • “Hybrid work schedule”
    • “Work from home [x] days per week”

  • On the road roles

    • “Travelling job title”
    • “On the road”
    • “Travelling required”
How to list occasional work-from-home options

Some jobs are mostly in-person but allow occasional work-from-home days. These are not remote or hybrid jobs. If you want to highlight this benefit, use phrases like:

  • “Flexibility to work from home as needed”
  • “Work from home days possible”
  • “Ability to occasionally work from home”
Location information for remote and hybrid jobs

Even when you post a job as remote or hybrid, we still ask for a location. If you decline to provide residency requirements for a fully remote job, Indeed will assign the entire country as the location. This is expected and ensures the job appears broadly.

For hybrid jobs, provide the exact in-person location. List the correct state or region for fully remote jobs with residency restrictions (for example, must live in a specific state for tax purposes).

Posting a remote or hybrid job

  1. Sign in to or create an employer account on Indeed.

  2. Go to your Jobs page.

  3. Select Post a Job in the top-right corner.

  4. Select Create a brand new post and then Continue.

  5. Fill out job basics:

    1. Enter the Job title.

    2. Select the Job location type:

      1. In person
      2. Fully remote: no on-site work required
      3. Hybrid: some on-site work required
      4. On the road

    3. Provide the job location (street address or ZIP code).

    4. Add residency requirements if needed for remote jobs, or the exact location for hybrid work.

Updating a job to be a remote or hybrid job

Important: We recommend creating a new post if you need to make significant changes, such as changing the job’s location. For more information, read our article on editing your job posts.

  1. Sign in to your employer account on Indeed.
  2. Go to your Jobs page.
  3. Select the three dots on the job you want to edit.
  4. Select Edit job.
  5. Click the pencil icon next to Location.
  6. Use the dropdown to change the Job location type.
  7. Select Done and then Save changes.

Posting a remote or hybrid job using XML files

If you post jobs via XML, add a 0 field with one of these values:

  • Fully Remote
  • Hybrid Remote

If the job is fully remote but has residency rules (for example, must live in a certain state), include that in the 0 field of the XML. It is rare to use city-based restrictions, so use state or region instead.

FAQs

Question: Do I have to list a location for a remote job?

Answer: Yes. If you don’t provide a specific residency requirement, Indeed will set the entire country as the location by default.

Question: Can I post my job as remote or hybrid if it’s in-person with occasional work-from-home days?

Answer: No. These jobs aren’t considered remote or hybrid. Instead, describe the perk in your job description using phrases like “Flexibility to work from home as needed.”

Posting Jobs From Your Career Site or Applicant Tracking System (ATS)

This article explains how employers can have their jobs automatically listed on Indeed by either requesting job aggregation from their career site or using an integrated Applicant Tracking System (ATS). It outlines requirements for aggregation (unique URLs, full descriptions, apply methods) and highlights that ATS integration—preferred under Indeed’s single-source feed policy—helps prevent duplicate listings, improve application quality, and enable enhanced candidate matching features.

  • Jan 28, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

What-is-Aggregation

Title

Posting Jobs From Your Career Site or Applicant Tracking System (ATS)

Details

Overview

You can ask us to pull jobs from your career site or Applicant Tracking System (ATS) if you don’t want to post jobs directly on Indeed. This is called job aggregation. Indeed uses aggregation technology to collect jobs from all over the internet. These jobs are then listed on Indeed.

Requesting job aggregation

Aggregation requirements

Your job posts must include:

  • A clear, complete job title
  • A full job description (find out how to write a good job description)
  • One job location per job
  • A way for job seekers to apply, like an email address or application link
  • A unique, valid job URL for each job post

If you’re using an XML feed instead of a website, check our XML guidelines.

What happens next
  • After you request aggregation, it may take some time for your jobs to appear on Indeed.
  • Timing depends on how your website is set up and how many requests we’re handling.

Important: Meeting these requirements does not guarantee that your jobs will be aggregated on Indeed. We’ll review all jobs and websites before they are posted.

Using ATS integration instead

Important: If your ATS is already integrated with Indeed, you must use that integration to post jobs on Indeed. Learn more about this in our single-source feed policy.

What is a single-source feed?

A single-source feed is a job feed that includes jobs from only one employer. These feeds are sometimes created by the company itself or by an agency on its behalf.

Why use your ATS integration?
  • Prevent duplicate or conflicting job listings
  • Improve application quality and conversion rates
  • Enable stronger candidate matching via Indeed Apply, Candidate Sync, and future ATS capabilities

Prohibited Job Seeker Costs

Indeed prohibits job posts that require job seekers to pay fees, make investments, or assume financial risk. Roles involving application fees, MLM schemes, or unpaid training are not allowed and may be hidden or removed. Employers should ensure posts describe genuine paid opportunities with clear details on costs, training, and relocation support to stay compliant.

  • Mar 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Prohibited-Job-Seeker-Costs

Title

Prohibited Job Seeker Costs

Details

Overview

This policy applies to all employers posting jobs on Indeed. Our goal is to provide job seekers with fair and trustworthy opportunities. Job posts that ask job seekers to pay fees, make investments, or take on financial risks are not allowed on our site. These jobs may be removed, hidden, or require sponsorship.

Types of jobs not allowed on Indeed

Roles requiring the job seeker to pay fees
  • Don’t ask job seekers to pay a fee to apply, interview, or be hired.
  • Don’t ask contractors to use a specific brand or model tool.
Examples of fees that aren’t allowed:
  • Application or start-up fees
  • Background check fees
  • Training costs
  • Uniform or licensing costs
Exceptions
  • Equipment independent contractors must have
  • Chair or booth rentals in cosmetology
Business opportunities
  • Don’t post jobs that promote starting a new business.
  • Don’t ask job seekers to invest money up front with no guarantee of income or security.
Exceptions
  • Contractor or subcontractor roles
Multi-level marketing (MLM) or network marketing schemes
  • Only post real jobs, not MLM or networking marketing opportunities.
  • Don’t ask job seekers to buy products, pay for memberships, or purchase marketing tools.
Examples of costs found in MLMs:
  • Starter kits
  • Membership fees
  • Required product purchases
  • Marketing materials
  • Training or convention fees
Training opportunities
  • Posts that advertise training courses or programs are not allowed.
Exceptions

Paid training may be allowed if all these apply:

  • Job seekers are paid during the entire training period.
  • The job description explains what happens after training.
  • After training, job seekers either:
    • Get a job offer, or
    • Pay no fees and receive a certificate, license, or skills to help them find work.
Truck driving jobs
  • Truck driving job posts must state the type of role in the description. Posts missing this detail may not show up as often.
Types of trucking roles
  • Owner-Operator: Job seeker owns the truck and covers equipment costs.
  • Company Driver: Employer provides the truck at no cost.
  • Lease Purchase: Job seeker leases the truck through the employer. Include costs and payment plans in the job post.
Relocation roles
  • Jobs requiring relocation must include details of any employer assistance with moving costs.
  • Employers may reimburse relocation costs after the move.
  • You may not list jobs outside of their true working locations.

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Review the guidelines above.
  • Remove fees, costs, or investment requirements.
  • Make sure your post describes a real job with clear details about pay, role type, and location.

Contact Indeed Support if you think we removed your post by mistake.

A Smart Sourcing subscription lets you contact candidates with resumes on Indeed. Review the subscription plans and pricing options to find the best fit for your hiring needs. This article explains how to buy a Smart Sourcing subscription as well as contacts.

How to purchase a Smart Sourcing subscription

Watch now: Smart Sourcing with Sourcing Assistant webinar

  1. Go to your Subscriptions page.

  3. Select Buy contacts.
  4. Enter the number of contacts you’d like to buy.

  1. Go to your Subscriptions page.

  4. Confirm the details and Place order.

Question: Do my Smart Sourcing contacts expire?

Answer: Yes. Smart Sourcing contacts expire after 6 months.

Question: How do I transfer contacts to another user?

Answer: You can only move contacts to your shared pool or to someone with a Professional subscription. Here’s how:

  1. Go to your Subscriptions page.
  2. Scroll down to Subscriptions and click the three dots next to the one you want.
  3. Choose the user you want to move contacts from.
  4. Choose the user or shared pool you want to move the contacts to.

Recognizing Phishing Scams

This article helps you recognize phishing scams by identifying common tactics (copied logos, urgent messages, suspicious links, fake email addresses, and requests for personal information) and explains how to protect your account through enabling two-factor authentication (2FA), using strong unique passwords, or reviewing account activity regularly. If you suspect phishing or account compromise, you can protect yourself by not clicking suspicious links, deleting the message, changing your password immediately, enabling 2FA, contacting Indeed support, or reporting suspicious activity to help protect the community.

  • Jun 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Recognizing-phishing-scams

Title

Recognizing Phishing Scams

Details

Overview

Scammers may pose as Indeed to trick you into sharing personal information. This could be your password or a two-factor authentication (2FA) code. They use this information to access your account and sensitive data. This tactic is called phishing.

Recognizing phishing tactics is the best way to protect yourself and your account. In this article, we’ll share a few common scams. We’ll also explain how to keep your account safe, and what to do if you suspect a phishing scam.

Common phishing tactics

Copied logos and design
  • Scammers copy Indeed’s logo, colors, and email format to make their messages seem real.
  • A recognizable Indeed logo doesn’t always mean the email came from Indeed.
Urgent or threatening messages
  • Scammers create pressure by demanding immediate action.
  • They may threaten to suspend your account or delete your data if you don’t respond fast.
    • Example: “We’ll disable your account in 24 hours unless you verify your information now.”
Suspicious links
  • Phishing emails often include links that lead to fake websites or mobile app stores.
  • They’re designed to steal your sign-in information or install malware on your device.
How to check a link
  • Hover over the link without clicking to see where it leads.
  • Check for misspelled domains like “Lndeed.com” or “ind33d.com” instead of “indeed.com.”
  • Don’t click if the link doesn’t go to an indeed.com domain.

Important: Some fake sites may redirect you to the real site after you enter your credentials. This makes the scam harder to detect.

Fake email addresses

Scammers use email addresses that look like Indeed’s official domains with slight variations.

Official Indeed domains
  • @indeed.com
  • @indeedemail.com
Fake domains
  • @ihdeedemployer.com
  • @Indeed.com (with a lowercase “L”)
  • Any other variation

Tip: If you’re unsure about an email, sign in to indeed.com instead of clicking links in the message.

Requests for personal information

Scammers may ask for your information through:

  • Web forms or surveys
  • Email replies
  • Text messages (also called “smishing”)
What Indeed will never ask for
  • Your password outside of the official sign-in page on indeed.com
  • Your 2FA code
  • Downloads of software to access your account

Protecting your account

Enable two-factor authentication (2FA)
  • 2FA adds an extra layer of security to your account.
  • Learn more about 2FA and how to set it up.
Use strong, unique passwords

Create a password that:

  • Is at least 8 characters long
  • Includes a mix of letters, numbers, and symbols
  • Is different from the passwords you use on other sites
Review your account often

Check your account activity for any suspicious behavior, such as:

  • Jobs you didn’t post
  • Messages you didn’t send
  • Applications you didn’t submit
  • Changes to your profile you didn’t make

What to do if you suspect phishing

If you receive a suspicious email or text
  1. Don’t click any links or download any attachments.
  2. Don’t reply to the message.
  3. Mark it as spam.
  4. Delete it immediately.
If you think your account was compromised
  1. Change your password immediately by signing in to your Indeed account.
  2. Enable 2FA if you haven’t already.
  3. Contact our support team right away.

Learn more in our article Think You Were Hacked? Here’s What to Do.

Report suspicious activity

Help protect the Indeed community by reporting phishing attempts and suspicious activity. Contact our support team if you:

  • Notice unauthorized activity on your account
  • Encounter a fake Indeed website or job posting
  • Have questions about whether a communication is legitimate

Reporting a Job Seeker Message or Resume

See how to report a resume or message on Indeed. Follow simple steps to flag spam, fraud, or misuse and help keep the platform safe.

  • Apr 24, 2026

URL Name

Inappropriate-Behavior-From-a-Job-Seeker

Title

Reporting a Job Seeker Message or Resume

Details

Overview

Indeed aims to provide a positive, professional experience for all users. While we don’t check job seeker credentials, you can report resumes that may violate our Job Seeker Guidelines.

When to report a message or resume

Report issues such as:

  • Malware
  • Spam
  • Fraud
  • Solicitation
  • Inappropriate or abusive communication
  • Resume misuse

How to report a job seeker message

There are a few ways to report a job seeker message.

From your email

When you receive email notifications about new messages from job seekers, you’ll see the option to Report this message to Indeed.

From your account
  1. Go to your Messages.
  2. Select the conversation you’d like to report.
  3. Click or tap on the three dots menu and Report conversation.
  4. Follow the prompt and select Report.
Contacting Indeed

Contact us directly to report it.

How to report a resume

If you have the Smart Sourcing subscription
  1. Sign in to your Indeed account and open the Smart Sourcing tab.
  2. Find and select the candidate whose resume you want to report.
  3. In the candidate overview, click the three-dot menu.
  4. Select Report this resume.
  5. Choose the reason.
  6. Click Report this resume to submit your request.
If you do not have the Smart Sourcing subscription

If you don’t have Smart Sourcing, contact us directly to report a resume.

What happens next

We’ll review the resume and investigate the report. If the resume violates our guidelines, it may be edited or removed. While not all reports result in action, your feedback helps us keep Indeed safe and professional for everyone.

Running an Indeed Hiring Event

Learn how to access the virtual interview lobby, invite interviewers, message candidates, start and end interviews, and properly close your Indeed Interview Hiring Event.

  • Mar 18, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Running-an-Indeed-Interview-event

Title

Running an Indeed Hiring Event

Details

Overview

After creating a Hiring Event, you’re ready to start interviewing. This article will explain how to access your virtual interview lobby and run your event.

Accessing the lobby

You’ll receive an email from Indeed with a link to Join the interview lobby. If you haven’t received this email, ask the event organizer to add you as an interviewer. We strongly recommend you sign in one day before the event to ensure everything is set up properly.

As soon as you join the lobby, you’ll have access to the Hiring Event. You can start interviewing as soon as the event begins. For the best experience, we recommend running your event with Google Chrome.

Note: You must have an Indeed account to access the lobby as an interviewer.

Adding interviewers

  1. Go to your Hiring event dashboard.
  2. Open the three-dot menu next to your status.
  3. Select Invite interviewers.
  4. Enter the email addresses of the interviewers you’d like to add.

Messaging candidates

When they join the event

Set a welcome message for candidates joining the event. You can set this message before your interview or event date.

  1. Open the three-dot menu next to your status.
  2. Select Update welcome message.
  3. Enter your welcome message.
  4. Select Save.
When they’re waiting in the lobby

You can make an announcement to candidates waiting in the lobby. This feature is helpful if interviews are running behind or you have a large number of candidates in the lobby.

  1. Open the three-dot menu next to your status.
  2. Select Make Announcement.
  3. Enter your announcement message.
  4. Select Save.

Note: We recommend assigning one person to update the welcome message and make announcements.

Starting or joining an interview

Select I’m ready to interview or set your status to active to make yourself available to join or start an interview. Set your status to Away at any time to take a break.

In the Interviewing tab, you’ll see the option to Start interview next to the candidate’s name, or Join interview. The second option lets you join a panel-style interview in progress.

Note: The number of available candidates will never exceed the number of available interviewers.

Ending an interview

Ending the call

When the interview time slot is over, you’ll see a pop-up window to complete the interview.

  • Select Yes if you’re done interviewing the candidate. End the call and provide feedback on whether the candidate is a good fit.
  • If you want the candidate to return to the lobby and be interviewed by someone else, select No and end the call. Alert the next interviewer that the candidate is ready.

Note: Candidates will not see the feedback you provide.

Returning a candidate to the lobby

Once the candidate returns to the lobby, they must return to the waiting room and join the interview room. The candidate must take these actions on their end; support can’t place a user back in the interview room.

When the interview is complete, the interviewer must select End Call.

Note: Don’t close the window or tab before selecting End call, or the system may register that the interview is ongoing.

Ending the event

End the event when you’re finished interviewing all candidates and are ready to close the entire Virtual Hiring Event. For multi-day events, don’t end the event until the final day.

  1. Open the three-dot menu and select End Interviewing.
  2. Select the red Yes, end interviewing button.

Tip: Designate one person to end the event.

FAQs

Question: Can I view recorded interviews?

Answer: Yes. If recording was enabled for the hiring event and you were an interviewer, you’ll get an email with a link to view the recording. Ask your Indeed Representative to generate a new link if you accidentally deleted it.

Scheduling Interviews on Indeed

Learn how to schedule, confirm, and manage interviews on Indeed. Set up interview requests, coordinate availability, edit or cancel interviews, add team members, and track post-interview notes using Indeed interview scheduling and candidate management tools.

  • May 13, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

How-to-Use-the-Indeed-Scheduling-Tool-to-Schedule-Interviews

Title

Scheduling Interviews on Indeed

Details

Overview

Indeed makes it easy to interview candidates. You can message them, manage your schedule, and take notes after each interview. This article shows you how to set up and manage interviews on Indeed.

Setting up an interview

After you post a job and get applications, you can start scheduling interviews. Here’s how:

  1. Sign in to your employer account.
  2. Go to the Candidates tab.
  3. Click or tap the three dots next to the candidate’s name, then select Set up interview.
  4. Set the following:
    1. Duration
    2. Format
    3. Add hiring team members (optional)
    4. Enable text reminders (optional)
    5. Select a specific time
  5. Click or tap Send interview request.

Receiving confirmation from the candidate

  • The candidate will be asked to confirm after they get the invite.
  • When they confirm, you’ll get an email with a calendar invite file (.ics). You can sync this with your calendar.
  • If you set up a video interview, you’ll get a video link once it’s confirmed.

Viewing and editing your interviews

Navigate to the Interviews tab to check all your interviews. From this page, you can:

  • Join
  • Reschedule
  • Cancel
  • Add team member(s)
  • Take notes

Important: When you change an interview, an updated invite is sent to the candidate right away.

Post-interview actions

After the interview, you can:

  • Schedule a follow-up interview
  • Add notes (these won’t be shared with the candidate)
  • Send a message to the candidate

Troubleshooting

Issue: The candidate hasn’t confirmed the interview.

Solution: Select the Candidates tab and send them a follow-up message. You can also reschedule or pick a new time from the Interviews tab.

Issue: I didn’t get a calendar invite file (.ics) after confirmation.

Solution: Check your spam folder first. If it’s not there, check the Interviews tab to find your interview details. You can add the event to your calendar from there.

Issue: I set up a video interview, but didn’t get a video link.

Solution: Video links are sent once the candidate confirms. If the interview is confirmed and you still don’t have a link, navigate to the Interviews tab and check the details. The video link is visible there, even if you can’t find the original email. You can join directly from that page.

Set up automated communications in Smart Screening

Set up automated messages and interview scheduling in Smart Screening to quickly engage qualified applicants. Learn to send no-reply emails, trigger interview invites based on screening scores, and manage automated actions to streamline hiring workflows.

  • Mar 2, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Set-up-automated-communications-in-Smart-Screening

Title

Set up automated communications in Smart Screening

Details

Overview

You decide which applicants are the best fit for your role. Automated actions help you move faster with the candidates you choose by automatically sending messages or interview invites based on criteria you set.

Important: Automated messages are sent from a no-reply email address and are designed to prompt a specific action, like scheduling an interview. They’re not intended for back-and-forth conversation with applicants.

Set up automated messages through Smart Screening

  1. In the left-hand rail, select Smart Screening.
  2. Choose the job you want to set up automated messaging for.
  3. Scroll down to Automated actions.
  4. Next to Message applicants, click Add.
  5. Choose to message all applicants or only applicants who score above a certain threshold based on your screening criteria.
  6. Select a template and customize the message that will be sent to applicants.
  7. By default, responses go to the account owner shown at the bottom of the template. To include additional team members, select their accounts under Send responses to additional accounts.
    1. Note:Only Indeed accounts with Candidate access can be selected. They will be notified about candidate responses.
  8. Click Turn on.

Set up automated invites to schedule a call

  1. In the left-hand rail, select Smart Screening.
  2. Choose the job you want to set up automated scheduling for.
  3. Scroll down to Automated actions.
  4. Next to Invite applicants to schedule a call, click Add.
  5. Choose to invite all applicants or only applicants who score above a certain threshold based on your screening criteria.
  6. Enter the URL where applicants can schedule the call.
  7. Select a template and customize the message that will accompany the scheduling link.
  8. By default, responses go to the account owner shown at the bottom of the template. To include additional team members, select their accounts under Send responses to additional accounts.
    1. Note: Only Indeed accounts with Candidate access can be selected. They will be notified about candidate responses.
  9. Click Turn on.

Note: Automated scheduling supports only one calendar link per job. If multiple recruiters need to schedule calls, use a shared scheduling tool or team calendar.

Setting Up a Sponsored Job Campaign

This article guides employers on creating and managing Sponsored Job campaigns on Indeed, including adding multiple jobs, setting shared or per-job budgets, scheduling, choosing advertising channels, and selecting campaign objectives. Users can create campaigns manually or rule-based, edit advanced settings, and track performance through analytics and reporting.

  • Feb 25, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

What-is-a-Campaign

Title

Setting Up a Sponsored Job Campaign

Details

Overview

Campaigns help you manage a group of Sponsored Jobs. You can put multiple jobs into one campaign so they all share a single budget. A campaign also lets you view analytics and reports that track performance. This article provides steps on how to create and edit a Sponsored Job campaign.

Creating a Sponsored Job Campaign

Step 1: Sign up for an employer account or log in
  1. Sign up for an employer account or sign in to your existing account.
  2. Navigate to your employer dashboard.
Step 2: Start a New Campaign
  1. Go to your campaigns page.
  2. Select the Create campaign button at the top of the page.
Step 3: Select Your Jobs

Choose the jobs you want to include. Select jobs from your list or choose Rule-based campaign so that any job that meets certain criteria will be automatically added to the campaign.

Step 4: Set a Budget and Schedule
  1. Set a budget for the entire campaign to be shared among all the included jobs. You can set a one-time budget with a fixed or target end date or a monthly recurring budget.
  2. You can set your schedule to Monthly or One-time, depending on your needs.

Note: Sponsored Job campaigns have a job limit depending on the budget you set. If you have specific budget questions, reach out to your sales or customer success representative.

Step 5: Edit Advanced Settings
Set start or end dates. Choose when to start or end the campaign.

Prorate the first month. This will prorate the budget in the first month based on how much time in the month is remaining.

Start campaign paused. Your campaign will not be active or spending until you activate it manually.
Set a spend limit per job. This allows you to set a per-job budget limit for all of the jobs in the campaign. Spending for each job will stop once the limit is reached.
  • For example, I created a campaign with 5 different jobs with a total budget of $500. With a specified limit of $100 per job, each individual job will cease spending once it reaches the $100 mark.

Note: The per-job budget limit will reset monthly on a monthly recurring campaign.

Step 6: Name Your Campaign and Choose Advertising Channels

  1. Set a campaign name. This will help you easily find and manage it later. We recommend using a job title and location along with a unique number.

    1. For example: Registered Nurses - Austin, TX 001

  2. Optional: Add any tracking tokens. If you have third-party tracking tools, you can add tokens to monitor performance.

  3. By selecting Trusted Media Network, your job will appear on our partner websites.

Step 7: Choose a Campaign Objective
  1. Select the edit icon to set your Objective.
  2. You can choose Balanced, which aims to spread the budget evenly for all jobs, or Maximum, which focuses on getting as many applications as possible for the highest-performing jobs.
Step 8: Publish Your Campaign
  1. The campaign will begin spending your budget to promote the selected jobs as soon as it’s published.

Editing a Campaign

  1. Navigate to your Campaigns page.
  2. Select the three dots next to the Campaign name and then Edit campaign.
  3. Select Edit full campaign, then you can add or remove jobs from your Campaign.

Setting up Screening Criteria

Customize Smart Screening criteria to screen candidates using job requirements, update AI-generated follow-up questions, and automate applicant evaluation. Learn to manage screening criteria, AI recruiter questions, and post-apply automations to improve candidate screening. This is only available to clients who have purchased Smart Screening.

  • Apr 17, 2026
