URL Name

Setting-up-Screening-Criteria

Title

Setting up Screening Criteria

Details

Overview

After you enroll your jobs in Smart Screening, Indeed uses your job description to help you screen candidates based on your objective criteria. This article guides you through customizing those criteria.

Set up and manage screening criteria

  1. In the left-hand rail, select Smart Screening.
  2. Choose the job you want to edit.
  3. Review the screening criteria pulled from the job description.
  4. Select Edit on the right-hand side.
  5. On the Edit page, update the criteria directly or use the AI chat to make changes.
    1. Tip: Use this section to define required and preferred criteria.
  6. Select Save.

Note: Updates won’t change your job description, but they may affect AI recruiter questions and other post-apply automations.

Managing follow-up questions

Activate follow-up questions via voice, chat, or video right after they apply. These questions are generated based on your scoring instructions, and they will be automatically updated when those instructions change. Certain questions will be skipped if evidence can be found in the resume.

To use follow-up questions:

  1. In the left-hand rail, select Smart Screening.
  2. Choose the job you want to edit.
  3. Scroll down to Enable follow-up questions by AI Recruiter.
  4. Choose how candidates should respond: text, video, or audio.
  5. If your screening criteria are up to date but you want to change the questions, click Edit.
  6. Make your changes and select Save.

Note: If you manage your jobs through an Applicant Tracking System (ATS), your ATS platform may control its own screening question settings. You’ll see these on your screening page, but you need to edit them directly in your ATS platform.

Licenses and certifications

Indicate which license or certification the role requires and allow applicants to provide additional details about their licenses and certifications.

To enable:

  1. In the left-hand rail, select Smart Screening.
  2. Choose the job you want to edit.
  3. Scroll down to Licenses and certifications.
  4. Select Add.
  5. Choose from a curated list.
  6. Select Save.

Note: Features are available for specific job types. Contact your Indeed representative to learn which verifications are available for your account.

Next Steps

Once your criteria are set up:

  • Preview the candidate experience to see how applicants will interact with your screening.
  • Set up automated communications based on screening results.
  • Configure which candidates automatically forward to your ATS.

Learn more about Smart Screening automation features.

Sharing Candidate Information Using Smart Sourcing

Indeed’s employer account has a feature called Projects. This helps you keep your candidate searches organized. You can group candidates based on categories like job title or location. If you have a Professional Smart Sourcing plan, you can collaborate in a project with your team. This lets you work together to review resumes and see what is happening with each candidate.

  • Jun 25, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Managing-matched-candidates-in-Indeed-Resume-s-Projects

Title

Sharing Candidate Information Using Smart Sourcing

Details

Overview

If you have a Professional Smart Sourcing plan, you can work with your team by collaborating in Projects. Projects help you keep candidates organized by job title or location.

Professional subscribers can invite teammates to look at resumes, activity, and status updates. This helps your team work better and makes hiring easier. Teammates who do not have a subscription can also be invited to projects for collaboration.

This article is for employers and recruiters using Indeed’s Smart Sourcing tool who want to collaborate with their team.

Who can collaborate using projects?

Professional plan users
  • Can make and collaborate within projects
  • Can invite teammates to work together
  • Can find candidates with you in the same project
Standard plan users
  • Cannot collaborate with others in projects
  • Can only find candidates on their own
  • To join a project, they must get a Professional plan or be unassigned from their Standard plan

What information is shared?

When you collaborate in a project, teammates can see:

  • Resumes
  • Outreach activity
  • Status
  • Experience and interview notes

Note: Only the person who first contacted a candidate can see their contact information. This information is only shared if the candidate replies and shows interest.

How to view your projects

Follow these steps to find and manage your projects:

  1. Sign in to your Indeed Employer account.
  2. Go to the Smart Sourcing page.
  3. Click the Projects tab.
  4. Select a project.

Inside each project, you will see two groups of candidates:

  • Matched candidates: These are suggested by Indeed based on what you do in the project. You can save their resumes or reach out to them.
  • All candidates: These are the people you have contacted. You can filter them by status, such as “Awaiting response” or “Interviewed.”

How to invite a teammate to a project

  1. Sign in to your Indeed Employer account.
  2. Click or tap Smart Sourcing on the left.
  3. Select the Projects tab.
  4. Find the project you want to collaborate in. You can either:
    1. Click the three dots next to the project and select Share project, or
    2. Open the project and click the Share Project button at the top right.
  5. Select the emails of the people you want to collaborate with.
  6. Click or tap Share.

Note: You can only collaborate with people who are already on your Indeed account. Once you share a project, you cannot unshare it.

Your teammates will get an email or notification.

    • If they have a Professional plan, they can click to start collaborating with you.
    • If they have a Standard plan, they will be asked to upgrade before they can join.

Signing In to Your Indeed Employer Account

This article explains how to sign in to Indeed using email, Google, Apple, or phone number, including options for single sign-on (SSO) and password-free sign-in. It also guides users through troubleshooting common sign-in issues, such as missing emails, forgotten passwords, and accounts created without a password.

  • Jun 16, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

209727223

Title

Signing In to Your Indeed Employer Account

Details

Overview

Sign in to an existing Indeed account using a variety of methods. The options may vary depending on your location and the credentials available on your account.

Important: Indeed is moving away from password-based sign in. All new accounts are created via passwordless sign in methods. If you sign in with a social passwordless method like Google or Apple, Indeed does not store your Google or Apple Password.

Signing in

We recommend signing in the same way that you created your account.

  1. Create an account or sign in.
  2. Choose from the available options to sign in:
    1. Continue with Google
    2. Continue with Apple
    3. Enter your email address and continue
  3. If you selected Google or Apple, follow the steps provided to sign in.
  4. If you entered an email address, Indeed will surface the sign in method most appropriate for your account:
    1. Most accounts will have a code sent to the phone number on the account.
    2. If opted out of providing a phone number, the code will be sent to the email address.
  5. Check your phone or email for a code from Indeed and enter the code to sign in.

Important: If your company uses single sign-on (SSO), you will be redirected to that page to complete sign-in.

Technical support

Issue: I forgot my password.

Solution: Click “forgot password” to follow the steps to reset your password.

You might have to answer some security questions. If you don’t know the answers, contact us. We can verify your account by phone and send you a link to reset your password.

Issue: I am not getting email codes.

Solution: There are a few actions you can take. You can:

  • Make sure you are using the right email address. You can try typing it in again to check.
  • Check your spam or junk folder.
  • Ask your IT team to allow emails from Indeed.com.
  • If you are still unable to receive your email codes, please contact us.
Issue: I am not getting phone codes.

Solution: There are a few actions you can take. You can:

  • Verify that your device is on and within cell phone coverage.
  • Review and adjust your device’s Do Not Disturb settings as needed.
  • Make sure that you’re receiving text messages from other services or senders.
  • Check your message storage. If it’s full, delete old messages.
  • Remove any third-party apps that may block or filter text messages.
  • Check your phone’s junk or spam folder.
  • Make sure your device is connected to your home carrier’s network. Messages may not arrive if you’re roaming or out of network coverage.
  • If you are still unable to receive your phone codes, please contact us.
Issue: I’m unable to sign in via password.

Solution: You’ll get this error if your Indeed account was created using a sign-in code instead of a password. Try signing in with your Apple or Google account. If that doesn’t work, contact us—we’re happy to help you get back in.

Single-Source Feed Policy (Effective March 31, 2026)

This article explains Indeed’s Single-Source Feed Policy effective March 31, 2026, which phases out free visibility for jobs sent through single-source XML feeds when an employer’s Applicant Tracking System (ATS) already integrates with Indeed Apply. It helps employers and agencies understand policy timelines, determine whether their ATS integration is affected, and outlines migration options—either switching to an ATS integration or using the Job Update API to manage jobs at scale.

  • Feb 26, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Single-Source-Feed-Policy-Effective-March-31-2026

Title

Single-Source Feed Policy (Effective March 31, 2026)

Details

Overview

Indeed is updating how jobs are sent to our platform. This will help employers hire faster and give job seekers a better experience. This policy applies to employers and agencies that send jobs through a single-source feed.

Starting March 31, 2026, jobs sent through single-source XML feeds will no longer appear for free if they can be sent through an Applicant Tracking System (ATS).

Read on to find out:

  • What’s changing, with examples
  • The different timelines for employers and ad agencies
  • Next steps for anyone currently using single-source feeds

Learn more about ATS integration and single-source feeds.

Policy details

What’s changing

Indeed is standardizing how jobs are delivered to our platform. This update helps improve job quality and make hiring easier for employers.

Benefits:

  • Reduces duplicate or outdated postings
  • Keeps job details accurate and consistent
  • Gives candidates a smoother, faster application experience
Here’s what you need to know
If your ATS is integrated with Indeed, you must use that integration to post jobs.

Contact your Indeed account manager if your jobs come from an ATS, but you are not using an integration.

Feeds from ATSs without an Indeed integration (for example, Taleo or homegrown systems) are not affected.

Example: Your ATS is not yet integrated with Indeed. This change does not apply to you right now.

Key dates

For all employers
  • Now – March 30, 2026:
    • Prepare to move to an ATS integration.
    • We no longer accept new single-source job feeds from employers who already have an ATS integration with Indeed, even if a third party or agency built the feed.
  • March 31, 2026: Jobs sent through single-source feeds are visible only when sponsored.
For agencies
  • March 31, 2026: Organic single-source feeds move to Sponsored Only visibility (if the ATS has an Indeed integration).
  • June 30, 2026: Sponsored-only single-source feeds from ad agencies will no longer be visible (if the ATS has an Indeed integration).
  • December 31, 2026:
    • Jobs from integrated ATS feeds on ad-agency multisource feeds will no longer be visible.
    • Agency single-source feeds affected by the March 31st policy will also no longer be visible.
    • After this date, ad agencies can only manage jobs on Indeed using the Job Update API for integrated ATS jobs.

Next steps

If you send jobs through a single-source feed:

  • Check your ATS. Confirm if your system integrates with Indeed using Indeed’s Integrations Hub.
  • Plan your migration early. Work with your ATS provider or ad agency to move to an ATS integration as soon as possible.
  • Make job updates at scale. Agencies can use the Job Update API to edit titles, descriptions, and campaign details.

Contact us

Contact your ATS support team or Indeed account team for help transitioning to an ATS integration.

Special Job Posting Types

This article explains Indeed’s Special Job Posting Types policy, helping employers understand how to post unique, event-based, or gig-related jobs while keeping listings clear and compliant. It outlines when non-traditional posts (like job fairs, general applications, or gig platform roles) must be sponsored, the requirements for AI training and platform-based gigs, and provides guidance for correcting or appealing removed posts to ensure each listing represents one real, defined job opportunity.

  • May 19, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Special-Job-Posting-Types

Title

Special Job Posting Types

Details

Overview

This policy applies to all employers posting jobs on Indeed. Our goal is to keep job searches simple, clear, and fair for everyone. Each job post should describe one real job so job seekers know exactly what they’re applying for.

We understand that some employers use unique or non-traditional application methods. These are fine as long as the job itself is clearly defined.

Posts that group many jobs, use general or unconventional applications, or promote unclear roles may need sponsorship to stay visible on Indeed.

Policy requirements

Tailored applications

Your job post may not need sponsorship if:

  • It matches one clear, specific job
  • It lists more than one available shift for the same role
  • You split several roles into separate job posts
  • You use plural job titles (for example, “Servers” instead of “Server”)
General recruitment and applications

Your job post may need sponsorship if:

  • It’s for a specific role, but leads to a general application
  • It promotes several different jobs in one post
Job fairs, hiring events, and career fairs

Posts that promote or advertise hiring events must be sponsored or submitted through Indeed Hiring Events (IHE).

Advertising your event
  • Applies to posts created for the event itself, like “Hiring Event: Retail Roles.
  • These rules apply to all event ads, even when the event isn’t managed through IHE.
  • The job title, description, and location must describe the event, not a single job.
Individual jobs in Indeed Hiring Events
  • Jobs that are part of an IHE (for example, “Sales Associate” at a hiring fair) must still follow all job posting rules.
  • The job title and location should describe the specific job, not the event.

Gigs & Short-Term Work Platforms

Gigs and short-term work platforms provide job seekers opportunities that can be single tasks, shifts, or ongoing projects. Workers can accept or decline tasks at any time. These opportunities are usually offered through apps or websites that connect clients and workers.

Important: All gigs and short-term work opportunities need sponsorship to appear on our platform.

Allowed

We allow real, task-based, or shift-based gig opportunities where:

  • Workers can choose which shifts or tasks to take
  • The platform guarantees payment and supports real work
  • The platform is already active in the opportunity’s location
  • Workers choose their schedule and have no obligations to take on work
  • Workers can refuse any and all work, and can quit the platform at any time

Examples include:

  • Delivery or rideshare apps that operate in your region
  • Task-based apps offering paid household or online work

Not allowed

  • Apps that haven’t launched yet or don’t have active opportunities in the posted location.
  • “Passive income” or fee-based opportunities, like pay-to-join or investment offers
  • Posts that advertise platforms only, without real work available
  • Traditional jobs for other companies or clients (Indeed classifies these as job boards, as would be subject to that policy)
  • Third-party forms like Google Forms for applications

AI training gigs specific requirements

  • The job title must include “AI Trainer” (or a similar term)
  • The salary field should show the average pay per task
  • If the post doesn’t use Indeed Apply, the link must go to the platform’s signup or task page

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Check if the post meets the requirements above.
  • Contact Indeed Support if you believe we removed your post by mistake.

Suspicious Job Postings Using Your Company’s Name: What To Do

This article helps employers identify and respond to fraudulent job postings that misuse their company name on Indeed by: reporting suspicious listings directly through Indeed’s reporting feature, directing affected job seekers to official support resources and verified Company Pages, communicating with audiences through careers page notices or social media updates, and implementing preventive best practices including verifying employer accounts, claiming Company Pages, and training recruiting teams to spot and report fake postings. Employers learn to recognize warning signs such as job seekers inquiring about unposted positions, suspicious communications mimicking company domains, and requests for money or inappropriate personal information.

  • Jun 25, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Suspicious-Job-Postings-Using-Your-Company-s-Name-What-To-Do

Title

Suspicious Job Postings Using Your Company’s Name: What To Do

Details

Overview

In some cases, job listings may appear that aren’t real job openings from the company. These situations can confuse job seekers and employers.

This can happen to businesses of any size. If you find a listing on Indeed that doesn’t match your company’s current hiring, you can report it for review.

We take these reports seriously. Protecting employers and job seekers is a priority.

Spotting suspicious listings using your company’s name

Warning signs:

  • Job seekers asking about positions your company never posted.
  • Email addresses or other communications that look like your company domain.
  • Job descriptions or requirements that don’t match your normal hiring practices.
  • Job seekers reporting requests for money or personal information.

Important: Here’s what to do if you think your Indeed account was hacked.

Reporting a suspicious job post on Indeed

How to report a job

To report a suspicious posting on Indeed:

  1. Find the posting on Indeed.
  2. Click or tap Report job at the bottom of the posting.
  3. Choose the option that best fits the issue.
  4. Explain why you believe the job is fake.
  5. Submit the report.

Tip: Learn more about reporting a job on Indeed.

What happens next
  • After you report a job, our Trust & Safety team will review it.
  • Indeed may take action as appropriate based on its policies and guidelines.

Important: Reporting a job doesn’t guarantee a specific outcome. But these reports help us address low-quality or unauthorized content.

Responding to job seekers

If job seekers contact your company about a fake posting, you can direct them to official support resources. This is optional, and at your discretion.

You can share these resources from the Job Seeker Help Center:

  • How To Report a Job
  • Reporting a Message or Phishing Attempt
  • I May Have Been Scammed, What Can I Do?

You can also direct job seekers to your official, verified Company Page. There, they can review current openings and your standard hiring process.

Communicating with your audience

If needed, you can notify job seekers and your audience about fake listings. You can:

  • Add a notice to your careers page
  • Share a general update on your company’s social media channels
  • Email recent applicants using your usual candidate communications channels

Best practices for your employer profile

You may not be able to prevent others from using your company name. But you can take steps to reduce confusion. Consider the following:

  • Verify your Indeed employer account, if you can
  • Claim and update your Company Page. This helps job seekers identify official company information and active openings. Here’s how to claim your Company Page
  • Make sure your recruiting or HR teams know how to identify and report suspicious jobs
  • Outline on your careers page how your company communicates with candidates. Explain what information or is not requested during your standard hiring process

Summary

If you believe your company name is linked to a suspicious or fake job posting, you can:

  • Report the job through Indeed
  • Keep your official employer information up to date
  • Direct job seekers to trusted sources such as your claimed Company Page on Indeed

Important: This article is for general informational purposes only. It doesn’t count as legal or professional advice. Readers should consult their own advisors for guidance.

Think You Were Hacked? Here’s What to Do

If you notice unusual activity on your Indeed account, such as password changes, unfamiliar job posts, or being locked out, your account may be compromised. This can happen through phishing, malware, or hacked email access. To stay secure, change your passwords, run a virus scan, contact Indeed Support, and use two-factor authentication and strong, unique passwords to protect your account.

  • Oct 20, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

I-Think-My-Account-May-Have-Been-Compromised

Title

Think You Were Hacked? Here’s What to Do

Details

Overview

Indeed takes your security seriously. If you see strange activity on your account, it might be compromised. This can happen if someone gets your login information through phishing scams, malware, or by accessing your email. This article helps you spot the signs of a compromised account and what to do next.

Signs your account may be compromised

Look out for these signs of unusual activity:

  • Changes to your email, password, or two-factor authentication that you didn’t make
  • Job posts that you or your team didn’t create
  • Unfamiliar charges on your bill
  • Changes to your company profile that you didn’t make
  • Messages sent from your account that you didn’t write
  • Being unexpectedly locked out of your account

Compromises often occur as a result of phishing. You can find out more about this in our article Recognizing Phishing Scams.

What to do

If you think your account is compromised, you should:

Change your Indeed password
  • Change the password for the email linked to your Indeed account. It may have been hacked, too.
  • If you clicked a strange link, run a virus scan on your computer.
Contact us and explain the problem
  • You may also want to contact your local law enforcement to report what happened.

What happens next

Once you contact us, we will review the issue. We’ll help you get back into your account and set up new security measures. We can also give you tips to help keep your account safe in the future.

Keeping your account protected

To help keep your account secure, you should:

  • Use two-factor authentication.
  • Create a strong, unique password for your Indeed and email accounts.
  • Never click on strange links from people you don’t know.

Visit our Security page to learn more about keeping your account safe.

Third-Party Recruiters and Aggregators

Learn how to meet Indeed’s third-party job feed requirements, prevent duplicates, and ensure working apply links. Guidance supports staffing firms, ad agencies, and programmatic feeds to maintain visibility and compliant job content.

  • Mar 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

360018069792

Title

Third-Party Recruiters and Aggregators

Details

Overview

Indeed works with third-party partners to help connect job seekers with jobs. These partners may include staffing firms, ad agencies, and programmatic providers. Each type follows rules to make sure job posts are clear, correct, and easy to apply to.

Approved third parties

Ad agencies and programmatic providers

Ad agencies and programmatic partners can post and sponsor jobs for clients. They help employers manage job ads and get more applicants.

Note: Before setting up a feed, the agency must have an Agency of Record (AOR) from the client.

Your feed must:
  • Only include jobs from one company or its subsidiaries
  • Have job titles and locations that match the real job
  • Send job seekers to the relevant end client’s apply page
  • Include unique and correct job listings
  • Be the only visible version (if prioritized by the end client), or the only visible duplicate, of the client’s jobs on Indeed
  • Include the client’s external reference/requisition number
  • Be sent via secure sFTP (for programmatic feeds)
Your feed must not:
  • Include jobs from job board clients
  • Use title or location expansion
  • Send two identical files or feeds for the same client
Staffing, recruiting, and placement firms

Staffing, recruitment, and placement companies may post jobs if they sponsor their listings. This rule applies in most markets, except Japan and Puerto Rico.

Indeed gives priority to jobs posted directly by employers.

Recruitment Process Outsourcing (RPO) providers may be reviewed individually to determine their relationship with the end client and if the content provided is unique.

Prohibited sources

Aggregators

Aggregators are not allowed on Indeed. Aggregators collect jobs from other websites instead of posting original content. These jobs are often duplicates, missing information, or outdated. They cannot appeal for visibility.

Special cases

Distributors

Distributor content is allowed on Indeed with Sponsored Only visibility if it meets quality standards.

Distributor content should:

  • Come from one XML or Job Sync API feed only
  • Not include any job board content

Integration and quality standards

All third-party partners should follow these best practices:

  • Keep job details correct and up-to-date
  • Avoid duplicate or outdated posts
  • Make sure all apply links work
  • Use secure XML or Job Sync feeds
  • Contact Indeed Support if you need technical assistance

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Make sure your partner is approved
  • Check that the apply link works and the job info is correct
  • Review your feed for errors

Contact Indeed Support if you believe we removed your post by mistake.

Troubleshooting 2FA Issues

This guide helps Indeed users troubleshoot common two-factor authentication (2FA) issues, including inaccessible phone numbers, switching sign-in methods, repeated verification prompts, and missing verification codes. It provides multiple solutions, such as updating phone numbers, using an authenticator app, adjusting browser and device settings, and checking message delivery or Do Not Disturb settings.

  • Mar 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Troubleshooting-2FA-Issues

Title

Troubleshooting 2FA Issues

Details

Overview

Use this guide to find solutions to common problems with two-factor authentication (2FA).

Issue: I can’t access the phone number linked to my account.

Solution: Contact us if your phone number is no longer active or the person who set up the account no longer works at your company. We’ll help remove the old number and add a new one to your account.

Important: You won’t be able to sign in again until you provide a new phone number.

Issue: I want to use a different sign in method.

Solution: For security reasons, it’s not possible to switch back to email-based 2FA once text message-based 2FA is enabled. However, you can switch to an authenticator app from your Security settings.

Issue: I’m being asked to verify every time I sign in.

Typically, 2FA is only required every 90 to 365 days. You may receive the verification prompts more often if you:

  • Sign in on different devices or browsers
  • Switch Wi-Fi networks or use a VPN
  • Use a browser or software that blocks cookies or clears them automatically

Solution:

  1. Use the same device and browser when signing in.
  2. Allow cookies in your browser settings.
  3. Avoid private or incognito mode.

Issue: I didn’t receive the verification code.

Solution:

  1. Change your Do Not Disturb setting.
    1. Unblock any phone numbers from employers in your device’s Do Not Disturb setting.
    2. Ensure your Do Not Disturb setting is also set to Allow Notifications From.
  2. Verify that your device is on and within cell phone coverage.
    1. If the device’s signal is weak, turn it off, wait 30 seconds, and then turn it back on.
  3. Confirm that you are receiving text messages from other services or senders.
  4. Check your phone’s message memory.
  5. Ensure you don’t have a third-party app installed that might block, filter, or interfere with text message delivery.
  6. Ensure the messages are not delivered to your mobile phone’s junk or spam folder.
  7. Check that the device is connected to your home carrier’s network. Messages may not be delivered on devices roaming internationally or off-network.

This article helps you troubleshoot connection issues with Indeed’s interview platform by providing solutions for common errors. Fix a non-working camera or microphone by checking browser permissions (Chrome, Safari, Edge, or Firefox); resolve browser compatibility issues by using a supported device; or fix network connection problems by running a network test and updating your IT team’s network allowlist.

URL Name

Troubleshooting-device-issues-with-the-Video-Interview-Platform

Title

Troubleshooting Issues With Indeed’s Interview Platform

Details

Overview

We’re sorry you’re having trouble connecting to Indeed’s interview platform. These problems are often caused by network settings, camera or microphone access, or browser compatibility. This article will show you how to fix each one.

Tip: Test your camera and microphone before the interview to make sure your permissions are set correctly.

Issue: Your camera and microphone are not working.

Solution: Make sure your browser has permission to use your camera and microphone.

Select Allow when prompted. If that didn’t work, visit the Interview Test page and follow the instructions below:

Chrome

  1. Click or tap the red camera icon in the address bar.
  2. Ensure camera and microphone permissions are allowed.

Safari

  1. Click the red camera icon in the address bar.
  2. Set the camera and microphone to Always Allowed.

Edge

  1. Click the lock icon in the address bar.
  2. Set the camera and microphone to Allow.

Firefox

  1. On the test screen, click the lock or camera icon to ensure permissions are allowed.

Issue: Your browser isn’t working properly.

Solution: Use a supported browser.

Supported devices and browsers:

  • Desktop or laptop: Chrome, Firefox, Edge, Safari
  • iPad or iPhone: Safari
  • Android: Chrome, Firefox

Incompatible devices and browsers:

  • Internet Explorer
  • Chrome on iOS

Note: Using other apps during the interview may turn off the microphone if you’re using a phone or tablet. If the interviewer can’t hear you, restart your device and re-enter the interview.

Issue: You’re unable to connect to the interview platform.

Solution: Run a network test and check your connection settings.

If your camera and microphone work but you still can’t connect, try this:

  1. Run a network connection test. This checks if your network supports Indeed’s video technology.
  2. If the test fails, share this article with your IT team. They can use the information below to update your network settings.

For IT and network teams

Use this information to configure your network for Indeed Interview and our video vendor, Daily.

Step 1: Update your network’s allowlist.

Add these domains, protocols, and port ranges to your approved list (allowlist):

*.daily.co

  • Protocol: TCP
  • Port: 443

*.wss.daily.co

  • Protocols: TCP, UDP
  • Ports: 443, 40000-49999 (TCP), 23000-26999, 40000-49999 (UDP)

*-wss.daily.co

  • Protocols: TCP, UDP
  • Ports: 443, 40000-49999 (TCP), 23000-26999, 40000-49999 (UDP)

*.dailywebrtc.com

  • Protocol: TCP
  • Port: 443

*.wss.dailywebrtc.com

  • Protocols: TCP, UDP
  • Ports: 443, 40000-49999 (TCP), 23000-26999, 40000-49999 (UDP)

*-wss.dailywebrtc.com

  • Protocols: TCP, UDP
  • Ports: 443, 40000-49999 (TCP), 23000-26999, 40000-49999 (UDP)

*.dailywebrtc.net

  • Protocol: TCP
  • Port: 443

*.wss.dailywebrtc.net

  • Protocols: TCP, UDP
  • Ports: 443, 40000-49999 (TCP), 23000-26999, 40000-49999 (UDP)

*-wss.dailywebrtc.net

  • Protocols: TCP, UDP
  • Ports: 443, 40000-49999 (TCP), 23000-26999, 40000-49999 (UDP)

prod-ks.pluot.blue

  • Protocol: TCP

  • Protocols: TCP, UDP
  • Ports: 443, 3478, 5349

  • Protocols: TCP, UDP
  • Ports: 443, 3478, 5349
Step 2: Rerun the network test.
  1. Rerun the network test to confirm everything works.
  2. If issues remain, download the test results and contact your Indeed representative for help.

U.S. Military Policies

This article explains Indeed’s U.S. Military Policies, helping employers understand posting requirements for military jobs: civilian positions must clearly state they don’t require military service; medical, officer, and chaplain roles must include service requirements and military branch in the job title and description; general enlistment roles must state military service is required in the title, include the branch, explain enlistment rules, and specify minimum active duty or reserve years. If a military job post is removed or hidden, employers can review and update their post to meet policy guidelines, sponsor posts that require enlistment or commission, or contact Indeed Support if they believe the removal was a mistake.

  • Dec 2, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

New-Policies-Governing-Military-Roles-on-Indeed

Title

U.S. Military Policies

Details

Overview

Since January 31, 2022, Indeed requires more clarity for U.S. military job descriptions. This helps job seekers understand military service needs. The military can’t use Smart Sourcing or other tools to promote roles that need enlistment or are not for a specific position.

Jobs that need military enlistment or commission must be sponsored. Jobs that don’t need enlistment or commission can be posted for free. All military jobs must follow this policy to appear on Indeed.

Important: This policy is currently enforced only in the US.

Policies for military roles

Civilian positions

Does the job need military service? If not, state in the description that it is a civilian position.

Medical, officer, and chaplain roles

These job posts must include:

  • Military service requirements in the job title and description
  • The military branch in the job title or company name
General enlistment roles

For roles that need military service:

  • The job title must:
    • State that military service is required
    • List the military branch in the title or company name.
  • The job description must:
    • Explain enlistment rules and requirements
    • State the minimum years of active duty or reserve enlistment required
    • Focus on the general nature of enlisting. Don’t target a specific job that someone could get by enlisting

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Review your post considering the guidelines above.
  • Sponsor your post if it requires military enlistment or commission.

Contact Indeed Support if you believe we removed your post by mistake.

Unavailable Jobs on Indeed

Learn how to ensure your job posts appear on Indeed by fixing broken URLs, confirming job availability, avoiding expired or internal-only roles, and using approved application methods. Keywords: job visibility, broken links, expired jobs, internal hiring, application process.

  • Mar 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Unavailable-Jobs-on-Indeed

Title

Unavailable Jobs on Indeed

Details

Overview

Indeed connects people with jobs that are open, accessible, and ready to apply. We may remove, hide, or suspend job posts that are out of date, missing key details, or lead to poor application experiences.

Below are the main types of unavailable jobs and the rules for each.

Policy requirements

URLs
  • Links directing job seekers to a third-party site must work.
  • We may remove jobs with broken URLs from search results until the link is fixed.
Examples of broken URLs:
  • Connection errors or blank pages
  • Security or access errors
  • Site maintenance or service unavailable
    • “This page isn’t working,” “503 Service Unavailable,” or “The site is undergoing maintenance.”
Job availability
  • Jobs linked to businesses or locations that are not yet open (“coming soon”) must show a clear start or hiring date.
  • Jobs submitted more than 60 days before the start date may have reduced visibility.
Examples:
  • Jobs posted more than 60 days before a business opens
  • Jobs posted more than 60 days before the role is available
  • Jobs advertised as “opening soon” without a clear start date
Expired jobs
  • Jobs that are no longer open must be removed or shown with reduced visibility.
Examples:
  • The application or start date has passed
  • The employer has shared that they are no longer hiring
  • The role is generally no longer available
Internal-only roles
  • Jobs open only to current employees or members of a specific program may not appear on Indeed.
Examples:
  • Work-study programs
  • University research positions for enrolled students only. Examples are:
    • Graduate assistantships
    • Research positions
    • Work-study jobs that require the applicant to be currently enrolled at the institution
  • Roles posted as part of an internal mobility program
  • Jobs stating “This position is only open to current employees”
  • Jobs requiring a referral from a current employee
  • Department- or program-specific restrictions
    • For example, “Only members of the XYZ Leadership Program may apply”
Application methods
  • Jobs must use approved application methods.
    • For example, vetted ATS, or other integrated apply tools
  • Posts asking job seekers to apply in other ways may have reduced visibility.
  • Including an email or phone number in the description is allowed.

Next steps

If we removed or hid your job post:

  • Compare your post against the guidelines above.
  • Test and repair broken links before posting.
  • Check for expired dates and start dates.
  • Make sure the role is open to all qualified candidates.

Contact Indeed Support if you believe we removed your post by mistake.

Understanding Job Details and Profile Insights

Learn how to use job details and profile insights on Indeed to show job seekers if they’re a strong fit, increase engagement, and attract qualified candidates by highlighting skills, experience, pay, job type, schedule, location, and other preferences; you can add these details by writing a clear job description or editing an existing posting, including via ATS or career site integration.

  • Nov 27, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Adding-Job-Preferences-Qualifications

Title

Understanding Job Details and Profile Insights

Details

Overview

Job details and profile insights help job seekers understand if your role is a good match. They appear in search results and on your job post as tags. Tags highlighted in green show a strong match to the job seeker’s experience or preferences. Adding job details and insights can help increase engagement and applications.

Purpose

Job details give job seekers the information they need to decide if they should click on your job and apply. Profile insights show job seekers whether they’re a good fit for the role. Describe what the role offers and what it requires to better match with candidates. Job details and insights also help Indeed show your job to job seekers whose profiles fit your needs.

Benefits

  • Increase engagement, job visibility, and applications
  • Attract the right candidates
  • Help job seekers understand job requirements and expectations
  • Highlight matching details to show job seekers when they are a strong fit

Features

Profile insights

These insights show what you need from candidates. They can include:

  • Skills
  • Education
  • Certifications
  • Licenses
  • Languages
  • Years of experience
Job details

These details show if your job matches a job seeker’s preferences. They can include:

  • Pay
  • Job type (full-time, part-time, etc.)
  • Shift and schedule
  • Remote or on-site work
  • Commute or location details

Adding job details and profile insights

How to add details and insights to your job

Indeed extracts details and insights from your job description. Make sure your job includes relevant tags by:

  • Writing a good job description when creating your job
  • Editing your job to add more details if needed

Did you know? If you’re using an ATS or your own career site, you can add details directly to the job description. We’ll extract and display them on Indeed.

Best practices
Be specific.

Clear and detailed job descriptions are more accurately extracted.

Keep formatting simple.

Avoid extra symbols, spaces, or long-form numbers.

Describe what is offered.

Phrasing details as “not offered” may reduce extraction accuracy.

Updating a Candidate’s Application Status

This guide explains how to update a candidate’s application status and manage your hiring workflow. It covers filtering, sorting, and shortlisting candidates to help you quickly find the best matches. Use these tools to stay organized and make faster hiring decisions.

  • May 4, 2026

URL Name

How-to-update-a-candidate-s-application-status

Title

Updating a Candidate’s Application Status

Details

Overview

This feature lets you change the status of a candidate’s job application. It helps you keep track of who you want to move forward with and who is not a good fit. You can also sort and filter candidates in the Candidates tab to quickly find the most important profiles.

How to update a candidate’s application status

  1. Sign in to your account and navigate to the Candidates tab.
  2. By default, All open and paused jobs will open, which shows all job seekers who applied to your jobs.
    1. The Matched Candidates section shows people who have not applied yet but may be a good fit. You can invite them to apply. Learn more about Smart Sourcing.
  3. Use the All open and paused jobs drop-down menu to search and filter candidates.
  4. Use the filters below the drop-down to sort by candidate status, application date, or location.
    1. Interest (or shortlist) means you saved a resume because you want to look at it again.
  5. Use the search or magnifying glass icon in the upper right corner to search for candidates by name.
  6. Check the boxes next to the chosen candidate’s name, and the Change status option becomes available.
Candidate status options
  • Reviewed: You looked at this candidate’s resume. You can add notes or send them a message.
  • Contacting: You are in contact with this candidate or setting up interviews.
  • Rejected: You are not moving forward with this candidate, and they will be notified. Learn more about rejecting candidates.
  • Hired: You chose this candidate for the job. Marking them as hired helps you understand hiring costs.

Adding candidates to your shortlist

What it is

A shortlist is a group of top candidates you want to learn more about. These are people who might be a good match for your job.

How it works
  1. Sign in to your account and navigate to the Candidates tab.
  2. Filter candidates by using the search and filter options.
  3. Check the boxes next to the chosen candidate’s name.
  4. Click the checkmark under the “Interested?” column to add them to your shortlist.

Next steps

Contact Indeed support if you have any trouble updating your candidate’s status.

Using Apply URL

The article explains how employers can post a job on Indeed while directing applicants to apply on their own website using the Apply URL feature. It covers eligibility and link requirements, when and why to use Apply URL, how to request activation from Indeed, and how to add the external application link when creating or editing a job post.

  • Jul 6, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

360003389972

Title

Using Apply URL

Details

Overview

With the Apply URL feature, you can post a job directly on Indeed while keeping the application process on your own website. This option is useful if your job isn’t easily indexed but requires a specific external or pre-existing application process. In this article, we’ll explain the Apply URL requirements and how to use this feature.

Don’t want your job post directly on Indeed?Check out how to post a job from your career site or ATS instead.

Apply URL guidelines

Employer eligibility
  • You have an employer account on Indeed.
  • The job and application meet Indeed’s policies.
Link requirements
  • The URL must directly lead to:
    • An application process, OR
    • A job description page that includes a direct application link.
  • The URL can lead to a log-in or sign-up page, but must immediately redirect the job seeker to the application or job description page afterward.
Business justification
  • The role isn’t easily indexable, demonstrating a clear business need for the Apply URL feature.
Tracking and sponsorship
  • Tracking must be installed to identify traffic from Indeed.
  • Generic or non-specific applications may require sponsorship.

Requesting Apply URL

Please contact us if you think you are a good candidate for Apply URL. An Indeed representative will enable the feature for you.

Adding an Apply URL to your job

  1. Follow the steps in posting a job on Indeed or editing your job post.
  2. Scroll to Application method.
  3. Turn off Receive applications on Indeed.
  4. Paste your application link in the website URL field that appears.
  5. Save or post your job.

Using Premium Sponsored Jobs

This article helps employers understand how to use Premium Sponsored Jobs to increase job visibility and hire faster by using automatic features like candidate and location targeting, as well as optional features such as matched candidates, urgently hiring labels, branded jobs, and Tech Network. It also explains how to set up a Premium Sponsored Job, review and manage matched candidates, and turn optional features on or off.

  • Jul 9, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

Using-Premium-Sponsored-Jobs

Title

Using Premium Sponsored Jobs

Details

Overview

Use Premium Sponsored Jobs to get more visibility and hire faster. This article explains the automatic features that are always included and the optional features you can choose to turn on. It also shows how to set up a Premium Sponsored Job and use the features to reach the right candidates.

Premium Sponsored Jobs features

Automatic features
  • Advanced matching
    • Improves quality by placing your job in front of relevant job seekers more often.
  • First page boost
    • Shows your job higher in search results, so it gets more visibility.
  • Location targeting
    • Shows your job to people who live or work within the area you choose.
Optional features
  • Matched candidates
    • Lets you invite candidates whose qualifications match your job criteria.
  • Candidate highlights
    • These automatic messages can help you review and contact job seekers faster.
  • ‘Urgently hiring’ label
    • Shows you’re already actively reviewing candidates.
  • Branded jobs
    • Let you stand out by adding your logo and a company image to your job post.
  • Tech Network
    • Targets existing audiences on specialized industry sites to reach skilled candidates.
  • Multi-location reach
    • Lets you advertise your job in more than one location for a wider reach.

How to use Premium Sponsored Jobs

Getting started
  1. Go to your Jobs page.
  2. Find a job and select Sponsor.
  3. Select Premium.
  4. Click or tap Save and continue.
Viewing your matched candidates
  1. Open a Premium Sponsored Job from the list of jobs on your Jobs page.
  2. In the Candidates column, select Matches.
  3. From this page, you can:
    1. Invite matched candidates to apply for the job
    2. Select Not a match to remove a job seeker from being a matched candidate for this job

Important: You can’t undo Not a match, so make sure to review candidates before selecting this option.

Turning on the ‘urgently hiring’ label
  1. Open a Premium Sponsored Job from the list of jobs on your Jobs page.
  2. Click or tap the three dots on the right side of the screen.
  3. Select More Actions.
  4. Find the Premium Sponsored Job box.
  5. Turn on the Urgently hiring label.

Troubleshooting

Issue: I can’t invite matched candidates using my Premium Sponsored Job.

Solution: There are a few reasons why you may be having this issue:

  • You reached your daily invite limit (30 invites per day)
  • The job is not eligible for matched candidate invites
  • You currently have account or billing-related issues

Using Two-Factor Authentication (2FA) on Indeed

This article explains how two-factor authentication (2FA) secures Indeed employer accounts by requiring a one-time verification code via text message or authenticator app. Users will learn how to set up 2FA, update their phone number, and understand why 2FA protects accounts from unauthorized access.

  • Mar 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

What-is-two-factor-authentication

Title

Using Two-Factor Authentication (2FA) on Indeed

Details

Overview

Two-factor authentication (2FA) keeps your employer account secure. Also called two-step verification, it works by asking you to confirm your identity with a one-time code when you sign in. You’ll usually receive this code as a text message (SMS) to your mobile phone. If you have a verified phone number, you are automatically signed up for text message-based 2FA.

Why do we use 2FA?

2FA helps protect your account by:

  • Preventing unauthorized access, even if someone gets your password
  • Ensuring that only verified individuals can access sensitive account or hiring information

Typically, 2FA is required to sign in once every 90-365 days, unless you’re:

  • Using different devices or networks
  • Using a VPN
  • Clearing your cookies or cache frequently

Setting up 2FA

Text messages
  1. Sign in to your employer account.
  2. Navigate to your Security settings.
  3. Select Add 2FA under Account protection.
  4. Find the Text messages option and select Add.
  5. Enter your phone number and select Send Code.
  6. Enter the code that you receive on your phone and select Submit.
Authenticator app
  1. Sign in to your employer account.
  2. Navigate to your Security settings.
  3. Select Add 2FA under Account protection.
  4. Find the Authenticator App option and select Add.
  5. Follow the instructions shown on screen.
  6. Enter the verification code provided by your authenticator app.
  7. Select Confirm.

Updating your 2FA phone number

  1. Sign in to your employer account.
  2. Navigate to your Security settings.
  3. Select Manage 2FA Settings.
  4. Select Change phone number.
  5. Enter your new phone number and Send Code.
  6. Type in the code you receive on your phone and Submit.

Tip: Having trouble with 2FA? Read our Troubleshooting 2FA Issues article.

Viewing Hiring Event Performance

The Hiring Event Performance feature lets employers review event results, including RSVPs, attendance, interviews, and event costs. You can track key metrics like show rate, quality interviews, and average cost per RSVP, interview, and offer to see how your event performed. Comparing your results to industry averages helps you understand effectiveness and plan better for future hiring events.

  • Dec 8, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

How-to-view-event-performance

Title

Viewing Hiring Event Performance

Details

Overview

The Hiring Event Performance feature helps you check how your event went. It highlights details like RSVPs, interviews, attendance, and event costs.

These results show what worked well. They include the number of candidates who joined and the cost of each interview or offer. Reviewing this data can help you plan better for future events.

Key metrics and what they mean

When does the data become available?
  • Your event data becomes available one day after the event ends.
  • If you added candidates by hand, their details may take up to 24 hours to show up.
What information is included?

You can track your results using these key metrics:

  • Total RSVPs: Number of candidates who scheduled an interview
  • Show rate: Percentage of RSVPed candidates who attended
  • Total interviews: Number of candidates interviewed
  • Quality interviews: Candidates marked as “Yes” after the interview
    • For virtual events, this means strong candidates who performed well.
    • For in-person events, this data comes from the candidate management tool.
  • Time to interview: How long it took from creating the event to the first interview
Cost metrics

You can also track how your budget was used:

  • Budget spent: Total money spent on the event
  • Average cost per RSVP: Average cost for each candidate who RSVPed
  • Average cost per interview: The average cost for each interview completed.
  • Average cost per offer: The average cost for each offer made, based on the budget and offers entered on Indeed.
Comparing results
  • Compare your event’s results to industry averages.
  • This helps you understand how your event performed compared to others.

Important: To keep your data accurate, record all event details and offers in Indeed.

Reviewing your Hiring Event results

Step 1: Access the Hiring Event dashboard
  1. Go to the Interviews tab in the left-hand menu.
  2. Choose Hiring Events.
  3. Find your event and select the graph icon to open performance details.
  4. Select Download in the upper right corner to share your event’s performance in PDF format.
Step 2: Check your event results
  • Review RSVPs, attendance, and interviews.
  • Check your cost metrics to see how your budget was used.
  • Compare your results to the industry average for similar events.
Step 3: Verify data accuracy
  • Record all candidate information and offers on Indeed.
  • Allow up to 24 hours for manually added candidates to appear.

What Are Started RSVPs?

Started RSVPs measure when a job seeker begins the interview scheduling process for an Indeed Hiring Event. PPSRSVP pricing replaces pay-per-click by charging only for actions that indicate real interview intent.

  • Jan 26, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

What-Are-Started-RSVPs-EN

Title

What Are Started RSVPs?

Details

Overview

A Started RSVP happens when a job seeker clicks “Schedule an interview” and begins the sign‑up steps for your online or in-person hiring event. With Indeed Hiring Events, this is what you pay for under pay‑per‑started‑RSVP (PPSRSVP).

In the past, with the old PPC (pay‑per‑click) model, costs were based on clicks, not on starting the interview sign‑up.

Purpose

Indeed is moving from paying for clicks to paying for results. This helps you spend money on people who are more likely to interview. For Sponsored Jobs, this is called pay‑per‑started‑application (PPSA). For Hiring Events, it is PPSRSVP. You pay when a job seeker starts to schedule an interview.

Important: Availability and features can vary by country. If you are not sure what applies to you, please check with your Indeed rep.

How Started RSVPs Work

You pay when a job seeker starts the interview sign‑up (a Started RSVP). You can see counts and costs in your reports (Campaigns and Analytics), including Started RSVP and Average cost per Started RSVP.

Getting Started With Started RSVP Pricing
  • No extra setup. If your event qualifies, it will use PPSRSVP automatically. You cannot switch that event back to PPC.
  • Budgets, setup, and invoices. You will keep using your current Sponsored Jobs payment method. Reports show your Started RSVP counts and costs on your usual dashboards and in your invoice details.
How to View Started RSVPs in Your Dashboard
Campaigns
  • Open the Campaigns tab.
  • Make sure the columns Started RSVP and Average cost per Started RSVP are visible. If not, use the column picker to add them.
Analytics
  • Open Analytics, then choose Interviews and events.
  • If you do not find Started RSVP, click the + icon to add it as a column.
  • Learn more in our article Measuring and Analyzing Job Performance.
Detailed billing report
  • Click your profile name (top right), then go to Billing and Invoices.
  • Next to the invoice, download the Itemized report to find line items like Started RSVPs.

What is a Responsive Employer Badge?

The Responsive Employer Badge highlights employers who respond quickly to job applications on Indeed. Employers earn the badge by keeping an application response rate above 51% over a 30-day period. This badge helps job seekers find employers with strong hiring communication and a responsive recruitment process.

  • Dec 18, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

What-is-an-Employer-Response-Badge

Title

What is a Responsive Employer Badge?

Details

Overview

A Responsive Employer Badge is a small icon that shows you are a company that answers job applications quickly. Job seekers can use this information to decide which jobs to apply for. This article explains how to get the Responsive Employer Badge.

How to get the Responsive Employer Badge

To get this badge, your company needs an Employer Response Rate higher than 51%.

The Employer Response Rate is a number that changes every week. It looks at how many job applications you answered in the last 30 days.

Hosted jobs
  • The response rate tracks when you:
  • Email a candidate about their application
  • Reply to a candidate on the Indeed platform
  • Message a candidate through your Indeed email
  • Send an automatic email to candidates who were not selected
  • Call a candidate privately through Indeed
  • Send an automatic rejection email at any time, even when closing a job
Indexed Indeed Apply jobs

The response rate tracks when you:

  • Use an Applicant Tracking System (ATS) with Disposition Sync
  • Update the status of more than 50% of your Indeed applications in the ATS
  • Have an Indeed account with a claimed source. Learn more about ATS integration.

What Is the Trusted Media Network (TMN)?

Learn what the Trusted Media Network (TMN) is, how it expands the reach of Sponsored Job campaigns through trusted partner sites, and how to turn it on when creating a new campaign. This article also explains how TMN optimizes budget automatically, keeps a consistent application experience, and shows how to measure performance across all channels.

  • Mar 3, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

What-is-the-Trusted-Media-Network-TMN

Title

What Is the Trusted Media Network (TMN)?

Details

Overview

The Trusted Media Network (TMN) helps more people find your jobs. It shares your sponsored jobs with trusted partner sites. When you turn it on, Indeed sends your jobs to the best partners for each role. This helps you reach more job seekers with no extra work.

Purpose

TMN helps your Sponsored Job campaigns reach more people. It shows your jobs to a wider group of job seekers, including those with special skills. The process is automatic. You don’t need to pick partner sites or manage ads. All candidates still apply through Indeed or your company career site.

Benefits

  • Reach more job seekers through trusted partner sites.
  • Get more candidates for hard-to-fill or specialist roles.
  • Improve campaign results without extra setup.

Features

Automatic partner distribution
  • Jobs are shared with partner sites based on job details, like location and role type.
  • You don’t need to choose or manage partners.
Consistent application experience
  • Job seekers who click a job on a partner site are redirected to Indeed.
  • All candidates use the same application process.
  • This includes Indeed Apply or your career site.
Budget optimization
  • Indeed automatically manages spend across Indeed and TMN.
  • TMN works with all campaign goals.
  • You can improve results without extra steps.

Actions

Turn on the Trusted Media Network
  1. Set up a new Sponsored Job campaign.
  2. Go to Ad placements under Select ads placement.
  3. Select Edit (Advanced).
  4. Choose Trusted Media Network and save.

Once saved, the campaign will use TMN to bring in extra traffic from Indeed’s partner sites.

Important: You can’t change the TMN settings after a campaign starts. To make changes, create a new campaign.

Measure success
  • Check performance in your existing Sponsored Job reports.
  • Results show how your campaign performs across all channels.

Learn more:

  • Measuring job performance with Indeed Analytics
  • Indeed analytics for jobs and campaigns

FAQs

Question: I already advertise jobs with a media partner. Will turning on TMN affect this?

Answer: No. If Indeed sends a job to a partner you already work with, the Indeed version will not appear. Traffic will still go through your direct partnership.

Contact your Client Success Specialist to learn which partners are in the network or how TMN affects your campaigns.

What is the Work Wellbeing Score?

Learn what the Indeed Work Wellbeing Score measures, how it’s calculated from employee survey responses (happiness, stress, job satisfaction, and sense of purpose), when it appears on Company Pages, and how employers can improve their rating by encouraging participation and taking action on results.

  • Feb 24, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

What-is-the-Work-Wellbeing-Score

Title

What is the Work Wellbeing Score?

Details

Overview

In 2020, we shared our ESG goals, which include improving wellbeing at work. The Work Wellbeing Score shows how people feel about their jobs and why. It is the largest study in the world focused on wellbeing at work. Experts helped design it.

The score looks at four main parts of work wellbeing:

  • Happiness
  • Stress
  • Job satisfaction
  • Sense of purpose

Indeed collects this information to help people find better jobs. It also helps companies create workplaces where employees can do their best.

Job seekers can use this data to compare companies. Employers can use it to see how they compare to others and better understand how their employees feel.

How the Work Wellbeing Score is calculated

When someone leaves a company review on Indeed, they are asked to complete a work wellbeing survey.

The survey includes four main questions:

  • Happiness: I feel happy at work most of the time.
  • Purpose: My work has a clear purpose.
  • Satisfaction: Overall, I am satisfied with my job.
  • Stress: I feel stressed at work most of the time.

The survey also asks extra questions to understand why people feel the way they do at work. These include:

  • Achievement: I am achieving most of my goals at work.
  • Appreciation: There are people at work who appreciate me as a person.
  • Belonging: I feel a sense of belonging in my company.
  • Compensation: I am paid fairly for my work.
  • Energy: In most of my work tasks, I feel energized.
  • Flexibility: My work has the time and location flexibility I need.
  • Inclusion: My work environment feels inclusive and respectful of all people.
  • Learning: I often learn something at work.
  • Management: My manager helps me succeed.
  • Support: There are people at work who give me support and encouragement.
  • Trust: I can trust people in my company.

People answer each question on a scale from 1 to 5:

  • 1 = Strongly disagree
  • 5 = Strongly agree

Each answer is given a score between 40 and 100. For example:

  • Strongly agree = 100 points
  • Strongly disagree = 40 points

When and where the Work Wellbeing Score appears

A company’s Work Wellbeing Score appears on its Indeed Company Page after at least 10 people have answered the four main questions (happiness, purpose, satisfaction, and stress).

Companies are then labeled based on how they compare to others:

  • Low
  • Below Average
  • Average
  • Above Average
  • High

About 20% of companies fall into each group. These rankings are updated every year and adjusted by country to keep results fair.

Ways to improve a company’s Work Wellbeing Score

The best way to improve the score is to take action. Companies can:

  • Encourage employees to complete the survey
  • Track results over time
  • Find areas that need improvement
  • Highlight strengths to attract talent

Development of the Work Wellbeing methodology

The Work Wellbeing method was created with the guidance of:

  • Dr. Sonja Lyubomirsky, Distinguished Professor of Psychology and Vice Chair, University of California, Riverside
  • Prof. Jan-Emmanuel de Neve, Professor of Economics and Behavioral Science, University of Oxford

The methodology is based on existing standards in social science for measuring general wellbeing (incorporating evaluative, affective, and eudaimonic dimensions of wellbeing) and is applied by institutions such as the OECD, UK Office of National Statistics, and the Oxford Wellbeing Research Centre.

When and Why You Get Invoices from Indeed

Indeed invoices explained: see how billing cycles, billing thresholds, and multiple invoices work for sponsored jobs.

  • Mar 23, 2026
  • Knowledge

URL Name

203366460

Title

When and Why You Get Invoices from Indeed

Details

Overview

This page covers how billing thresholds and billing cycles work, when Indeed sends invoices, and why you may receive multiple invoices for sponsored jobs.

How Indeed’s billing cycles work

  1. Start of the month: Your Sponsored Job goes live.
  2. End of the month: We check how many days your job has been running from the 1st of the month until the last day.
  3. Invoice generation: An invoice is created for the cost of that usage and sent to you.
  4. Next month: If your job is still active with a budget, we will repeat the process.
  5. End of the next month: A new invoice is generated for the time the job ran during this new billing cycle.

When will you receive an invoice?

You’ll receive a separate invoice for each of Indeed’s hiring solutions. These invoices appear on your Billing and Invoices page.

If you sponsor a job on Indeed, charges can occur in one or both of the following situations:

  • On the first day of the new month
  • When you reach your billing threshold (the maximum amount you can spend before your payment method is automatically charged).
How to check your billing threshold
  1. Click your account login in the top right corner.
  2. Select Billing and Invoices from the drop-down menu.
  3. Find the Payment method box on the right-hand side. Your billing threshold is listed there.

If your charges don’t reach the threshold during a billing cycle, you’ll be invoiced on the first day of the next month.

Why might you receive multiple invoices?

You may receive multiple invoices if:

  • You use multiple hiring solutions on Indeed.
  • You reach your billing threshold before the first of the next month.
Example scenarios
  • Threshold = $500
    • If you spend $650 in June → $500 is charged immediately, and $150 is invoiced on July 1.
    • If you spend $1200 in June → $500 is charged twice (as you hit the threshold twice), and $200 is invoiced on July 1.
    • If you spend $450 in June → the $450 is invoiced on July 1.

FAQs

Question: If I close my job post, will I get another invoice?

Answer: Yes, you will receive an invoice the following month. Your monthly billing period will always cover a full calendar month, from the 1st to the last day of the month. For example, if you close out a sponsored job posting mid-month, the invoice will still only be generated at the beginning of the next month when the full billing cycle of a month has been reached.

Question: Will I get one invoice per campaign?

Answer: No, our billing period covers an entire calendar month. If your campaign is spread across several months, you will receive an invoice for every month that your Sponsored Jobs are running.

Why You Can’t Find Your Job on Indeed

Indeed may not display a job post in search results due to various issues, including unverified employer accounts, pending reviews, or a missing payment method for sponsored jobs. Other reasons include the job being paused, reaching a daily budget, or not meeting our Job Posting Standards. If your job is still missing after troubleshooting, contact our support team.

URL Name

203366510

Title

Why You Can’t Find Your Job on Indeed

Details

Overview

There are a few reasons why you may not be able to find your job on Indeed. This article explains how to check if your job is active. We’ll also share common reasons jobs might not be visible, and how to fix these issues.

Important: Indeed may reject or remove jobs or company listings at any time and for any reason. We do this to help protect job seekers and maintain platform quality.

How to check if your job is active

Understanding job relevance

Indeed shows jobs based on relevance. This means:

  • Different people get different results based on their search, location, and interests
  • Your job may not appear in every search, even when it’s active
  • The job connects with people who are the best fit

Because of this, searching for your job may not show accurate results.

Did you know?Sponsored Jobs and jobs using Indeed Apply receive more visibility when similar jobs compete for the same candidates.

Check your Company Page

The best way to confirm your job is active:

  1. Go to your Company Page.
  2. Find your job in the Jobs tab.
Check your Jobs tab
  1. Go to the Jobs tab in your employer account.
  2. Navigate to All jobs to review your open jobs.
  3. Learn more about how to view your job posts.
Don’t search for your own job

Searching for your job on Indeed can be misleading because:

  • Search results are personalized
  • Your activity affects your search results
  • Clicking Sponsored Jobs might use your budget

Reasons why your job isn’t visible

Can’t find the job on your Company Page? Here are some possible issues and their solutions.

Account or verification issues
Issue: Your account isn’t verified.

Problem: Before jobs you posted in your Indeed account can go live, you need to verify your account.

Solution: Follow the steps to verify your account.

Issue: Your account is under review.

Problem: Your account may have reduced visibility while it’s under review.

Solution: Contact us for help. Possible reasons for a review are:

  • A potential violation of our job posting quality standards
  • You need to supply proof of identity
Issue: You posted a Sponsored Job, but there’s no payment method on file.

Problem: Sponsored Jobs require a payment method.

Solution: Set up your billing information and payment method. Learn more about how to pay your Indeed invoices.

Job status or settings
Issue: You paused or closed your job.

Problem: Paused or closed jobs don’t appear in search results.

Solution: Change the job’s status to Open.

Issue: Your Sponsored Job reached its daily limit.

Problem: Your Sponsored Job is removed from search results once it reaches a daily spend limit.

Solution: The job will be back once the budget is reset the next day. Contact your account executive if you need help adjusting your budget.

Issue: You posted several similar jobs.

Problem: If you’ve posted similar jobs, we may have grouped them together in the search results. Indeed automatically selects the most relevant job to display.

Solution: Job seekers can select View similar jobs with this employer for a list of all the jobs you posted.

Quality and policy issues
Issue: Your job may not meet our quality standards.

Problem: We may remove jobs that aren’t accurate, easy to read, or professional.

Solution: You may need to update the job title or description to reinstate these jobs. Review our job posting quality standards for more information.

Issue: You reposted the job.

Problem: You may have posted a job that appears to be a copy of an existing job already on Indeed. Posting the same job more than once may result in it being hidden or requiring sponsorship.

Solution: Review our policy on job posting integrity and duplicates.

Tip: Visit our Indeed Policy Center for an overview of all our policies.

Writing Effective Job Descriptions and Titles

This article helps employers optimize their Indeed job posts to attract better candidates by outlining best practices for writing clear, concise job titles (by including field or job level) and comprehensive job descriptions (including compensation/benefits, job type, requirements, and a company description).

  • Oct 31, 2025
  • Knowledge

URL Name

203134734

Title

Writing Effective Job Descriptions and Titles

Details

[Embedded video omitted]

Overview

Writing a good job post helps you find the right people for your job. On Indeed, good job titles and descriptions help more people see your jobs. They also help you attract better candidates.

How to write a good job title

A clear and concise job title helps job seekers quickly understand the role and its level. This improves the chances of them finding and applying to your job.

Keep it short and clear.
  • Explain what the job is.
  • If you want a person from a certain field, add that to the title. For example, “Pharmaceutical Sales Representative” is better than “Sales Representative.”
Say what the job level is.
  • If the job is for a certain level, like a junior role, you can add that. For example, “Junior Accountant” is clear.
Don’t use:
  • Titles that are too long
  • Short forms or numbers that job seekers won’t know
  • Exclamation marks

How to write a good job description

A clear job description helps people decide if they are right for the job. It tells them about the pay, the work, the skills needed, and what your company is like.

Include details on compensation and benefits.
  • A good job post should list the pay and benefits
Provide details on the job type.
  • Is the job part-time or full-time?
  • Is the job on-site, remote, or hybrid?
  • If on-site, where is the job located?
  • Is shift work required?
Add requirements for the job.
  • What is this job about?
  • What are the required skills or education?
  • How many years of experience are required?
  • Are any licenses required?
Use gender-neutral words.

Using words that are gender-neutral can help more people apply to your job. This means you will reach job seekers of any gender.

Examples:

  • Instead of “Businessman” use “Business Person”
  • Instead of “Craftsman” use “Artisan”
  • Instead of “Doorman” use “Door Attendant”
Add a company description.

The company description should include important facts about your company, its culture, and what it offers employees.

To edit a company description, follow the steps below:

  1. Sign in and open your jobs page.
  2. Select the three dots next to the job you want to change.
  3. Select Edit job.
  4. Select the pencil icon next to Job Description.
  5. Add your company description then click Done.
  6. Select Save changes at the bottom of the page.

About Indeed’s Mobile Apps

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

There are only two official Indeed apps. Use this guide to confirm you’re downloading legitimate software. We’ll also explain how to protect yourself from common scams and what to do if you downloaded an unsafe app.

Purpose

Indeed offers official mobile apps to make job searching easier. These apps are built and managed by Indeed. They’re safe when downloaded from official app stores.

Important: Indeed will never ask you to download apps or other software from outside official app stores. We also won’t ask you to install files sent directly to you, such as .apk or other installer files.

Indeed’s mobile apps

To stay safe, make sure you’re using one of our two official apps:

Indeed Job Search (Job Seeker Mobile App)
  • Search and apply for jobs
  • Get alerts about new jobs
  • Track your applications
  • Upload and update your resume
  • Message employers
Indeed Flex (for hourly shifts and temporary work)
  • Find and book flexible work shifts
  • Manage your Flex work schedule
  • Track earnings and payments
  • Communicate with Flex employers

Actions

Downloading the app
  1. Go to the Apple App Store or Google Play Store.
  2. Search for “Indeed Job Search” or “Indeed Flex”.
  3. Check that the publisher is “Indeed Jobs”, “Indeed Flex”, or “Indeed, Inc.” before you download.
Verifying that the app is legitimate
Only download from official sources

Download apps only from the Apple App Store or Google Play Store.

Check the following details
  • Developer name: “Indeed, Inc.”, “Indeed Jobs”, or “Indeed Flex”
  • Download count and reviews: Official apps have millions of downloads and many reviews
  • Verified badge: Some app stores show a badge for trusted apps.
Warning signs of fake apps
  • Requests to download files outside app stores
  • App names like “Indeed Interview” or “Indeed Hiring”
  • Developers that aren’t Indeed, Inc, Indeed Jobs, or Indeed Flex
  • Requests to install an app as part of a job interview or hiring process
  • Requests for unusual permissions (full file access, camera, or microphone without reason)
Protecting yourself if you’ve downloaded a suspicious app
  1. Delete the app immediately.
  2. Don’t enter any personal information.
  3. Run a security scan on your device.
  4. Report the fraudulent app to the app store.

Important: Learn more about what to do if you think you’ve been scammed.

FAQs

Question: Does Indeed have an “Indeed Interview” app?

Answer: No. Indeed doesn’t have an app called “Indeed Interview”. Any request to download this app is not legitimate.

Question: Will Indeed ever ask me to install an .apk file?

Answer: No. Indeed apps are available only through the Apple App Store and Google Play Store. If someone sends you an .apk file claiming to be from Indeed, don’t install it.

Question: I received a message asking me to download an Indeed app to complete my interview. Is this real?

Answer: No. Indeed doesn’t require special apps for interviews. Don’t click links or download files from the message.

Tip: Learn more about verifying emails and text messages claiming to be from Indeed.
