About Jobs on Indeed: What You Need to Know

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

You may have noticed that the application process is different for some jobs on Indeed. That’s because there are two types of job listings. In this article, we’ll explain the differences between these two types of jobs and answer some common questions. We’ll also help you fix problems with the Apply now button.

Types of jobs on Indeed

Some employers post their jobs on Indeed. We take other jobs from external sources, such as the employer’s website. Here’s the difference between the two.

Apply on Indeed

The apply button opens the application process on Indeed.

  • Apply without leaving Indeed.
  • Save your application as a draft on Indeed and finish it later.
  • Communicate safely with employers through your Messages.
  • Track your application status in My jobs.
  • Receive confirmation emails so you know your application was submitted.

Did you know? If you request a copy of your data, you’ll get a complete list of the jobs you’ve applied for on Indeed.

Apply on the company site

Does the apply button bring you to a different site? It’s like we scan the World Wide Web for jobs and pull them into your Indeed search results.

  • Indeed will redirect you to the employer’s applicant tracking system (ATS).
  • You’ll use your personal communication channels to keep the employer updated.
  • You won’t receive communication from Indeed about your application status.
  • Employers may communicate with you through their hiring platform. They may also email you.

FAQs

Question: Is Indeed free?

Answer: Yes, Indeed is a free search, apply, and interview tool.

Question: Can I use Indeed in other languages and locations?

Answer: Yes. Visit our Worldwide page for a list of available countries.

Technical support

Issue: The ‘Apply now’ button isn’t working.

Solution:

  1. Disable pop-ups or ad blockers.
  2. Refresh the page and try again.
  3. Open the application in a new window.
  4. Try using a different web browser (Chrome, Firefox, Safari, etc).
  5. Open the application in an incognito window.
  6. Move to a more stable internet connection.

How To Verify Emails From Indeed

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Indeed may email you about jobs, company alerts, messages from employers, or activity on your account. We will never ask for money, personal information, or login details by email. If an email does this, it might be a scam. This article shows you how to tell real emails from fake ones, what to do if an email looks suspicious, and how to stay safe while looking for work.

Identifying emails from Indeed

When do we email you?

You may get emails from Indeed about:

  • Updates on job applications
  • Job and company alerts you signed up for
  • Messages from employers sent through Indeed
  • Automated invitations to apply
  • Login security, like password resets, or changes to your account settings
How do we email you?

Emails from Indeed will always come from one of these domains:

  • @indeed.com
  • @indeedemail.com
  • @indeedmail.com

Tip: Hover your mouse over the sender to see the full email address.

Recognizing scam emails

Check for errors

Scammers often make mistakes you can spot:

  • Bad spelling or grammar
  • Wrong logos or colors
  • A sender address that is not an official Indeed domain
  • Links that go to strange websites. Hover over links to see the full address before clicking
Does it feel right?

Scammers may also try to trick you by:

  • Making you feel rushed, like “your account will be closed soon”
  • Asking for money, bank information, or other personal details
  • Claiming to be “processing” your account for another company, like Amazon or PayPal
  • Pretending to be from Indeed, using logos or colors
  • Offering jobs that sound too good to be true

Important: Indeed never emails job offers or asks for personal information.

What to do if you suspect a scam

  • Don’t click on links or attachments.
  • Mark the email as phishing and delete it.
  • Run a virus scan if you accidentally clicked a link or downloaded a file.

Staying safe while job searching

To stay safe, only log in and update your account on the Indeed website or through the official app. Check out these extra resources from the Job Seeker Help Center:

  • Guidelines for Safe Job Search
  • Spam vs. Scam: Spotting the Difference
  • How To Verify Suspicious Employer Communication
  • I May Have Been Scammed, What Can I Do?
  • Text Messages or Phone Calls Claiming To Be Indeed? It Might Be a Scam

Indeed Job Seeker Guidelines

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Indeed wants to keep its site safe and professional for job seekers. Be respectful when discussing jobs with employers that align with your skills and interests. Breaking these rules may result in your account being closed.

Important: You must meet certain requirements to create an Indeed account. Learn more about age, location, email, and phone number requirements.

General guidelines

Be professional.
  • Use polite and respectful language.
  • Reply to messages quickly.
  • Let the employer know if you want to stop the conversation.
  • Don’t use obscene, profane, or vulgar language.
  • Don’t use language that is hateful or discriminatory.
  • Don’t send messages that don’t relate to finding a job.
Be honest and respectful.
  • Be truthful about who you are.
  • Don’t pretend to be someone else.
  • Do not misrepresent your location or resume information.
  • Respect confidentiality when discussing any sensitive or proprietary information.
  • Don’t impersonate another person or entity or falsely represent yourself.
  • Don’t try to sell products or services to employers.
  • Don’t use third-party bots or other automated tools to apply for jobs.
Be safe and aware.
  • Don’t share personal or sensitive details that aren’t needed for the job.
  • Look up the employer to confirm the opportunity is legitimate. Check our guidelines for a safe job search.
  • Report suspicious activity or inappropriate behavior from an employer.
  • Don’t send or respond to job emails that are too general, like newsletters. Report them if needed.

Important: Using web forms on any Indeed Platform, Program, or Service is not allowed. Your access to Indeed’s Platform, Programs, or Services may be interrupted or blocked.

Resume guidelines

You can include
  • Your resume
  • A cover letter
  • Writing samples
  • Certifications
  • Professional licenses
Do not include
  • Resumes posted on behalf of someone else
  • Private details like:
    • Your full date of birth
    • Financial information
    • Government-issued identification numbers
  • Photos that don’t relate to your job search, such as:
    • Photos of children
    • Sexual or violent images
  • Anything not related to finding a job
  • AI prompts or scripts that could disrupt, interfere with, or manipulate how systems or services on our site function

Important: Indeed may hide resumes that don’t meet these quality standards.

Application guidelines

We may limit the number of job applications you can submit each day. This helps keep the platform fair and running well.

We’ll let you know when you’ve reached your daily application limit.

Social Media Guidelines

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Connect with Indeed and other job seekers through our social media platforms. You can:

  • Share your experiences
  • Ask questions
  • Get help from our support team

Indeed’s mission

Our mission is simple: We help people get jobs. We welcome your questions and ideas. When you post, please:

  • Stay on topic
  • Be respectful
  • Do not use offensive words or share illegal content

Posts and comments from users are their own and do not reflect Indeed’s views.

Using Indeed’s social media platforms

  • Companies that post jobs on Indeed handle their own hiring. They are responsible for replying to your applications.

  • When you see a reply from “Indeed” on our social pages, they are written by a real person on our Social Media team.

  • Need help with an Indeed question?

  • Ask us on our social pages.

  • You can also reach support on X (formerly Twitter).

Community guidelines

We want our pages to be safe and helpful. We reserve the right to remove or hide your post if it contains any of the following types of content:

  • Obscenities, discriminatory language, or other language not suitable for a public forum
  • Ads, spam, or promotions for other companies, products, or websites
  • Personal information (like emails, phone numbers, addresses, or full names you don’t have permission to share)
  • Scams, false or misleading information, or anything unlawful
  • Pretending to be Indeed or claiming to work for Indeed without permission

All posts and comments are subject to the terms set forth in our Terms of Service.

If someone keeps breaking these rules, we may block them from posting on our pages.

Important: These rules are for posts on Indeed’s social media pages (Facebook, X, Instagram, LinkedIn, TikTok).

Why Is Indeed Collecting Demographic Data From Job Seekers?

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

These FAQs explain demographic data, why Indeed or employers might collect it, and how it’s used.

Indeed collects demographic data through an optional survey. This data is confidential. Not sharing it will not result in adverse treatment from Indeed. 

Some employers also ask applicants to provide demographic data. You may get these voluntary self-identification questions when applying for jobs on Indeed. When answering these employer-asked questions, you may be able to share your answers with Indeed or save them to help pre-fill future applications. Declining to share this information with Indeed won’t result in adverse treatment.

Employers that ask for demographic data may be allowed or required to do so under US federal law. When they use Indeed functionality to ask about disability status, they state that they are federal contractors or subcontractors. These employers must collect this information from job applicants to comply with the Department of Labor’s Office of Federal Contract Compliance Programs (“OFCCP”) requirements.

Important: Learn more about Indeed’s Privacy Policy and Terms of Service.

About demographic data

What is demographic data, and what types of data does Indeed collect?

Demographics represent the various characteristics of a group of people. These can include age, race, and disability status.

Indeed’s demographic survey

Many types of demographic data exist. However, Indeed seeks to collect only a limited set from job seekers who choose to share it. US-based job seekers can share data across six categories. You can choose which ones to share: 

  • Age (birth year)
  • Race and ethnicity
  • Gender
  • Disability status
  • LGBTQIA+ community membership
  • Arrest or conviction history
Employer-asked self-identification questions

You may also share your answers to employer-asked self-identification questions with Indeed. This can help pre-fill applications or support other uses described below.

You can revoke your consent to share these answers at any time as described below.

Does the demographic data include my address, Social Security number, phone number, or email address?

No. You must have a profile on Indeed to submit your demographic data. However, Indeed doesn’t collect your address, Social Security number, phone number, or email as part of the job seeker demographic data survey.

You may also share your answers to employer-asked self-identification questions with Indeed. This can help pre-fill applications or support other uses described below.

You can revoke your consent to share these answers at any time as described below.

Why is Indeed collecting this data?

We want to ensure our products help everyone get jobs fairly. If our products aren’t serving certain user groups, we need to know so we can make improvements.

How will the demographic data that I share with Indeed be used?

Depending on your consent settings, Indeed may use your data to:

  • Improve our products and better understand job seekers

  • Check fairness across our tools

  • For example, our AI Ethics team is building tools, systems, and processes that advance responsible and inclusive technology.

  • Support employers with their inclusive hiring efforts. Don’t worry, your data will stay confidential.

  • Share aggregated data, such as application trends. This data doesn’t identify you.

  • Simplify applying by pre-filling future applications

  • Always check these pre-filled answers before submitting your application.

Indeed is committed to your privacy

We won’t:

  • Share your individual demographic data with employers without your permission
  • Show the demographic data you share with us through the survey on your profile or resume

Did you know? Indeed’s parent company is called Recruit Holdings. You can read their statement on algorithms and fairness.

I don’t want to share my demographic data. Can I still use Indeed?

Yes. Sharing your demographic data is optional. You can still use Indeed without sharing it, and you control what you share.

Refusal to share this information won’t result in adverse treatment from Indeed. We hope, however, that you’ll consider sharing to help us improve our offerings.

Where does Indeed collect demographic data from job seekers?

We currently collect demographic data from job seekers in the US, UK, Ireland, and France. We may expand to more countries.

Why am I getting demographic data promos?

Indeed collects data from a wide range of job seekers to make hiring fair for all. These promos appear on different parts of the site. They may be personalized based on your site activity.

Employers and demographic data

Can employers access my personal demographic data from Indeed’s demographic survey?

No. Indeed is committed to our job seekers’ privacy:

  • We don’t share your demographic survey data, the six categories listed above, with employers.

  • We won’t show your demographic survey data on your profile or resume.

  • Indeed may share a summary of aggregated group data with employers upon request. For example:

  • 45% of applicants identify as male

  • 10% of hired applicants identified as Hispanic

  • Employers can’t access your individual data responses without your permission.

  • You can read more in Indeed’s Privacy Policy.

Important: Your answers to the voluntary self-identification questions from employers are separate. If you choose to answer them, your responses are shared with the employer.

Will my demographic data be used to review my job application?

No. The demographic data that you share with Indeed is not part of what employers review when evaluating your application. Some employers are permitted or required by law to ask for demographic data. You may get these voluntary self-identification questions when applying for jobs. Your responses to these questions are shared with the employer. They are responsible for separating your demographic data when reviewing your job application.

Where employers request disability status through Indeed tools, they represent that they are federal contractors or subcontractors. These employers must collect this information to follow OFCCP requirements. 

When answering these employer-asked questions, you may be about to choose to share the answers with Indeed or save them to help pre-fill future applications. Declining to share this information with Indeed will not result in adverse treatment.

Sharing and removing demographic data

How can I share my demographic data?
Through the demographic data survey
  1. Sign in to Indeed.
  2. Click or tap the person icon in the top right.
  3. Select My demographics from the menu.
  4. Complete the survey.

Did you know? You can edit your responses at any time.

During your application

You may have the option to share your answers to employer-asked demographic data questions with Indeed.

How can I delete my demographic data?
If you completed the demographic survey
  1. Sign in to Indeed.
  2. Click or tap the person icon in the top right.
  3. Select My demographics from the menu.
  4. Click or tap Remove my demographic data.
If you shared answers to voluntary self-identification questions with Indeed
  • Uncheck the Share option for future applications, or
  • Delete your voluntary self-identification data.
If you saved answers to pre-fill future applications
  • Submit a personal data deletion request.
  • Follow the process described in Indeed’s Privacy Policy.

Changes to Personal Details in Your Indeed Resume

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

From April 8, 2026, Indeed no longer includes some sensitive personal details in Indeed Resumes. These details are no longer needed for how Indeed Resume works. This update does not apply to resume files uploaded to your profile.

Important: This change only affects demographic and identity-related fields in your Indeed Resume. Demographic data that Indeed collects in other parts of the site is not affected. Check our article on demographic data to learn more.

What changed

We removed the following fields from the Indeed Resume. You can no longer add, edit, or share this information with employers:

  • Citizenship
  • Ethnicity
  • Disability
  • ID (identification) number
  • Date of Birth
  • Gender

These fields no longer appear when creating or updating your resume.

Did you know? This change doesn’t apply to resume files you upload to your profile. If you upload a file that includes this information, employers may still be able to access it.

What happened to your existing data

  • We removed these fields from Indeed Resumes because they are no longer needed.
  • This information is no longer stored in your Indeed Resume.
  • Employers can no longer access this information through your Indeed Resume.

Impact on your job search

This change doesn’t affect your ability to apply for jobs:

  • Your Indeed account and resume remain active.
  • You don’t need to take any action.
  • Your job search and applications continue as usual.
  • You can still share information with employers during the application process, if needed.

Next steps

If you have questions about your data or want to learn more about how Indeed handles personal information, you can visit:

  • Privacy settings in your Indeed account
  • Indeed’s Privacy Policy
  • Requesting or Deleting Your Data

Changing Your Account Email Address and Phone Number

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

From your account settings, you can check your account type, email address, phone number, and passkey. On this page, you’ll learn how to edit the email address and phone number sections, as well as what to do if you experience technical issues.

Did you know? Changes to your email or phone on Indeed will update on Glassdoor if your accounts are linked. Need help? Contact us here.

Changing your email address

Important: You may encounter 2FA while changing your email address. Make sure your phone number is up to date before you change your email address.

Desktop
  1. Sign in to your Indeed account.
  2. Go to your account settings.
  3. Click Change email.
  4. Enter your new email address.
  5. Click Update email.
  6. We’ll send a passcode to the new email’s inbox. Type it in the Enter code box.
  7. Select Continue to confirm the email address change.
Mobile
  1. In your Profile, tap the menu bar and select Settings.
  2. Tap Account settings.
  3. Under Email, tap the Change email button.
  4. Enter your New email address and tap Update email.
  5. Check your new email’s inbox for a code. Type it in the Enter code box.
  6. Tap Continue.

Updating your phone number

Desktop
  1. Sign in to your Indeed account.
  2. Go to your account settings.
  3. Click Change phone number and enter the new number.
  4. Click Verify.
  5. When you receive the code, type it in the Enter code box and Submit.
Mobile
  1. In your Profile, tap the menu bar and select Settings.
  2. Tap Account settings.
  3. Under Phone number, tap the Change phone number button.
  4. Enter your new number and tap Verify.
  5. Check your text messages for a code. Type it in the Enter code box.
  6. Tap Submit.

Important: Click Not now if you can’t access the verification code right away. You’ll still need to Verify phone number from your account settings before the change can be processed.

Technical support

Issue: I received an error message when trying to change my email.

Solution: This likely means there’s already an account associated with the new email address you entered. We cannot merge accounts, so you’d need to close the account tied to the address you entered before completing the change email process.

Issue: I no longer have access to the email associated with my Indeed account.

Solution: Click or tap Don’t have access to this email to update your email. We’ll send a verification code to the phone number in your account settings. Then, you can change your email address.

Issue: I can’t find the Change email button in my account settings

Solution: It’s possible you have an employer account as well as a job seeker account. Contact us using the form below so we can help.

Issue: I need to change my email, but I can’t get past 2-step verification because I no longer have access to the phone number.

Solution: Change the phone number in your account settings. If you aren’t able to sign in, contact us, and we can remove the number tied to your account.

FAQs

Question: I need help remembering my email. Can I still sign in?

Answer: No. You’ll need to create a new account if you can’t access or verify your email.

Question: I changed the phone number on my profile. Does this also update my account settings?

Answer: No. Update the phone number from your account settings to change the number tied to your account.

Question: I’ve linked my Indeed and Glassdoor accounts. If I change my phone number on Indeed, will it also update on Glassdoor?

Answer: Yes. If your accounts are merged, updates to your email or phone on Indeed will automatically update your Glassdoor profile. Having issues? Contact us here.

Question: Can I use a temporary email for my Indeed account?

Answer: No. Use a stable, permanent email address to help protect your account. Find out more about our email requirements.

Closing an Account

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Leaving Indeed? We’re sorry to see you go. This page explains how to close your account and what to expect from the process. Do you also want to delete your Indeed data? This page can help you get started.

Two ways to close an account

Option one: From your account settings

Tip: Review your communications settings. Unsubscribe from any unwanted emails and notifications before closing your account.

Getting started
  1. Open your account settings.
  2. Click or tap Close my account. This link is located below your email address.
  3. Complete the optional account closure survey.
What if a link to “Close my account” is not available?

You may have an employer account. Contact us so we can close this account for you.

What to expect

When you close your account, all associated information will be removed. You can’t reactivate this account, but you can always create a new account with the same email address.

Option two: By deleting your data
Getting started
  1. Use the Personal Data Request form.
  2. Learn more about requesting or deleting your data.
What to expect
  • This action will delete your data and close your account. This process can take up to 30 days. Once you receive confirmation that the request is complete, you can create a new account with the same email address.
  • If you have a merged account, deleting your Indeed account means you can’t use the same login to sign in to Glassdoor.
What if your Indeed and Glassdoor accounts are linked?
  • Closing your Indeed account means you can’t use the same login to sign in to Glassdoor.
  • You’ll still have a Glassdoor account. You’ll need to delete your Glassdoor account separately if you want to remove all your data.
  • Contact us here if you need help with your merged accounts.

Using Indeed in the future

Create a new account using the same email address or a different one.

Technical support

Issue: I keep getting emails after closing my account.

Solution: Do you have more than one account? Check your email subscriptions to avoid receiving unwanted emails.

FAQs

Question: Can I pause my account?

Answer: No. However, you can unsubscribe from job alerts, emails, and other notifications in your communication settings.

Following Up On Your Application

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Some employers let job seekers start a conversation through Indeed after applying. This feature only works if the employer turns it on. After you apply, you’ll get a notification to let you know you can message the employer. In this article, we’ll explain when you can send a message and how to do so.

Benefits

  • Follow up on your application
  • Share why you’re a good fit for the job
  • Introduce yourself
  • Ask a question about the role

Important: This feature is not available for every job. The employer must choose to turn it on.

Sending a message

You’ll get an alert if the feature is available for a job for which you applied. There are three types of alerts:

  • A Send a message option on your My jobs page under the listing
  • An email from Indeed inviting you to send a message
  • A notification in your account

Did you know? You won’t get these alerts right after you apply. There will be a short delay of at least a few days.

How can I send a message?
  1. Select the option to send a message in one of the three alerts.
  2. Enter your message in the text box.
  3. Click or tap Send to start a conversation with the employer.
What happens next?

Your message will be:

  • Shared with your application
  • Saved in your Messages

Verifying Your Phone Number

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

You might have to add and confirm your phone number when you create an account or apply for a job on Indeed. Learn why you need to verify a phone number, when to verify, and how to do it.

Benefits of a verified phone number

  • Let employers know you’re active on Indeed
  • Keep your account and personal details safe
  • Easily recover your account if you forget your password

Important: The verified phone number doesn’t have to match the number on your profile. It does need to meet our phone number requirements.

When verification is required

You’ll have to verify your phone number to:

  • Create an account on Indeed
  • Apply for a job

How to verify your phone number

When you create an account
  1. Follow the steps to sign in.
  2. Enter your phone number.
  3. Click or tap Verify. You’ll receive a 6-digit code.
  4. Enter the code and Submit.
When you apply
  1. Open a job post to access the job details.
  2. Click or tap Apply now.
  3. Enter your phone number.
  4. Click or tap Verify. You’ll receive a 6-digit code.
  5. Enter the code and Submit it to continue with the application.
In your account settings
  1. Open your account settings.
  2. Click or tap Add phone.
  3. Enter your phone number.
  4. Click or tap Verify. You’ll receive a 6-digit code.
  5. Enter the code and Submit.

Ways to receive your code

Text message (SMS)

The most common method for verifying your number.

Phone call (including landlines)

You’ll get an automated call with your code.

WhatsApp

You can choose to receive the code as a WhatsApp message. You must have a WhatsApp account connected to the phone number you’re verifying.

Important: The verification code is valid for 10 minutes. Click or tap Resend code to get a new notification or phone call.

What if you don’t get a code?

Check out our basic troubleshooting tips for solving common phone verification issues.

Next steps

Your phone number was verified successfully.

You can now create an account on Indeed or complete your application. Your phone number is now in your account settings, and you can change or delete it at any time.

Job seekers: Company Pages and Reviews

Adding and Deleting Company Reviews

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Use our anonymous company reviews to share your experiences with other job seekers. Let them know what it’s like to work for your current or previous employer. In this article, we’ll explain how to add or delete a review.

Write a review

  1. Sign in to your Indeed account.
  2. Navigate to the Company Page for the employer you’re reviewing.
  3. Select the Reviews tab at the top of the Company Page.
  4. Click or tap Write a review and fill out the review form.

Once Indeed’s moderation team approves and publishes your review, it will appear on the Company Page.

Tip: You can’t edit your review, but you can Write an updated review. This resubmitted review will replace the original review you posted.

Remove your review

There are two ways to delete a review you wrote.

From your My Reviews page
  1. Sign in to your Indeed account.
  2. Click or tap the profile icon.
  3. Select My reviews from the dropdown.
  4. You’ll find all your submitted reviews and their status on your My reviews and contributions page.
  5. To delete a review, click or tap the three vertical dots, then select Delete.
From the employer’s Company Page
  1. Sign in to your Indeed account.
  2. Navigate to the Company Page for the employer you reviewed.
  3. Click or tap on the Reviews tab at the top of the Company Page.
  4. Find your review and click or tap the Delete link next to the title to remove it.

FAQs

Question: Can Indeed reject my review?

Answer: Yes, Indeed will reject your review if it violates our guidelines.

Question: Can I leave more than one review from the same company?

Answer: No, you can only write one review per company.

Question: Can I edit my review?

Answer: No, but you can resubmit your review. When you write and submit an updated review, it’ll replace your existing one.

Question: Is my review anonymous?

Answer: Yes. We don’t ask you to include your name or contact information. We do need job title, job status, and location.

Company Reviews: Best Practices, Policies, and Guidelines

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

This article explains how to write reviews and answer questions on a company page. It shows what you can include, what is not allowed, and why some posts may be removed. It also explains how to report content that breaks our guidelines and answers common questions.

Important: We may remove Company Page content that breaks our guidelines or policies.

Best practices

What you can submit
  • One review or answer per person, per company
  • Reviews or answers only for companies you work for or have worked for
  • You may submit questions about the working environment of any company
What you should do
  • Share your own, original review based on your time at the company.
  • Give your own opinion. Don’t state opinions as facts.
  • Use examples from your experience.
  • Avoid naming or identifying people who are not public figures.
  • Explain what you liked or did not like. You may share constructive suggestions.
  • Focus on the company’s environment and culture.
  • Be truthful and accurate. Your content should be your own opinion.
What you shouldn’t include
  • Review of the company’s products or services
  • Jargon, symbols, or made-up words
  • Someone else’s words or content
  • Non-public information or legal claims
  • Personal information about you or others

If we reject your review, but you believe it meets our guidelines, you may be able to submit a new one.

Content guidelines

Your anonymity
  • Content you submit is anonymous.
  • Your account’s identifiable details aren’t shared with the company.
  • Any extra information you include in your content will be visible.

Important: Indeed may honor subpoenas, search warrants, law enforcement requests, or court orders that require us to share your identity, user content, or other information.

How we handle your content
  • We don’t edit or change reviews, questions, or answers.
  • Content that violates Indeed’s guidelines or policies may be removed.
  • All content is subject to our Terms of Service.
  • Company Page content is user-generated. We don’t guarantee its accuracy or endorse it.
What your content should represent

Your submissions should show:

  • Your own opinions
  • Your personal experience with the company’s working environment

Your experience may involve others. You may mention people only in relation to the workplace, but:

  • Don’t include identifying information about non-public individuals.
  • Don’t share personal details that aren’t related to your workplace experience.
What is not allowed

Content is not allowed if it includes:

  • Unlawful, fraudulent, discriminatory, threatening, abusive, libelous, or defamatory statements
  • Obscene or otherwise inappropriate material
  • Sexual, ethnic, racial, or other discriminatory slurs
  • Suggestions for actions against the company
  • Irrelevant or unhelpful content
  • Personal details about someone outside their role at a company
  • Attempts to make factual claims about the company instead of sharing your experience
Not a place to report misconduct

This platform is not for:

  • Reporting crimes
  • Sharing internal company details
  • Making claims of illegal activity

Indeed reserves the right to remove such content.

Company involvement

An official company representative may claim a company page. They can add official information to the page.

Review integrity

We may remove reviews if there’s evidence that a reviewer was:

  • Paid
  • Forced or pressured to post content

Reporting Company Page content

How to report content

If you believe a review or other content breaks our guidelines:

  1. Click or tap the three dots on the review.
  2. Select Report review.
  3. Choose the best reason from the menu.
  4. Click or tap Submit.
What happens next
  • We’ll review the content and remove it within 1–2 business days if it breaks our guidelines.
  • We won’t update you about the outcome.

FAQs

Question: Can I remove my review?

Answer: Yes. Find out how to delete your review.

Question: I uploaded photos, but I can’t find them. Did Indeed remove them?

Answer: Yes. We removed the Photos tab from Company pages and deleted all uploaded photos. These photos are no longer accessible by Indeed.

Understanding the Work Wellbeing Score

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Work should feel good, not stressful or exhausting. That’s why Indeed created the Work Wellbeing 100. It ranks the best companies where people feel happy, supported, and valued. The Work Wellbeing Score is like a report card for how people feel at work. Learn how we measure wellbeing and find companies that fit your values. Ready to apply where you’ll feel your best?

Measuring work wellbeing

The Work Wellbeing 100 lists the top 100 public companies based on Indeed’s Work Wellbeing Score. The score measures:

  • Happiness: How happy people are with their work life
  • Purpose: How meaningful their work feels
  • Satisfaction: How much people enjoy their day-to-day life at work
  • Stress: How manageable people find their work stress

How the Work Wellbeing Score works

Employees can take the Work Wellbeing survey to rate their happiness, purpose, satisfaction, and stress. Experts made this short survey to understand how a company supports wellbeing.

When at least ten users take the survey, you’ll find the Work Wellbeing Score on Indeed’s Company Pages.

Did you know? If you’re an employer, the score can help you understand what you’re doing right and what you might want to work on.

We keep your information anonymous.
  • The results do not include your name. They do include your job title, location, and employment status.
  • The surveys are anonymous and optional. Check our Terms of Service for more information.
  • To keep the information anonymous, don’t include identifiable information in your review. All reviews are subject to Indeed’s guidelines.

Reading the scales and labels

Labels for wellbeing insights on Company Pages help you compare companies. Each indicator has a scale (PDF), and about 20% of companies fall into one of five levels:

  • Low
  • Below Average
  • Average
  • Above Average
  • High

Browsing the top companies

This Career Guide article shows the top twenty companies that focus on how their workers feel.

Finding jobs with work wellbeing

Use this job board to browse employers who care about work wellbeing and are hiring now.

Job seekers: Messages

About Indeed Messages

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Indeed Messages lets you safely send and receive messages with employers. This page explains the main features, shows the benefits, and answers common questions. It also gives tips for fixing simple problems. Knowing how to use Indeed Messages can help you stay organized, keep your information private, and reply to job opportunities quickly.

Benefits

  • Connect with employers about jobs of interest.
  • Track which messages have been read with read receipts.
  • Maintain privacy while managing multiple applications.

Features

Messaging
  • Send messages to employers directly from job listings.
  • Some job listings include employer contact details for direct messaging.
Read receipts
  • Messages display as “read” or “unread”.
  • You’ll know whether employers have seen your message.
Online status
  • Employers can see a green dot next to your name when you’re online.

Tip: Turn on read receipts and online status from your communications settings.

Actions

Access your messages
  • Open the app or browser to view your inbox.
  • If a message doesn’t appear, check your personal email for a copy.
Follow up on an application
  • Some employers let you start a conversation after applying.
  • Check the job description for the employer’s contact details if you can’t message them through Indeed.

Did you know? Use My jobs to check your application status.

Archive or delete messages you no longer need
What’s the difference?
  • Archive a conversation you want to keep, but not in your inbox. You can restore it to your inbox at any time.
  • Delete a conversation to permanently remove it from your Messages. You can only delete conversations, not individual messages.

Important: Deleting a conversation can’t be undone.

How to do it
  • Swipe the message, or or click or tap the three dots next to it.
  • Click or tap Archive or Delete.
Report a message or phishing attempt

Use the Report Spam feature to report conversations that aren’t about work.

Troubleshooting

Issue: I opened a new message and got a “Something went wrong” error.

Solution: This is usually a system error. Try clearing your cache and cookies, then restart your device or browser.

Here are instructions for clearing cache and cookies on desktop and mobile browsers.

Issue: The message doesn’t load.

Solution:

  1. Check your email inbox for a copy of the message.
  2. Close and reopen the app or browser.
  3. Try again later.
Issue: My messages appear frozen with a blue banner: “Messaging this employer is currently disabled. Please check back later.”

Solution: Messages may be frozen for privacy reasons while the employer account is under moderation review.

Issue: I am missing messages.

Solution: You may have more than one account. Check the email address associated with your Indeed account.

FAQs

Question: I sent a message by mistake. Can I unsend or undo it?

Answer: No. Unfortunately, this option is not available.

Question: I have more than one account on Indeed. Can I open a message sent to one of my accounts using another account?

Answer: No, you must use the account linked to your application.

Question: Does Indeed offer tools to help write messages or letters?

Answer: Yes. For a variety of templates and examples, review the Cover Letter FAQ or the Indeed Career Guide.

Question: I received a message inviting me to apply. Is this real?

Answer: Maybe. Employers can message you if your resume is set to Employers can find you on Indeed, and you’ve been active on Indeed for at least 30 days.

Still not sure? Learn more about verifying suspicious employer communication.

Question: Can a deleted conversation reappear in my inbox?

Answer: Yes. A deleted conversation can show up in your inbox again if the employer sends you a new message in that same thread. If you don’t want to keep getting messages from this employer, you can mark the conversation as Spam.

Blocking Employers on Indeed

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Certain account details may be visible to employers depending on your profile settings. This page will show you how to hide your profile from certain employers on Indeed.

Getting started

  1. Create an account or sign in.
  2. Click Profile in the navigation or tap the Profile icon.
  3. Click or tap Employers can/can’t find you to open your profile settings menu
  4. Open the Block employers section by scrolling to the bottom of your profile settings page.

Using the feature

Blocking employers
  1. Enter the name of the employer you want to block.
  2. Select all relevant matches from the search results.
  3. Click or tap Save.
Unblocking an employer

Click or tap the trash can icon next to an employer to remove them from the list. This will allow them to find your profile again.

Did you know? You can block up to 100 employers.

What happens when you block an employer

When you set your profile to Employers can find you.

Blocked employers won’t be able to find your profile or resume in searches when logged into their account.

When you set your profile to Employers can’t find you.

No employers will be able to find your profile or resume in searches.

When you apply for a job.

Employers you’ve blocked can still access your application and profile details if you’ve applied to a job for that employer.

Find out more about what you share with employers.

Important: Blocking isn’t guaranteed. To fully prevent any employer from viewing your profile, set your profile visibility to Employers can’t find you in your profile settings.

Can’t find your employer?

Here are some tips if you don’t see your employer listed:

  • Double-check the spelling.
  • Try different versions of the company name.
  • The employer might not be listed on Indeed.

Reporting a Message or Phishing Attempt

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Indeed Messages are a way for you and employers to communicate about the job listing, resume, or application. You may receive a message containing inappropriate, misleading, or concerning content. Use the Report Spam feature in your Indeed Messages inbox to report conversations that aren’t about work. These spam messages are usually about outstanding bills, health insurance, banking issues, and more. Never respond to a message you believe to be spam.

Reporting a message

  1. Go to your Messages and open the message you want to report.
  2. Click or tap the three dots in the top-right corner of the message and select Report Spam.
  3. Select the main reason why you are reporting the conversation. You can provide additional information in the text field.
  4. Click or tap Report and move to spam.
What happens next?

We’ll review the message you reported. If it violates Indeed guidelines, we’ll remove the sender’s job post. We won’t follow up with you on the action we took. Future messages from the sender will automatically go to your Spam folder.

Important: You can also delete the conversation from your spam folder.

Restoring a message

  1. Go to your Messages and select Spam instead of Inbox.
  2. Open the message you want to restore.
  3. Click or tap Restore at the bottom of the message.

FAQs

Question: Can I delete messages from the Spam folder?

Answer: No, sorry. This option is not available. Read our article about Indeed Messages for more frequently asked questions.

Text Messages or Phone Calls Claiming To Be Indeed? It Might Be a Scam

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Indeed will never reach out to job seekers to offer employment through phone calls, text messages, or apps like WhatsApp or Telegram. If you get these types of messages, they are scams. This article explains how to recognize real texts from Indeed, spot common scam tactics, and protect yourself.

Identifying messages from Indeed

When do we text you?

You may receive text messages from Indeed for:

  • Reminders about job applications or saved jobs
  • Notifications, such as new matches or alerts from employers to apply through Indeed
  • Login security, including two-factor authentication codes

Indeed doesn’t directly text you to offer a job. However, you may receive notifications from employers to apply to a job through Indeed.

What number do we use?
Reminders and notifications

Messages from Indeed may appear in two ways on your device:

Verified sender (on compatible devices)

  • Sender name: “Indeed - Employer & Interview Updates”
  • Indeed’s verified logo with a blue checkmark

Short codes (standard SMS)

  • 36666
  • 56666
  • 463333

These short codes are registered with all major phone carriers. When you get a text from these short codes, your phone may display “Indeed” as the sender name. This is called CNAM, or Caller Name.

Two-factor authentication (2FA)

You may also receive login security codes from these numbers:

  • 22395
  • 78156

These short codes are shared with other companies, so not every message from them will be from Indeed. Learn more in our guide to two-factor authentication on Indeed.

Important: Only trust messages from our official short codes or our verified sender. Block and report any other numbers claiming to be Indeed.

Recognizing scam calls or texts

Scammers use many different tactics. Here are common ones:

Scams impersonating Indeed
  • Offering a remote or flexible job by call or text
  • Claiming to be an Indeed HR Representative or recruiter
  • Asking you to pay money through WhatsApp, Telegram, or phone calls
  • Creating a sense of urgency, such as revoking access to your Indeed account or resolving a technical issue within a time limit
  • Asking for personal information or your Indeed sign-in details

Important: You can only change or update your account at Indeed.com or through the mobile app.

Scams pretending to be employers
  • Making job offers that seem too good to be true
  • Asking you to click a link or contact them outside of Indeed
How employers should contact you

Employers can:

  • Message you directly on Indeed
  • Call or text you only if you shared your number in your application

Learn more about verifying suspicious correspondence from an employer.

Important: Indeed doesn’t share your contact details with employers. Find out what personal information you share with employers when you create your profile or apply for a job.

Other common scams

Scammers may also pose as:

  • A bank, service provider, or even your boss or a relative
  • A postal service saying you have a package on hold, with a “Click here” link
  • A tax agency demanding immediate payment
  • Tech support offering an urgent “fix” for your device

These scams try to steal your identity, money, login details, or even access to your devices.

Examples of text messages pretending to be Indeed

What to do if you get a scam text or call

Take action fast.

Learn the steps to take if you believe you’ve been scammed.

Report the call or text.
Android
  • Block and report a Google Message.
  • Mark calls as spam.
iOS
  • Block, filter, and report messages on iPhone.
  • Block unwanted calls on iPhone.

Staying safe while job searching

We want you to stay safe when searching for a job. Use these extra Job Seeker Help Center resources:

  • Guidelines for Safe Job Search
  • Common Job Scams to Avoid
  • How To Identify Scam Job Titles on Indeed
  • Spam vs. Scam
  • About Indeed’s Mobile Apps

Cloudflare Errors: Basic Troubleshooting

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Cloudflare is a service that helps protect and speed up websites. Errors usually mean a security setting or network issue blocked or interrupted your connection to the site. While you can’t opt out of Cloudflare, following these steps in order may help fix the problem.

Important: Contact us if you’re receiving a 403 Forbidden Cloudflare error. We’ll need a screenshot or recording of the issue.

Desktop and mobile browsers

Step 1: Disable your browser extensions.
  1. Disable all browser extensions, including privacy-related ones.
    • Examples are McAfee, Norton 360, Survey Junkie, or Surf to Earn.
  2. Reload the page.
Step 2: Turn off the VPN or proxy.

Switch off any VPN or proxy services you’re using.

Step 3: Disable ad blockers.

Switch off any ad-blocking extensions or software.

Step 4: Check your browser settings.
  • Use Chrome, Edge, Safari, or Firefox. These browsers give the best experience.
  • Check that your browser is up to date.
  • Enable JavaScript and cookies.
  • Clear your browser cache. Find out how to do this for mobile or desktop browsers.
Step 5: Try incognito mode.

Open your browser in incognito or private mode.

Step 6: Try a different browser.
  1. Open a different web browser.
  2. Make sure there are no extensions for this browser.
  3. Enter the website address to check if it loads.

Mobile app

Step 1: Delete and reinstall the app.
  1. Reinstall the app on your device.
    • Check the Apple or Android Help Centers for more info.
  2. Restart your phone after reinstalling the app.
Step 2: Turn off the VPN or proxy.

Switch off any VPN or proxy services you’re using.

Step 3: Clear your cache and cookies.

Check our Mobile Apps article for instructions on clearing app files and data.

Next steps

If you’re still experiencing issues after following the steps above, you can:

Step 1: Use a different network.

Switch to another Wi-Fi network in your device’s settings. You can also use your mobile network or hotspot.

Step 2: Reset your router.

Follow your router’s instructions to reset it.

Step 3: Use a different device.

Switch between mobile and desktop.

Contacting us

When to contact us
  • You still have issues after trying the steps above
  • You’re getting a 403 Forbidden Cloudflare error
What to include
  • Whether you tried the troubleshooting steps above
  • A recording of the issue (best option)
  • The RayID details (if possible)
  • When the issue happens:
    • Signing in
    • Searching for jobs
    • Applying
  • What the screen shows:
    • Do you get the Verify that you are human page?
    • After clicking it:
      • Can you continue?
      • Do you get stuck in a loop?
      • Do you get an error page?
If you can’t send a recording

Send screenshots instead:

  • Include the full page with the URL visible
  • Show each step as you move through the site
  • Explain how the URL changes as you go
Extra steps for browser users
  • Include a screenshot of enabled extensions.
  • Include a recording in incognito mode:
    • Open the Console Errors tab before the recording.
    • Here are the instructions on opening the Console tab.
For repeated or constant issues
  • Send a HAR file if you keep getting the same issue, like a loop or repeated checks.
  • Here are the instructions on generating this file.
Submit your request
  • Click or tap Submit a request.
  • Make sure you include all the details above so we can review your issue quickly.

Important: Always include a recording or screenshot of the issue.

Desktop Browsers: Clearing Chrome and Safari Cache and Cookies

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Clearing your cache and cookies from your browser may fix website issues, speed up your browser, and protect your privacy. The steps for desktop and mobile browsers are similar, but there are a few differences. Follow the instructions below for desktop browsers. For mobile browsers, check our article on clearing cache and cookies on a phone or tablet.

Chrome (desktop)

Clearing browsing data
  1. Open Google Chrome.
  2. Click the three dots in the top-right corner.
  3. Hover over More tools.
  4. Select Clear browsing data.
  5. In the new tab, choose a time range. To remove everything, select All time.
Clearing cookies and cached files
  1. Select Cookies and other site data and Cached images and files.
  2. Click Clear data.

Important: Menu names and steps may change between Chrome versions. For the latest instructions, visit Google’s Help Center.

Safari (desktop)

Clearing website data
  1. Open Safari.
  2. In the top menu bar, click Safari, then select Preferences.
  3. Go to the Privacy tab.
  4. Click Manage Website Data.
  5. Click Remove All, then Remove Now to confirm.
  6. Click Done when finished.
Managing cookie settings

In the Privacy tab, you can adjust cookie preferences by selecting Block all cookies or other options under Cookies and website data.

Important: Menu names and steps may change between macOS versions. For the latest instructions, visit Apple’s Help Center.

Email Issues: Basic Troubleshooting

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Are you having trouble signing in, receiving messages from employers, or facing other issues? We’ve collected a few basic email troubleshooting tips to help you resolve these problems.

Problem: Apple’s Hide My Email is causing issues.

Solution
  • Change your email address in your Account settings.
  • Try a different email address.
  • Sign in using the same method you used to create your account.

Important: You can find other troubleshooting methods in Fixing Hide My Email: Signing In, Applying, and Getting Messages.

Problem: Indeed’s emails are marked as spam or junk.

Solution

Check your spam and blocked senders. You may have to unblock Indeed. Here are the instructions for some common email providers:

  • Gmail
  • Outlook
  • Yahoo Mail
  • Apple Mail

Problem: I may have more than one account.

Solution

Close the duplicate account. We aren’t able to merge two or more active accounts.

Problem: I can’t verify my account.

Solution

You’ll get a pop-up reminder to confirm your email address. Click or tap Confirm in the email we delivered to your inbox.

Mobile Apps: Crashes, Freezes, and Performance Issues

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Try these basic troubleshooting tips if the Indeed job search app crashes, can’t open, or won’t respond. Check our guides for mobile browsers and desktop browsers to clear cache and cookies if you use Indeed in a browser.

Reinstall the app

  1. Delete the app from your device.
  2. Download it again from the App Store (iOS) or Google Play (Android).
  3. Restart your phone.

Visit Apple’s or Google’s Help Center for the latest instructions on deleting and reinstalling apps.

Clear app files and data

Important: After clearing app data, you’ll need to sign in again. Some things might get cleared out, like your job search history.

Android
  1. Open Settings.
  2. Tap Apps or App Manager.
  3. Select the Indeed app.
  4. Tap Storage or Storage & cache.
  5. Tap Clear cache and Clear data.
  6. Restart your phone.
iOS
  1. Press and hold the Indeed app icon.
  2. Tap Remove App.
  3. Tap Delete App to remove the app and its data.

Check device compatibility

The Indeed app works with most modern devices and operating systems. You may experience performance issues if:

  • You use an older device
  • Your operating system is out of date

Mobile Browsers: Clearing Chrome and Safari Cache and Cookies

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Clearing your cache and cookies from your Chrome and Safari browsers may fix website issues, speed up your browser, and protect your privacy. The steps for desktop and mobile browsers are similar, but there are a few differences. Follow the instructions below for mobile browsers. For desktop browsers, check our article on clearing cache and cookies on a computer.

Important: Check our app troubleshooting guide if you’re experiencing issues with the Indeed job search app.

Chrome (mobile)

Clearing browsing data
  1. Open Google Chrome.
  2. Tap the three dots in the top-right corner.
  3. Tap History.
  4. Tap Delete browsing data.
  5. Choose a time range. To remove everything, select All time.
Clearing cookies and cached files
  1. Select Cookies and other site data and Cached images and files.
  2. Tap Clear data.

Important: The steps may vary, depending on the version of your browser and the mobile device you are using. Learn more in Google’s Help Center.

Safari (mobile)

  1. Open your device’s Settings app.
  2. Scroll down and tap Safari.
  3. Tap Clear History and Website Data.
  4. Confirm your choice.

Important: The steps may vary, depending on the version of your browser and the mobile device you are using. Learn more in Apple’s Help Center.

FAQs

Question: Should I clear my phone’s cache and cookies regularly?

Answer: Yes, clearing stored data can help your browser run smoothly and load pages faster.

Question: Can you help me fix performance issues with the Indeed job search app?

Answer: Yes. Check our app troubleshooting page for steps to improve performance.

Troubleshooting Device Issues With Indeed Interview

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Most issues with joining or using Indeed Interview can be solved with a few quick checks. In this article, we’ll share some common troubleshooting tips on fixing device issues, camera and microphone permissions, and video and audio settings.

Step 1: Check your device and browser

Confirm that you are using a compatible device and browser. We recommend a desktop or laptop, but a mobile device can also work.

Related guides
  • Updating a Web Browser: Learn which browsers work best, how to check your version, and how to update your browser.
  • Browser Issues: Basic Troubleshooting: Try quick fixes like checking browser compatibility, updating or reinstalling your browser, turning off ad blockers, or clearing cache and cookies.

Step 2: Set your camera and microphone permissions

Allow camera and microphone access for Indeed Interview. Use the audio and video test screen to review and adjust your permissions.

Desktop
Google Chrome (recommended)
  • On the video test screen, click the camera icon in the address bar.
  • Make sure your microphone and video permissions are set to Allow.
Safari
  • On the video test screen, click the red camera icon in the address bar.
  • Confirm your camera and microphone are set to Always Allowed.
Microsoft Edge
  • On the video test screen, click the lock icon in the address bar.
  • Check that the camera and microphone are set to Allow.
Firefox
  • On the video test screen, click the lock or camera icon in the address bar.
  • Make sure permissions are allowed.
Mobile device
iOS (iPhone or iPad with Safari)
  • When asked, tap Allow for microphone and camera.
  • If blocked, tap aA in the top left corner to access Website Settings and set camera and microphone to Allow.
Android (Chrome browser)
  • When asked, tap Allow for microphone and camera.
  • You do not need to let Chrome “record audio” for interviews.

Step 3: Fix video and audio issues

Video

Before joining a hiring event, you’ll get a link to test your video, audio, and network.

  • Check your camera permissions if you don’t see yourself in the video test.
  • Run a Daily Network Test if the issue continues.
  • Don’t worry if you hear sounds. This means your microphone and speakers are being tested.
  • If the test fails, your network may be blocking the video platform.
Audio

If the other person can’t hear you:

  • Restart your device.
  • Rejoin the interview.

Important: iOS Safari has a known issue where the microphone stops working if another app is opened during an interview. Restarting the device should resolve this.

Two-Factor Authentication Issues: Basic Troubleshooting

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

Two-factor authentication (2FA) adds an extra layer of security by using a second form of verification, like a code from your phone or a fingerprint. This helps protect your account from hackers. In this article, we’ll cover the most common reasons why you’re encountering 2FA. Check our article on verifying your phone number if you’re sent a code while creating an account or applying for a job.

Did you know? 2FA is sometimes called two-step verification. No matter what it’s called, it helps keep your account secure.

Your email is linked to an employer account.

Problem: You clicked Employers/Post Job and accidentally created an employer account. 2FA is mandatory for all employer accounts.

Solution: Contact us for help. We’ll try and fix the issue. If we can’t, you may need to close your account and create a new one.

We noticed an unusual sign-in attempt.

Problem: You signed in from a new device or a different location.

Solution: Enter the verification code you received as a text or an email.

Your device has its own 2FA.

If you have an Apple device, review your iOS’s two-factor authentication in Apple’s Help Center.

If you use an Android, here’s how to turn on or troubleshoot Android’s 2-step verification.

Updating a Web Browser

Help Center

Help Center for

TopicsGuidesContact

On this page, we’ll guide you through the process of updating your web browser. Whether a banner appears at the top of your screen or you want to make sure you’re using the most up-to-date version, we’ll cover which browsers work best on Indeed and provide links to help you auto-update Chrome, Safari, Firefox, and Edge.

Which browsers work best with Indeed?

Desktop
  • Chrome: Latest and previous 30 major versions
  • Safari: Latest and previous four major versions
  • Firefox: Latest and previous three major versions
  • Microsoft Edge: Latest version
Mobile
  • Chrome: Latest and previous 30 major versions
  • Safari: Latest and previous four major versions

Did you know? These browsers are updated each month.

What if I don’t use a supported or outdated browser when using Indeed?

Other job seekers and workers have reported issues with these native browsers.

  • Samsung Internet
  • Kindle Fire

How to know if your browser needs an update

The message below means you need to update your browser, operating system, or device.

How do I know what version I’m using?

Tools like What is My Browser? identify your browser version.

What if I need more troubleshooting help?

Review the technical support pages listed below.

Manually updating your browser or using auto-update

Tip: Close your browser and re-open it after an update.

Google Chrome
Desktop

Chrome automatically checks for updates. If you want to check for updates, follow these steps:

  1. Click on the three dots in the upper-right corner.
  2. Go to Help.
  3. Select About Google Chrome. This page will trigger an update check.

Android

  1. Open the Google Play Store.
  2. Tap the three horizontal lines in the upper-left corner.
  3. Select My apps & games.
  4. Find Chrome and Enable auto-update.

iOS

  1. Open the App Store.
  2. Tap your profile picture in the upper-right corner.
  3. Scroll down to find Chrome.
  4. Enable Automatic Updates:
    • Settings
    • App Store
    • App Updates
Mozilla Firefox

Desktop

  1. Click the menu button (three horizontal lines) in the top-right corner.
  2. Go to Options.
  3. Select General.
  4. Scroll down to Firefox Updates and select Automatically install updates.

Android

  1. Open the Google Play Store.
  2. Tap the three horizontal lines in the upper-left corner.
  3. Select My apps & games.
  4. Find Firefox and check Enable auto-update.

iOS

  1. Open the App Store.
  2. Tap your profile picture in the upper-right corner.
  3. Scroll down to find Firefox.
  4. Enable Automatic Updates in:
    • Settings
    • App Store
    • App Updates
Microsoft Edge

Desktop

Edge automatically updates. To manually check:

  1. Click the three dots in the upper-right corner.
  2. Go to Help and feedback.
  3. Select About Microsoft Edge. This page will trigger an update check.

Android

  1. Open the Google Play Store.
  2. Tap the three horizontal lines in the upper-left corner.
  3. Select My apps & games.
  4. Find Edge and check Enable auto-update.

iOS

  1. Open the App Store.
  2. Tap your profile picture in the upper-right corner.
  3. Scroll down to find Edge.
  4. Enable Automatic Updates in:
    • Settings
    • App Store
    • App Updates
Safari
macOS

Safari updates are included with macOS updates. Follow these steps to enable automatic updates:

  1. Open System Preferences.
  2. Go to Software Update.
  3. Check the box that says Automatically keep my Mac up to date.

iOS

Safari updates are included with iOS updates. Follow these steps to enable automatic updates:

  1. Go to Settings > General > Software Update.
  2. Enable Automatic Updates.
